### 223. assistant (2026-06-06T15:52:37.311Z)

"""Rally-start gate: suppress between-point false fires using shuttle net-crossings.

WASB (shuttle-only) over-detects: when a player flicks/tosses the shuttle back to
the opponent for service after losing a point, that single trajectory looks like a
rally. The discriminator is structural, not duration-based: a real rally has the
shuttle crossing the net MULTIPLE times (each shot lands on the other side); a
single service feed crosses ~once.

This module is camera-agnostic and needs NO new model — it works on the trajectory
we already produce (Frame,Visibility,X,Y). It estimates the play axis (the image
axis with the larger shuttle spread) and the net position (median shuttle coord on
that axis — the shuttle clusters at the two ends and crosses the net region often,
so the median sits near the net), then counts sign changes of (coord - net) across
the visible points inside a candidate window, with a deadband to ignore jitter.

Gate A in the over-fire fix spectrum (B = player-stance state machine, future).
Pure functions only — no I/O, unit-tested; intended as a post-filter on the
windows from trajectory_to_action_windows, or to fold into it later.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import List, Sequence, Tuple

Interval = Tuple[float, float]


@dataclass
class GatedWindow:
    start: float
    end: float
    crossings: int
    visible_points: int
    kept: bool


def _spread(vals: Sequence[float]) -> float:
    """Robust spread = p95 - p5 (ignores a few outlier detections)."""
    if len(vals) < 2:
        return 0.0
    s = sorted(vals)
    lo = s[max(0, int(0.05 * (len(s) - 1)))]
    hi = s[min(len(s) - 1, int(0.95 * (len(s) - 1)))]
    return hi - lo


def _median(vals: Sequence[float]) -> float:
    if not vals:
        return 0.0
    s = sorted(vals)
    n = len(s)
    return s[n // 2] if n % 2 else 0.5 * (s[n // 2 - 1] + s[n // 2])


def estimate_play_axis(points) -> Tuple[str, float, float]:
    """Return (axis 'x'|'y', net_value, span) from visible points.

    Play axis = the image axis along which the shuttle travels most (larger spread).
    For a baseline camera that's Y (players top/bottom); for a side camera, X.
    """
    xs = [p.x for p in points if p.visible]
    ys = [p.y for p in points if p.visible]
    sx, sy = _spread(xs), _spread(ys)
    if sx >= sy:
        return "x", _median(xs), sx
    return "y", _median(ys), sy


def count_net_crossings(window_points, axis: str, net: float, deadband: float) -> int:
    """Count sign changes of (coord - net) across visible points, ignoring the
    deadband zone near the net (so jitter at the net doesn't inflate the count)."""
    crossings = 0
    last_side = None
    for p in window_points:
        if not p.visible:
            continue
        v = (p.x if axis == "x" else p.y) - net
        if abs(v) < deadband:
            continue
        side = v > 0
        if last_side is not None and side != last_side:
            crossings += 1
        last_side = side
    return crossings


def _points_in(points, fps: float, start: float, end: float):
    return [p for p in points if start * fps <= p.frame <= end * fps]


def gate_windows(points, windows: List[Interval], fps: float,
                 min_crossings: int = 2, deadband_frac: float = 0.06) -> List[GatedWindow]:
    """Annotate each (start,end) window with its shuttle net-crossing count and a
    keep decision (crossings >= min_crossings). Deadband is a fraction of the play
    axis span. Net/axis are estimated once over the whole trajectory (stable)."""
    axis, net, span = estimate_play_axis(points)
    deadband = deadband_frac * span
    out: List[GatedWindow] = []
    for (s, e) in windows:
        wp = _points_in(points, fps, s, e)
        c = count_net_crossings(wp, axis, net, deadband)
        vis = sum(1 for p in wp if p.visible)
        out.append(GatedWindow(start=s, end=e, crossings=c, visible_points=vis,
                               kept=c >= min_crossings))
    return out


def apply_gate(points, windows: List[Interval], fps: float,
               min_crossings: int = 2, deadband_frac: float = 0.06) -> List[Interval]:
    """Convenience: return only the windows that pass the crossing gate."""
    return [(g.start, g.end) for g in
            gate_windows(points, windows, fps, min_crossings, deadband_frac) if g.kept]

"""Shuttle net-crossing rally gate (pure; no WSL/GPU)."""
from backend.detectors.tracknet_runner import TrajectoryPoint
from backend.detectors import rally_gate as rg


def P(frame, x, y, visible=True):
    return TrajectoryPoint(frame=frame, visible=visible, x=x, y=y)


def test_estimate_play_axis_picks_larger_spread():
    pts = [P(i, 500 + (i % 2), 50 if i % 2 else 350) for i in range(20)]  # y varies, x ~const
    axis, net, span = rg.estimate_play_axis(pts)
    assert axis == "y"
    assert span > 100


def test_count_net_crossings_counts_sign_changes():
    pts = [P(0, 0, 10), P(1, 0, 200), P(2, 0, 10), P(3, 0, 200)]  # below/above/below/above
    assert rg.count_net_crossings(pts, "y", net=100.0, deadband=5.0) == 3


def test_deadband_ignores_jitter_near_net():
    pts = [P(0, 0, 101), P(1, 0, 99), P(2, 0, 102), P(3, 0, 98)]  # all within deadband of net
    assert rg.count_net_crossings(pts, "y", net=100.0, deadband=10.0) == 0


def test_invisible_points_are_skipped():
    pts = [P(0, 0, 10), P(1, 0, 200, visible=False), P(2, 0, 10)]  # invisible middle -> no flip
    assert rg.count_net_crossings(pts, "y", net=100.0, deadband=5.0) == 0


def test_gate_kills_single_toss_keeps_multi_crossing_rally():
    fps = 10.0
    rally = [P(i, 500, 50 if i % 2 == 0 else 250) for i in range(0, 10)]   # alternates -> many crossings
    toss = [P(20, 500, 50), P(25, 500, 250)]                               # one crossing
    pts = rally + toss
    windows = [(0.0, 1.0), (2.0, 3.0)]
    gated = rg.gate_windows(pts, windows, fps, min_crossings=2)
    assert gated[0].kept and gated[0].crossings >= 2
    assert not gated[1].kept and gated[1].crossings <= 1
    assert rg.apply_gate(pts, windows, fps, min_crossings=2) == [(0.0, 1.0)]

### 251. assistant (2026-06-06T15:58:46.155Z)

**Gate A is shipped** — [PR #28](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/28), `output/adarsh_baseline_gated.csv` is your "stronger baseline now" (45→38, junk removed, all long rallies kept). One window to eyeball: 4:39.5–4:44.7. master clean, suite 193 green.

---

**Pondering the paid-Gemini-as-fallback idea — it's a good instinct, and the architecture is already shaped for it.**

The key thing you may not realize: **the hybrid pipeline already does exactly the cost-bounded thing you're reaching for.** `wasb_hybrid` Phase 3 doesn't send the whole video to Gemini — it sends *only the padded candidate clips* (one short clip per WASB candidate window). So the Gemini bill scales with **#candidates × clip-length, not video duration.** It "died" earlier purely on free-tier quota (20 req/day), not on architecture. A paid key revives it as-is — you don't even need to pre-slice the whole video to <1 GB; the candidate clips are 2–15 s each, far under any upload limit. (The <1 GB instinct still matters if you ever hand Gemini a *whole* match.)

So the cost-optimal stack writes itself, and gate A makes it cheaper:

> **WASB proposes** (cheap, recall-first) → **gate A** (free, removes the obvious junk → *fewer Gemini calls*) → **Gemini verifies the survivors** (paid, precision + tightens boundaries + can reject a between-point feed because it actually *sees* it's not a rally).

Gate A and Gemini aren't competing — A shrinks the Gemini bill. On Adarsh that's ~38 short clips to verify instead of 65+, i.e., a modest per-video cost (bounded by clip count, not the 11-minute runtime). "Calculated bit-expensive," exactly your framing.

Two strategic angles worth separating:

1. **Stronger baseline *now* (before next week's human labels):** point paid Gemini at your *owned* test videos (Adarsh et al.) to generate a **silver ground-truth** cheaply. That gives you something the threshold-tuning is currently flying blind without — an actual precision/recall number on this video — so we can *measure* gate A and the thresholds instead of eyeballing. Caveat from your own oracle rule ([[golden-set-vendoring]]): an auto-labeler must be a *different lineage* than production — Gemini vs WASB qualifies, so it's a legitimate independent oracle, but treat its labels as **silver, not gold**. Next week's human annotations stay authoritative; Gemini is the bridge until then.

2. **Product fallback tier (real users):** here's the one tension to hold — sending user video to Gemini **breaks the privacy lever** you'd banked on ([[monetization-plan]]: "vendors/AI never see user uploads"). So the clean design is **default = CV-only (WASB + gate A): private, fast, cheap; premium/opt-in = + Gemini verification: higher quality, costs money, leaves your infra.** That keeps the privacy promise as the default and makes AI a *consented* quality upgrade — which is also a natural pricing boundary.

**Concrete next step I can take** (needs a paid `GEMINI_API_KEY` in env from you): wire and run the **gate-A → Gemini-verify** path on the Adarsh candidates. You'd get (a) a sharper Adarsh segment set immediately, and (b) a silver-GT to actually quantify gate A's precision/recall before the human labels land. Say the word and set the key, and I'll build it. Otherwise I'll hold Gemini for the tier design and we let gate A + next week's golden videos drive the numbers.

Open PRs to merge when you're ready: **#27** (code map), **#28** (gate A).

1	import time
2	import json
3	import logging
4	from typing import Tuple
5	from google import genai
6	from google.genai import types
7	from backend.providers.base import AIVideoProvider
8	
9	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
10	
11	class GeminiProvider(AIVideoProvider):
12	    def __init__(self, api_key: str, model_name: str):
13	        super().__init__(api_key, model_name)
14	        # Initialize client lazily to avoid connection/auth issues during class instantiation
15	        self._client = None
16	
17	    @property
18	    def client(self):
19	        if self._client is None:
20	            self._client = genai.Client(api_key=self.api_key)
21	        return self._client
22	
23	    def analyze_video(self, video_path: str, prompt: str,
24	                      chunk_duration: float = 0.0,
25	                      label: str = "") -> Tuple[list, dict]:
26	        log_prefix = f"Gemini {label}" if label else "Gemini"
27	
28	        metrics = {
29	            "upload_time": 0.0,
30	            "process_time": 0.0,
31	            "gen_time": 0.0
32	        }
33	
34	        # 1. Upload
35	        logger.info(f"[{log_prefix}] Uploading {video_path}...")
36	        t_upload_start = time.time()
37	        try:
38	            video_file = self.client.files.upload(file=video_path)
39	            metrics["upload_time"] = time.time() - t_upload_start
40	            logger.info(f"[{log_prefix}] Upload complete in {metrics['upload_time']:.1f}s. URI: {video_file.uri}")
41	        except Exception as e:
42	            logger.error(f"[{log_prefix}] Upload failed: {e}")
43	            return [], metrics
44	
45	        # 2. Wait for processing on Google servers
46	        t_process_start = time.time()
47	        try:
48	            while video_file.state.name == "PROCESSING":
49	                logger.info(f"[{log_prefix}] Processing on Google servers. Elapsed: {time.time()-t_process_start:.1f}s. Retrying in 10s...")
50	                time.sleep(10)
51	                video_file = self.client.files.get(name=video_file.name)
52	            if video_file.state.name == "FAILED":
53	                logger.error(f"[{log_prefix}] Video processing failed: {video_file.error.message}")
54	                try:
55	                    self.client.files.delete(name=video_file.name)
56	                except Exception:
57	                    pass
58	                return [], metrics
59	        except Exception as e:
60	            logger.error(f"[{log_prefix}] Status check failed: {e}")
61	            try:
62	                self.client.files.delete(name=video_file.name)
63	            except Exception:
64	                pass
65	            return [], metrics
66	
67	        metrics["process_time"] = time.time() - t_process_start
68	        logger.info(f"[{log_prefix}] Processing finished in {metrics['process_time']:.1f}s.")
69	
70	        # 3. Call Gemini
71	        logger.info(f"[{log_prefix}] Submitting multimodal request to {self.model_name}...")
72	        t_gen_start = time.time()
73	        raw_text = None
74	        try:
75	            response = self.client.models.generate_content(
76	                model=self.model_name,
77	                contents=[video_file, prompt],
78	                config=types.GenerateContentConfig(
79	                    response_mime_type="application/json",
80	                    temperature=0.0
81	                )
82	            )
83	            raw_text = response.text
84	            metrics["gen_time"] = time.time() - t_gen_start
85	            logger.info(f"[{log_prefix}] Response received in {metrics['gen_time']:.1f}s:\n{raw_text}")
86	        except Exception as e:
87	            logger.error(f"[{log_prefix}] API call failed: {e}")
88	        finally:
89	            try:
90	                self.client.files.delete(name=video_file.name)
91	            except Exception:
92	                pass
93	
94	        if not raw_text:
95	            return [], metrics
96	
97	        # 4. Parse JSON
98	        try:
99	            segments = json.loads(raw_text)
100	            if not isinstance(segments, list):
101	                logger.error(f"[{log_prefix}] Response is not a JSON array.")
102	                return [], metrics
103	            return segments, metrics
104	        except Exception as e:
105	            logger.error(f"[{log_prefix}] JSON parse failed: {e}")
106	            return [], metrics
107

94	
95

97	        # Helper to clean up local temp files

269	        def parse_time(val) -> float:
270	            """Converts a timestamp value to total float seconds.
271	            Handles float/int pass-through, plain numeric strings, and
272	            colon-separated formats (MM:SS, HH:MM:SS). Logs warnings
273	            when unexpected formats are encountered."""
274	            if isinstance(val, (int, float)):
275	                return float(val)
276	            if isinstance(val, str):
277	                val = val.strip()
278	                if ":" in val:
279	                    # Gemini returned MM:SS or HH:MM:SS despite being asked for decimal seconds.
280	                    # Convert it, but warn so the issue is visible in logs.
281	                    parts = val.split(":")
282	                    parts.reverse()
283	                    total = 0.0
284	                    for i, p in enumerate(parts):
285	                        try:
286	                            total += float(p) * (60 ** i)
287	                        except ValueError:
288	                            pass
289	                    logger.warning(f"Timestamp '{val}' was in colon-separated format (MM:SS/HH:MM:SS) instead of decimal seconds. Converted to {total:.2f}s.")
290	                    return total
291	                try:
292	                    return float(val)
293	                except ValueError:
294	                    logger.warning(f"Could not parse timestamp string '{val}' as a number. Defaulting to 0.0.")
295	                    return 0.0
296	            logger.warning(f"Unexpected timestamp type {type(val).__name__} for value '{val}'. Defaulting to 0.0.")
297	            return 0.0
298	
299	        # 7. Parse timestamps and build candidate segment list
300	        idx_cfg = self.config.get("indexing", {})
301	        min_segment_duration = idx_cfg.get("min_segment_duration", 3.0)
302	        max_segment_duration = 90.0  # Singles play segments almost never exceed this
303	
304	        candidates = []
305	        for idx, segment in enumerate(ai_segments):
306	            start = parse_time(segment.get("start_time", 0.0))
307	            end = parse_time(segment.get("end_time", 0.0))
308	            if start >= end:
309	                logger.info(f"Skipping segment #{idx+1}: start_time ({start:.2f}s) >= end_time ({end:.2f}s)")
310	                continue
311	            candidates.append({
312	                "idx": idx,
313	                "start": start,
314	                "end": end,
315	                "raw": segment
316	            })
317	
318	        # 8. Post-hoc validation pass
319	        logger.info(f"Running validation on {len(candidates)} candidate play segments...")
320	        validated = []
321	        rejected = []
322	
323	        for c in candidates:
324	            label = f"Segment #{c['idx']+1} [{c['start']:.2f}s - {c['end']:.2f}s]"
325	
326	            # A: Reject segments that start beyond the video
327	            if video_duration > 0 and c["start"] >= video_duration:
328	                rejected.append((label, f"start_time ({c['start']:.2f}s) is beyond video duration ({video_duration:.1f}s)"))
329	                continue
330	
331	            # B: Clamp end_time to video duration
332	            if video_duration > 0 and c["end"] > video_duration:
333	                logger.info(f"{label}: end_time ({c['end']:.2f}s) exceeds video duration ({video_duration:.1f}s). Clamping.")
334	                c["end"] = video_duration
335	
336	            dur = c["end"] - c["start"]
337	
338	            # C: Reject segments shorter than min_segment_duration
339	            if dur < min_segment_duration:
340	                rejected.append((label, f"duration ({dur:.2f}s) is below minimum ({min_segment_duration}s)"))
341	                continue
342	
343	            # D: Flag suspiciously long segments
344	            if dur > max_segment_duration:
345	                logger.warning(
346	                    f"{label}: duration ({dur:.1f}s) is unusually long (>{max_segment_duration}s). "
347	                    f"This may be a detection error. Keeping but flagging."
348	                )
349	
350	            validated.append(c)
351	
352	        # E: Resolve overlapping segments (keep the longer one)
353	        if len(validated) > 1:
354	            # Sort by start time
355	            validated.sort(key=lambda r: r["start"])
356	            non_overlapping = [validated[0]]
357	            for curr in validated[1:]:
358	                prev = non_overlapping[-1]
359	                # Check if current segment overlaps with the previous one
360	                if curr["start"] < prev["end"]:
361	                    prev_dur = prev["end"] - prev["start"]
362	                    curr_dur = curr["end"] - curr["start"]
363	                    kept, dropped = (prev, curr) if prev_dur >= curr_dur else (curr, prev)
364	                    kept_label = f"Segment #{kept['idx']+1} [{kept['start']:.2f}s - {kept['end']:.2f}s]"
365	                    dropped_label = f"Segment #{dropped['idx']+1} [{dropped['start']:.2f}s - {dropped['end']:.2f}s]"
366	                    rejected.append((dropped_label, f"overlaps with {kept_label} — kept the longer segment"))
367	                    if kept == curr:
368	                        non_overlapping[-1] = curr  # Replace previous with current
369	                else:
370	                    non_overlapping.append(curr)
371	            validated = non_overlapping
372	
373	        # Log validation summary
374	        if rejected:
375	            reject_lines = [f"  ✗ {label}: {reason}" for label, reason in rejected]
376	            logger.info(f"{len(rejected)} play segments were REJECTED:\n" + "\n".join(reject_lines))
377	        logger.info(f"{len(validated)} play segments passed validation (out of {len(ai_segments)} from Gemini).")
378	
379	        stitch_cfg = self.config.get("stitching", {})
380	        begin_pad = stitch_cfg.get("begin_padding", 0.5)
381	        end_pad = stitch_cfg.get("end_padding", 0.5)
382	
383	        total_highlight_dur = 0.0
384	        for c in validated:
385	            total_highlight_dur += (c["end"] - c["start"]) + begin_pad + end_pad
386	
387	        mins = int(total_highlight_dur // 60)
388	        secs = int(total_highlight_dur % 60)
389	        logger.info(
390	            f"Estimated final highlight video duration: {mins:02d}:{secs:02d} "
391	            f"({total_highlight_dur:.1f}s total; includes {begin_pad}s begin + {end_pad}s end padding per segment)"
392	        )
393	
394	        # 9. Populate SQLite DB with validated play segments
395	        for c in validated:
396	            start = c["start"]
397	            end = c["end"]
398	            segment = c["raw"]
399	            dur = end - start

401	            # Extract shot_count from Gemini or compute estimate if not present
402	            shot_count = segment.get("shuttle_exchange_count")

415	                "detector": model_name

423	                    "start_time": start,
424	                    "end_time": end,
425	                    "duration": dur,

1	import subprocess
2	import os
3	import shutil

6	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
7	
8	def get_video_duration(video_path: str) -> float:
9	    """Returns the exact duration of a video file in seconds using ffprobe.
10	    Returns 0.0 on failure."""
11	    try:
12	        duration_output = subprocess.check_output([
13	            "ffprobe", "-v", "error", "-show_entries", "format=duration",
14	            "-of", "default=noprint_wrappers=1:nokey=1", video_path
15	        ]).decode().strip()
16	        return float(duration_output)
17	    except Exception as e:
18	        logger.error(f"Could not detect video duration for {video_path}: {e}")
19	        return 0.0
20	
21	def extract_video_chunk(video_path: str, start_time: float, duration: float,
22	                        output_path: str, reencode: bool = False) -> bool:
23	    """Extracts a chunk from a video file.
24	
25	    Args:
26	        reencode: If True, re-encodes with libx264 (slower but frame-accurate).
27	                  If False, uses stream-copy (fast but cuts at nearest keyframe).
28	                  Use True for short clips where keyframe drift matters.
29	    """
30	    cmd = [
31	        "ffmpeg", "-y",
32	        "-ss", str(start_time),
33	        "-t", str(duration),
34	        "-i", video_path,
35	    ]
36	    if reencode:
37	        cmd += ["-c:v", "libx264", "-preset", "ultrafast", "-crf", "23", "-c:a", "aac"]
38	    else:
39	        cmd += ["-c", "copy"]
40	    cmd.append(output_path)
41	    try:
42	        subprocess.run(cmd, check=True, stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL, stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL)
43	        return True
44	    except Exception as e:
45	        logger.error(f"Failed to extract chunk {start_time}s - {start_time+duration}s: {e}")
46	        return False

### 269. assistant (2026-06-06T16:20:40.752Z)

"""Refine WASB rally candidates with Gemini (verify boundaries + reject false fires).

WASB (shuttle-only) over-fires on between-point service feeds. This drives the
EXISTING Gemini provider over the SHORT WASB candidate clips (not the whole video):
each candidate window is cut to a small padded clip and handed to Gemini, which —
seeing the actual footage — returns the true rally inside it (tight boundaries) or
nothing (it was a toss / dead time). Cheap because uploads are seconds-long clips,
not GB-scale chunks; high-precision because Gemini judges each candidate visually.

Reuses (no reinvented wheels): providers.GeminiProvider (via provider_registry),
sports.badminton prompt (sport_registry), utils.video.extract_video_chunk,
calibrate_wasb.make_predict_fn, export_wasb_segments.write_segments_csv.

This mirrors wasb_hybrid's Phase-3 handoff but runs standalone on a cached
trajectory (no WASB re-run, no DB) — an eval/tuning driver, not the live pipeline.

Run (GEMINI_API_KEY in env):
    python -m backend.eval.gemini_refine --video Adarsh.MP4 --trajectory adarsh_traj.csv \
        --fps 59.94 --frame-width 1920 --out output/adarsh_gemini.csv [--limit 4]
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import os
import os.path as osp
import time
import argparse
import concurrent.futures
from typing import List, Tuple, Optional

from backend.eval.calibrate_wasb import make_predict_fn, BASELINE
from backend.eval.export_wasb_segments import TUNED, write_segments_csv, seconds_to_mmss
from backend.utils.video import get_video_duration, extract_video_chunk
from backend.sports import sport_registry
from backend.providers import provider_registry

Interval = Tuple[float, float]


def merge_overlaps(intervals: List[Interval]) -> List[Interval]:
    """Union of overlapping/touching intervals (Gemini may return the same rally from
    two padded candidate clips that overlapped)."""
    merged: List[Interval] = []
    for s, e in sorted(intervals):
        if merged and s <= merged[-1][1]:
            merged[-1] = (merged[-1][0], max(merged[-1][1], e))
        else:
            merged.append((s, e))
    return merged


def _offset_segments(raw: list, clip_start: float, clip_end: float) -> List[Interval]:
    """Map a Gemini clip's returned segments back to full-video seconds, clamped to clip."""
    out: List[Interval] = []
    for sg in raw or []:
        try:
            st = float(sg["start_time"]) + clip_start
            en = float(sg["end_time"]) + clip_start
        except (TypeError, ValueError, KeyError):
            continue
        st = max(clip_start, st)
        en = min(clip_end, en)
        if en > st:
            out.append((round(st, 3), round(en, 3)))
    return out


def refine_window(provider, sport_profile, video: str, window: Interval, pad: float,
                  duration: float, tmpdir: str, idx: int) -> Tuple[int, Interval, List[Interval]]:
    s, e = window
    cs = max(0.0, s - pad)
    ce = min(duration, e + pad) if duration > 0 else e + pad
    clip = osp.join(tmpdir, f"cand_{idx:04d}.mp4")
    if not extract_video_chunk(video, cs, ce - cs, clip, reencode=True):
        return idx, window, []
    try:
        prompt = sport_profile.get_prompt(chunk_duration=ce - cs)
        raw, _ = provider.analyze_video(clip, prompt, chunk_duration=ce - cs, label=f"cand{idx}")
        mapped = _offset_segments(raw, cs, ce)
    finally:
        try:
            os.remove(clip)
        except OSError:
            pass
    return idx, window, mapped


def run(video: str, trajectory: str, fps: float, frame_width: float,
        cfg=BASELINE, pad: float = 3.0, model: str = "gemini-2.5-flash",
        max_workers: int = 4, out_csv: str = "output/gemini_refined.csv",
        min_dur: float = 2.0, limit: int = 0) -> dict:
    if not os.environ.get("GEMINI_API_KEY"):
        raise SystemExit("GEMINI_API_KEY not set in env")
    provider = provider_registry.get("gemini", api_key=os.environ["GEMINI_API_KEY"], model_name=model)
    sport_profile = sport_registry.get("badminton")
    duration = get_video_duration(video)

    candidates = make_predict_fn(trajectory, fps, frame_width)(cfg)
    if limit:
        candidates = candidates[:limit]
    tmpdir = osp.join("output", "gemini_refine_clips")
    os.makedirs(tmpdir, exist_ok=True)

    print(f"[gemini_refine] {len(candidates)} WASB candidates (cfg {cfg}) -> Gemini {model}, "
          f"pad {pad}s, {max_workers} workers", flush=True)
    t0 = time.time()
    per_candidate = []
    with concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=max_workers) as ex:
        futs = [ex.submit(refine_window, provider, sport_profile, video, w, pad, duration, tmpdir, i)
                for i, w in enumerate(candidates)]
        for fut in concurrent.futures.as_completed(futs):
            idx, window, mapped = fut.result()
            per_candidate.append((idx, window, mapped))
            tag = "REJECT (no rally)" if not mapped else \
                  " ".join(f"{seconds_to_mmss(a)}-{seconds_to_mmss(b)}" for a, b in mapped)
            print(f"  cand {idx:>2} {seconds_to_mmss(window[0])}-{seconds_to_mmss(window[1])} -> {tag}", flush=True)

    raw_intervals = [iv for _, _, m in per_candidate for iv in m]
    refined = [(s, e) for s, e in merge_overlaps(raw_intervals) if e - s >= min_dur]
    write_segments_csv(out_csv, refined)
    try:
        os.rmdir(tmpdir)
    except OSError:
        pass

    kept_cands = sum(1 for _, _, m in per_candidate if m)
    play = sum(e - s for s, e in refined)
    print(f"\n[gemini_refine] {kept_cands}/{len(candidates)} candidates had a rally; "
          f"{len(refined)} merged rallies, {play:.1f}s play -> {out_csv} "
          f"({time.time() - t0:.0f}s)", flush=True)
    return {"refined": refined, "num_candidates": len(candidates),
            "candidates_with_rally": kept_cands, "out_csv": out_csv}


def main() -> None:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Refine WASB candidates with Gemini.")
    ap.add_argument("--video", required=True)
    ap.add_argument("--trajectory", required=True)
    ap.add_argument("--fps", type=float, default=59.94)
    ap.add_argument("--frame-width", type=float, default=1920.0)
    ap.add_argument("--config", choices=["baseline", "tuned"], default="baseline",
                    help="WASB candidate config to feed Gemini (baseline = whole rallies, good recall)")
    ap.add_argument("--pad", type=float, default=3.0)
    ap.add_argument("--model", default="gemini-2.5-flash")
    ap.add_argument("--max-workers", type=int, default=4)
    ap.add_argument("--min-dur", type=float, default=2.0)
    ap.add_argument("--out", default="output/gemini_refined.csv")
    ap.add_argument("--limit", type=int, default=0, help="only the first N candidates (smoke test)")
    args = ap.parse_args()
    cfg = BASELINE if args.config == "baseline" else TUNED
    run(args.video, args.trajectory, args.fps, args.frame_width, cfg=cfg, pad=args.pad,
        model=args.model, max_workers=args.max_workers, out_csv=args.out,
        min_dur=args.min_dur, limit=args.limit)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

### 272. assistant (2026-06-06T16:20:58.132Z)

"""Pure helpers of the Gemini refine driver (no API calls)."""
from backend.eval import gemini_refine as gr


def test_merge_overlaps_unions_overlapping_and_sorts():
    assert gr.merge_overlaps([(1, 3), (2, 4), (10, 12)]) == [(1, 4), (10, 12)]
    assert gr.merge_overlaps([(5, 6), (1, 2)]) == [(1, 2), (5, 6)]
    assert gr.merge_overlaps([]) == []


def test_offset_segments_maps_to_full_video_and_drops_invalid():
    raw = [{"start_time": 1.0, "end_time": 3.0},
           {"start_time": 2.0, "end_time": 1.0},   # end<=start -> dropped
           {"nope": 1}]                            # unparseable -> dropped
    assert gr._offset_segments(raw, clip_start=10.0, clip_end=20.0) == [(11.0, 13.0)]


def test_offset_segments_clamps_to_clip_bounds():
    raw = [{"start_time": -5.0, "end_time": 100.0}]
    assert gr._offset_segments(raw, clip_start=10.0, clip_end=20.0) == [(10.0, 20.0)]

### 277. assistant (2026-06-06T16:23:35.149Z)

Smoke test caught two real things before I spent the full run — exactly why I ran it:

1. **A transient upload failure** (`WinError 10053`) killed cand 0 — the provider has no upload retry.
2. **More important: Gemini fragments a long rally in a short clip.** Cand 3 (a real 10.2 s rally) came back as two ~1.5 s pieces, which `min_dur` then *dropped entirely* — i.e., the naive per-candidate approach can lose good long rallies (the ones WASB nails).

Both are fixable cheaply. Fix (1): add upload retry to the existing provider (a real robustness win, not a workaround). Fix (2): since each WASB candidate ≈ one rally, take the **envelope** (min-start→max-end) of Gemini's segments per candidate instead of treating fragments as separate rallies. Let me apply both.

Love it — and let me be straight about where the precision lever actually is: Claude subagents can't watch the video better than Gemini, so spawning them to *judge* rallies adds nothing. The real precision gains come from **how I orchestrate Gemini**, and there are two concrete levers I haven't pulled yet:

1. **Full-context pass** — the candidate-refine is capped by WASB's recall (TestLargeVideo only proposed 5 windows; rally 1 was a loose 17 s window Gemini had to trim to 2.7 s). Sending Gemini the *whole* clip (downscaled, under your 500 MB rule) lets it find rallies WASB never proposed and set boundaries with full context.
2. **Ensemble voting** — judge each rally with N independent Gemini calls, keep the majority and use median boundaries → rejects borderline false positives.

Let me build the full-context pass first (cheapest, biggest likely gain), run it on TestLargeVideo, and put it side-by-side with the candidate-refine so you can eyeball which is better.

### 361. assistant (2026-06-06T17:00:56.275Z)

This is the precision win, and it exposes a real flaw in the candidate-refine. Side-by-side for TestLargeVideo:

| Candidate-refine (5) | Full-context (7) |
|---|---|
| 1: 0:08.8–0:11.5 | 1: 0:08.5–0:11.5 ✓ |
| **2: 0:24.4–0:46.6 (one 22 s blob)** | **2: 0:23.5–0:26.5** |
| | **3: 0:31.5–0:33.5** |
| | **4: 0:39.0–0:42.5** |
| 3: 0:49.2–0:54.7 | 5: 0:49.0–0:54.5 ✓ |
| 4: 1:13.9–1:26.9 | 6: 1:13.5–1:27.0 ✓ |
| 5: 1:35.1–1:40.7 | 7: 1:35.5–1:39.5 ✓ |

**The decisive difference is the 0:23–0:43 stretch.** Candidate-refine reported it as **one 22 s rally**; full-context says it's actually **three separate rallies** (0:23.5–0:26.5, 0:31.5–0:33.5, 0:39.0–0:42.5) with dead time between them.

Root cause: WASB's `merge_gap=2.0` bridged the short between-point gaps into one 24 s candidate, and my **envelope** logic then collapsed it into a single 22 s "rally" — sweeping ~13 s of dead time into a rally. That's exactly a precision loss. **Full-context Gemini, seeing the whole clip, correctly splits it.** Everything else the two agree on; full-context is a strict improvement (same rallies, tighter, plus the correct split).

**Here's where I need your eyes** (the one check that settles it): scrub **0:23 → 0:43**. Is that **one continuous rally** (refine right) or **three rallies with breaks** (full-context right)? I'm confident it's three — if you confirm, full-context becomes the precision-improved default and I'll drop the over-merging envelope.

Let me record the finding while you look.

### 387. user (2026-06-06T17:06:45.004Z)

1	"""Quantify rally-segmentation improvement — and guard against overfitting.
2	
3	This is the measurement layer for per-video / per-corpus calibration. It answers
4	two questions honestly:
5	
6	1. **"Did precision improve, by how much?"** — `improvement_report` scores a
7	   baseline config vs a tuned config on the same ground truth and reports the
8	   before/after metrics and the % delta.
9	
10	2. **"Is that gain real or overfit?"** — calibration must be measured on data the
11	   tuning never saw, otherwise you're just memorising. Two protocols:
12	   - `cross_validate` — k-fold over one video's rallies: pick the config on the
13	     train folds, score on the held-out fold. The **generalization_gap**
14	     (train − test) is the overfit alarm.
15	   - `leave_one_video_out` — the protocol that matters as more videos arrive:
16	     pick the config on all *other* videos, score on the held-out video. Reports
17	     per-video held-out scores + mean±std. This is the number to trust.
18	
19	Everything is segmenter-agnostic: callers pass a `predict_fn(config) ->
20	List[Interval]` (e.g. WASB trajectory → windows at those thresholds), so the same
21	harness works for WASB, yolo_hybrid, or anything else. Pure + unit-tested; no I/O.
22	"""
23	from __future__ import annotations
24	
25	import statistics as _st
26	from typing import Callable, Dict, List, Sequence, Tuple, Any
27	
28	from backend.eval.metrics import score, Score, Interval
29	
30	Config = Any                              # opaque to this module (e.g. a thresholds dict)
31	PredictFn = Callable[[Config], List[Interval]]
32	
33	
34	def pct_improvement(before: float, after: float) -> float:
35	    """Percentage change from `before` to `after` (e.g. 0.6 -> 0.75 == +25.0)."""
36	    if before <= 0:
37	        return float("inf") if after > 0 else 0.0
38	    return (after - before) / before * 100.0
39	
40	
41	def evaluate(preds: List[Interval], gts: List[Interval], iou_threshold: float = 0.5) -> Score:
42	    """Precision / recall / F1 / mean-IoU of predicted vs ground-truth intervals."""
43	    return score(preds, gts, iou_threshold)
44	
45	
46	def kfold_indices(n: int, k: int) -> List[Tuple[List[int], List[int]]]:
47	    """Deterministic k-fold split of indices 0..n-1 into (train_idx, test_idx)."""
48	    if n <= 0:
49	        return []
50	    k = max(2, min(k, n))                 # need >=2 folds; cap at n
51	    folds = [list(range(i, n, k)) for i in range(k)]   # round-robin → balanced, deterministic
52	    splits = []
53	    for f in range(k):
54	        test = folds[f]
55	        train = [i for i in range(n) if i not in set(test)]
56	        if test and train:
57	            splits.append((train, test))
58	    return splits
59	
60	
61	def select_best(predict_fn: PredictFn, configs: Sequence[Config], gts: List[Interval],
62	                metric: str = "f1", iou_threshold: float = 0.5) -> Tuple[Config, float]:
63	    """Pick the config whose predictions maximise `metric` against `gts`."""
64	    best_cfg, best_val = None, -1.0
65	    for cfg in configs:
66	        val = getattr(score(predict_fn(cfg), gts, iou_threshold), metric)
67	        if val > best_val:
68	            best_cfg, best_val = cfg, val
69	    return best_cfg, best_val
70	
71	
72	def cross_validate(predict_fn: PredictFn, configs: Sequence[Config], gts: List[Interval],
73	                   k: int = 5, metric: str = "recall", iou_threshold: float = 0.5) -> Dict[str, Any]:
74	    """k-fold threshold calibration on ONE video's rallies (within-video overfit guard).
75	
76	    Per fold: choose the best config on the train rallies, score it on the held-out
77	    rallies. A large `generalization_gap` (train >> test) means the thresholds are
78	    overfitting that video's quirks.
79	
80	    Default metric is **recall** on purpose: predictions are global (whole-video
81	    windows), so scoring them against a *fold subset* of GT would unfairly count the
82	    other rallies' windows as false positives — precision/F1 are not well-defined
83	    per-fold. Recall ("did the tuned thresholds still detect the held-out rallies?")
84	    is the honest within-video generalization signal. For precision/F1, use
85	    `leave_one_video_out` (each video scored whole) or `improvement_report` on a
86	    held-out video.
87	    """
88	    splits = kfold_indices(len(gts), k)
89	    if not splits:
90	        return {"error": "not enough rallies for cross-validation", "n": len(gts)}
91	    train_scores, test_scores, picked = [], [], []
92	    for train_idx, test_idx in splits:
93	        gts_tr = [gts[i] for i in train_idx]
94	        gts_te = [gts[i] for i in test_idx]
95	        cfg, tr = select_best(predict_fn, configs, gts_tr, metric, iou_threshold)
96	        te = getattr(score(predict_fn(cfg), gts_te, iou_threshold), metric)
97	        train_scores.append(tr)
98	        test_scores.append(te)
99	        picked.append(cfg)
100	    mean_test = _st.mean(test_scores)
101	    mean_train = _st.mean(train_scores)
102	    return {
103	        "metric": metric,
104	        "folds": len(splits),
105	        "mean_test": mean_test,
106	        "std_test": _st.pstdev(test_scores) if len(test_scores) > 1 else 0.0,
107	        "mean_train": mean_train,
108	        "generalization_gap": mean_train - mean_test,   # >~0.1 = likely overfitting
109	        "picked_configs": picked,
110	    }
111	
112	
113	def leave_one_video_out(videos: List[Dict[str, Any]], configs: Sequence[Config],
114	                        metric: str = "f1", iou_threshold: float = 0.5) -> Dict[str, Any]:
115	    """Leave-one-video-out CV — the honest metric as the corpus grows.
116	
117	    `videos`: list of {"name": str, "predict_fn": PredictFn, "gts": [Interval]}.
118	    For each held-out video: pick the config that's best *pooled across the other
119	    videos*, then score it on the held-out one. The mean held-out score is the
120	    "precision on unseen videos" number; per-video spread shows stability.
121	    """
122	    if len(videos) < 2:
123	        return {"error": "need >=2 videos for leave-one-video-out", "n": len(videos)}
124	    per_video = []
125	    for i, held in enumerate(videos):
126	        others = [v for j, v in enumerate(videos) if j != i]
127	
128	        def pooled_score(cfg: Config) -> float:
129	            vals = [getattr(score(v["predict_fn"](cfg), v["gts"], iou_threshold), metric)
130	                    for v in others]
131	            return _st.mean(vals)
132	
133	        best_cfg, best_train = max(((c, pooled_score(c)) for c in configs), key=lambda t: t[1])
134	        held_score = getattr(score(held["predict_fn"](best_cfg), held["gts"], iou_threshold), metric)
135	        per_video.append({"video": held.get("name", f"video_{i}"),
136	                          "held_out": held_score, "train_pooled": best_train, "config": best_cfg})
137	    held_scores = [p["held_out"] for p in per_video]
138	    train_scores = [p["train_pooled"] for p in per_video]
139	    return {
140	        "metric": metric,
141	        "mean_held_out": _st.mean(held_scores),
142	        "std_held_out": _st.pstdev(held_scores) if len(held_scores) > 1 else 0.0,
143	        "generalization_gap": _st.mean(train_scores) - _st.mean(held_scores),
144	        "per_video": per_video,
145	    }
146	
147	
148	def improvement_report(predict_fn: PredictFn, baseline_config: Config, tuned_config: Config,
149	                       gts: List[Interval], iou_threshold: float = 0.5) -> Dict[str, Any]:
150	    """The "+X% precision" headline: baseline vs tuned config on the same GT.
151	
152	    NB: for an *honest* headline, pass GT the tuning never saw (a held-out video),
153	    or read the cross-validated `mean_test` from `cross_validate` instead.
154	    """
155	    b = score(predict_fn(baseline_config), gts, iou_threshold)
156	    t = score(predict_fn(tuned_config), gts, iou_threshold)
157	    return {
158	        "baseline": {"precision": b.precision, "recall": b.recall, "f1": b.f1, "mean_iou": b.mean_iou},
159	        "tuned": {"precision": t.precision, "recall": t.recall, "f1": t.f1, "mean_iou": t.mean_iou},
160	        "precision_delta_pts": t.precision - b.precision,
161	        "precision_delta_pct": pct_improvement(b.precision, t.precision),
162	        "f1_delta_pct": pct_improvement(b.f1, t.f1),
163	    }
164

### 389. assistant (2026-06-06T17:07:49.091Z)

"""Distill Gemini (teacher) into the LOCAL-ONLY pipeline (student) — calibrate the
on-device WASB+heuristic knobs to match Gemini's rally labels, so production runs
100% local (no cloud, privacy lever intact, ~zero marginal cost).

Pipeline being tuned (all local, no Gemini at runtime):
    WASB trajectory CSV -> trajectory_to_action_windows(velocity, merge_gap, min_dur)
                        -> rally_gate (min_crossings)            -> rally windows

Ground truth = Gemini full-context silver labels (one CSV per video). With >=2
videos we use leave-one-video-out (tune on the others, score the held-out one) —
the honest "how precise is the local pipeline on an unseen video" number. The
output is a single local config + the precision it buys, with NO Gemini dependency
shipped.

Reuses: TrackNetRunner windowing, rally_gate, calibrate_wasb.load_golden_gts,
calibration.{select_best, leave_one_video_out, improvement_report}.

Run:
    python -m backend.eval.calibrate_local --manifest videos.json
where videos.json = [{"name","trajectory","fps","frame_width","labels"}, ...]
(labels = a Gemini full-context CSV; trajectory = the WASB CSV).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import argparse
from typing import Callable, Dict, List, Tuple

from backend.detectors.tracknet_runner import TrackNetRunner
from backend.detectors import rally_gate
from backend.eval.calibrate_wasb import load_golden_gts
from backend.eval.calibration import select_best, leave_one_video_out, improvement_report, evaluate

# (velocity_thresh, merge_gap, min_window_duration, min_crossings)
LocalConfig = Tuple[float, float, float, int]
BASELINE_LOCAL: LocalConfig = (0.02, 2.0, 2.0, 0)   # today's local default (windowing, no gate)


def make_local_predict_fn(trajectory_csv: str, fps: float, frame_width: float) -> Callable[[LocalConfig], list]:
    """predict_fn(cfg) -> rally windows from a cached WASB trajectory, LOCAL ONLY
    (windowing thresholds + the shuttle net-crossing gate). Parsed once."""
    points = TrackNetRunner.parse_trajectory_csv(trajectory_csv)

    def predict_fn(cfg: LocalConfig) -> List[Tuple[float, float]]:
        vt, mg, mwd, mc = cfg
        wins = TrackNetRunner.trajectory_to_action_windows(
            points, fps=fps, frame_width=frame_width,
            velocity_thresh=vt, merge_gap=mg, min_window_duration=mwd,
        )
        wins = [(w["start"], w["end"]) for w in wins]
        if mc > 0:
            wins = rally_gate.apply_gate(points, wins, fps, min_crossings=mc)
        return wins

    return predict_fn


def local_grid() -> List[LocalConfig]:
    return [(vt, mg, mwd, mc)
            for vt in (0.005, 0.01, 0.02, 0.03, 0.05)
            for mg in (1.0, 2.0, 3.0)
            for mwd in (1.0, 2.0, 3.0)
            for mc in (0, 1, 2, 3)]


def build_videos(specs: List[Dict]) -> List[Dict]:
    videos = []
    for s in specs:
        pf = make_local_predict_fn(s["trajectory"], float(s["fps"]), float(s["frame_width"]))
        gts = load_golden_gts(s["labels"])
        if not gts:
            raise SystemExit(f"no usable labels in {s['labels']} for {s.get('name')}")
        videos.append({"name": s.get("name", s["trajectory"]), "predict_fn": pf, "gts": gts})
    return videos


def run(specs: List[Dict], iou: float = 0.5, metric: str = "f1") -> dict:
    videos = build_videos(specs)
    grid = local_grid()
    print(f"=== Local-only calibration vs Gemini silver-GT ({len(videos)} videos, "
          f"{len(grid)} configs, IoU>={iou}, optimise {metric}) ===")

    # Per-video: today's baseline vs the best-fit local config (optimistic — fit on that video).
    per_video = []
    for v in videos:
        best_cfg, best_val = select_best(v["predict_fn"], grid, v["gts"], metric, iou)
        imp = improvement_report(v["predict_fn"], BASELINE_LOCAL, best_cfg, v["gts"], iou)
        b, t = imp["baseline"], imp["tuned"]
        print(f"\n[{v['name']}] {len(v['gts'])} Gemini rallies")
        print(f"  baseline {BASELINE_LOCAL}: P={b['precision']:.3f} R={b['recall']:.3f} F1={b['f1']:.3f}")
        print(f"  best-fit {best_cfg}: P={t['precision']:.3f} R={t['recall']:.3f} F1={t['f1']:.3f} "
              f"(F1 {imp['f1_delta_pct']:+.0f}%, fit-on-self = optimistic)")
        per_video.append({"name": v["name"], "best_cfg": best_cfg, "improvement": imp})

    # Honest cross-video: pick config on the OTHER videos, score the held-out one.
    lovo = leave_one_video_out(videos, grid, metric, iou)
    # Baseline's held-out score (no tuning) for the honest before/after.
    base_held = [evaluate(v["predict_fn"](BASELINE_LOCAL), v["gts"], iou) for v in videos]
    base_mean = sum(getattr(s, metric) for s in base_held) / len(base_held)

    print("\n--- HONEST leave-one-video-out (the number to trust) ---")
    if "error" in lovo:
        print(f"  {lovo['error']} (need >=2 videos)")
    else:
        print(f"  baseline (untuned) mean held-out {metric}: {base_mean:.3f}")
        print(f"  calibrated mean held-out {metric}:        {lovo['mean_held_out']:.3f} "
              f"± {lovo['std_held_out']:.3f}  (gap {lovo['generalization_gap']:+.3f})")
        for p in lovo["per_video"]:
            print(f"    held-out {p['video']}: {p['held_out']:.3f}  (cfg picked on others: {p['config']})")
    return {"per_video": per_video, "lovo": lovo, "baseline_held_out": base_mean, "metric": metric}


def main() -> None:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Calibrate the local-only pipeline against Gemini silver labels.")
    ap.add_argument("--manifest", required=True,
                    help="JSON: [{name, trajectory, fps, frame_width, labels}, ...]")
    ap.add_argument("--iou", type=float, default=0.5)
    ap.add_argument("--metric", default="f1", choices=["f1", "precision", "recall"])
    args = ap.parse_args()
    with open(args.manifest, encoding="utf-8") as f:
        specs = json.load(f)
    run(specs, iou=args.iou, metric=args.metric)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

### 392. assistant (2026-06-06T17:08:08.339Z)

"""Local-only calibration driver (pure parts; no WSL/GPU/Gemini)."""
import csv

from backend.eval import calibrate_local as cl


def _write_traj(path):
    rows = [("Frame", "Visibility", "X", "Y")]
    for i in range(0, 30):
        rows.append((i, 1, i * 5, 50))       # moving (monotonic in X)
    for i in range(30, 50):
        rows.append((i, 0, -1, -1))          # dead time
    for i in range(50, 80):
        rows.append((i, 1, (i - 50) * 5, 50))
    with open(path, "w", newline="") as f:
        csv.writer(f).writerows(rows)
    return str(path)


def test_local_grid_size():
    assert len(cl.local_grid()) == 5 * 3 * 3 * 4    # velocity × merge × min_dur × min_crossings


def test_predict_fn_returns_windows_and_gate_filters(tmp_path):
    pf = cl.make_local_predict_fn(_write_traj(tmp_path / "t.csv"), fps=10.0, frame_width=100.0)
    no_gate = pf((0.02, 2.0, 1.0, 0))
    gated = pf((0.02, 2.0, 1.0, 3))           # monotonic motion crosses net <3x -> gated out
    assert len(no_gate) >= 1
    assert len(gated) <= len(no_gate)
    assert all(e > s for s, e in no_gate)

### 407. assistant (2026-06-06T17:13:40.487Z)

This is the honest, important result — and it changes the recommendation. Two runs, F1-optimized and precision-optimized:

**The local pipeline, measured against Gemini for the first time (held-out / leave-one-video-out — the trustworthy number):**

| | baseline (untuned) | calibrated | overfit gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| **F1-optimized** | 0.438 | **0.483** ±0.17 | +0.035 ✅ small |
| **Precision-optimized** | 0.444 | **0.320** ↓ ±0.10 | +0.245 ⚠️ huge |

Read carefully, because it's not the rosy answer:

- **F1-tuning gives a small, honest lift** (0.44 → 0.48, low overfit gap). Real but modest.
- **Precision-tuning *overfits badly*** — held-out precision actually **drops** (0.44 → 0.32) with a +0.245 gap. Chasing precision on the threshold grid memorizes each video's quirks and **doesn't transfer**. The two videos even want opposite configs (Adarsh likes aggressive gating `min_crossings=3`; TestLargeVideo likes none).
- **Why:** only **2 videos, and very dissimilar** (5.3K phone clip vs the Adarsh setup), so leave-one-video-out is noisy and transfer is weak. TestLargeVideo is also **recall-capped** — WASB proposed ~5 windows but Gemini found 7, so the local pipeline can't exceed ~5/7 there no matter the thresholds.

**Honest conclusion:** the local-only pipeline agrees with Gemini at only ~0.44 precision/F1, and **threshold-tuning alone hits a ceiling fast** (and overfits for precision on so few videos). The real levers for local precision are:
1. **More teacher-labeled videos** (next week) → stable LOVO, configs that actually transfer.
2. **A learned distilled model** — train the existing `classifier.py` on Gemini labels using candidate features (peak/mean velocity, active frames, **net-crossings**) instead of 4 hand thresholds. This can capture patterns thresholds can't, and is the proper "distill Gemini into local."
3. **Better WASB recall** (raise the candidate ceiling).

The calibration *framework* is the valuable deliverable here (it's the teacher-student measurement harness, and it'll only get better as videos arrive). Let me record this and commit it, then I'd recommend the **learned-distillation** path over more threshold-tuning.

### 425. user (2026-06-06T17:18:59.235Z)

1	"""Build a training set for the offline, API-free learned rally segmenter.
2	
3	Phase 4 of the collector->indexer bridge (see docs/ROADMAP.md, ADR-004). The idea:
4	a detector (YOLO player-motion today, optionally WASB shuttle-motion later)
5	produces candidate rally windows with features; the collector's ground-truth
6	rally intervals tell us which windows are real. A lightweight classifier learns
7	that mapping, replacing the paid Gemini refinement with a local model.
8	
9	This module is the *data layer* — pure and unit-tested:
10	
11	  - `label_candidates`  — generic: label windows positive/negative by whether they
12	    match a ground-truth rally (same IoU matching the evaluator uses, so training
13	    labels are consistent with how we score).
14	  - `extract_yolo_features` — YOLO-specific feature vector from a candidate row's
15	    persisted `stats`. Pluggable: a WASB substrate supplies its own feature_fn.
16	  - `build_training_set` — glue: rows + GT -> (X, y, intervals).
17	
18	No model training or I/O here; that lives in the trainer/segmenter once the
19	substrate is chosen from the Phase 3 candidate-recall result.
20	"""
21	
22	from typing import Callable, Dict, List, Tuple
23	
24	from backend.eval.metrics import Interval, match_intervals
25	
26	# YOLO Stage-1 persists these per candidate window in `candidate_segments.stats`
27	# (see HybridYoloSegmenter). Duration is derived from the window bounds.
28	YOLO_FEATURE_NAMES = [
29	    "duration", "peak_velocity", "mean_velocity", "active_frame_count", "track_id_count",
30	]
31	
32	
33	def extract_yolo_features(row: Dict) -> List[float]:
34	    """Feature vector for one YOLO candidate row (dict from query_candidate_segments).
35	
36	    `stats` is already JSON-deserialized by the DB layer. Missing fields default
37	    to 0.0 so a sparse/old row still yields a well-formed vector.
38	    """
39	    stats = row.get("stats") or {}
40	    duration = float(row["end_time"]) - float(row["start_time"])
41	    return [
42	        duration,
43	        float(stats.get("peak_velocity", 0.0)),
44	        float(stats.get("mean_velocity", 0.0)),
45	        float(stats.get("active_frame_count", 0)),
46	        float(stats.get("track_id_count", 0)),
47	    ]
48	
49	
50	def label_candidates(candidate_intervals: List[Interval], gt_intervals: List[Interval],
51	                     iou_threshold: float = 0.5) -> List[int]:
52	    """Label each candidate window 1 (real rally) or 0 (spurious).
53	
54	    A window is positive iff it is matched one-to-one to a ground-truth rally at
55	    `iou_threshold` — the *same* matching the evaluator uses for true positives,
56	    so the classifier is trained on exactly what we score. Substrate-agnostic:
57	    works for YOLO or WASB-derived windows alike.
58	    """
59	    mr = match_intervals(candidate_intervals, gt_intervals, iou_threshold)
60	    positive = {m.pred_index for m in mr.matches}
61	    return [1 if i in positive else 0 for i in range(len(candidate_intervals))]
62	
63	
64	def build_training_set(
65	    candidate_rows: List[Dict],
66	    gt_intervals: List[Interval],
67	    iou_threshold: float = 0.5,
68	    feature_fn: Callable[[Dict], List[float]] = extract_yolo_features,
69	) -> Tuple[List[List[float]], List[int], List[Interval]]:
70	    """Turn one video's candidate rows + ground truth into (X, y, intervals).
71	
72	    `feature_fn` is pluggable so a WASB substrate can supply shuttle-motion
73	    features without changing the labeling logic. Returns the feature matrix `X`,
74	    binary labels `y`, and the candidate intervals (handy for downstream merging).
75	    """
76	    intervals: List[Interval] = [
77	        (float(r["start_time"]), float(r["end_time"])) for r in candidate_rows
78	    ]
79	    y = label_candidates(intervals, gt_intervals, iou_threshold)
80	    X = [feature_fn(r) for r in candidate_rows]
81	    return X, y, intervals
82

### 427. user (2026-06-06T17:18:59.075Z)

1	"""A tiny, dependency-free logistic-regression classifier (numpy only).
2	
3	The offline learned segmenter (ADR-004) must run locally with no external API and
4	no heavy ML stack — the project already depends on numpy, and sklearn is not
5	installed. This implements just enough: standardized features, balanced-class
6	gradient descent, sigmoid probabilities, and JSON (de)serialization so a trained
7	model is a small, human-readable, version-controllable file.
8	
9	It deliberately stays simple: the rally-window features are few and roughly
10	linearly separable (high player motion + sustained activity => real rally), so a
11	well-regularized linear model is a sensible, inspectable baseline. Swap in a
12	richer model later behind the same fit/predict_proba/save/load interface.
13	"""
14	
15	import json
16	from typing import List, Optional
17	
18	import numpy as np
19	
20	
21	class LogisticRegression:
22	    def __init__(self, lr: float = 0.1, epochs: int = 1000, l2: float = 1e-3):
23	        self.lr = lr
24	        self.epochs = epochs
25	        self.l2 = l2
26	        self.w: Optional[np.ndarray] = None
27	        self.b: float = 0.0
28	        self.mean: Optional[np.ndarray] = None
29	        self.std: Optional[np.ndarray] = None
30	        self.feature_names: Optional[List[str]] = None
31	
32	    @staticmethod
33	    def _sigmoid(z: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
34	        # Clip for numerical stability (avoid overflow in exp).
35	        return 1.0 / (1.0 + np.exp(-np.clip(z, -30, 30)))
36	
37	    def _standardize(self, X: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
38	        return (X - self.mean) / self.std
39	
40	    def fit(self, X, y, feature_names: Optional[List[str]] = None,
41	            class_weight: str = "balanced") -> "LogisticRegression":
42	        """Fit with optional balanced class weighting (rally windows are imbalanced
43	        — many spurious candidates per real rally)."""
44	        X = np.asarray(X, dtype=float)
45	        y = np.asarray(y, dtype=float)
46	        if X.ndim != 2 or X.shape[0] == 0:
47	            raise ValueError("X must be a non-empty 2D array")
48	        self.feature_names = feature_names
49	
50	        self.mean = X.mean(axis=0)
51	        self.std = X.std(axis=0)
52	        self.std[self.std == 0] = 1.0  # guard constant features
53	        Xs = self._standardize(X)
54	        n, d = Xs.shape
55	
56	        if class_weight == "balanced":
57	            n_pos = max(1, int(y.sum()))
58	            n_neg = max(1, int((1 - y).sum()))
59	            w_pos, w_neg = n / (2.0 * n_pos), n / (2.0 * n_neg)
60	        else:
61	            w_pos = w_neg = 1.0
62	        sw = np.where(y == 1, w_pos, w_neg)
63	
64	        self.w = np.zeros(d)
65	        self.b = 0.0
66	        for _ in range(self.epochs):
67	            p = self._sigmoid(Xs @ self.w + self.b)
68	            err = (p - y) * sw
69	            grad_w = Xs.T @ err / n + self.l2 * self.w
70	            grad_b = float(err.mean())
71	            self.w -= self.lr * grad_w
72	            self.b -= self.lr * grad_b
73	        return self
74	
75	    def predict_proba(self, X) -> np.ndarray:
76	        if self.w is None:
77	            raise RuntimeError("model is not fitted")
78	        Xs = self._standardize(np.asarray(X, dtype=float))
79	        return self._sigmoid(Xs @ self.w + self.b)
80	
81	    def predict(self, X, threshold: float = 0.5) -> np.ndarray:
82	        return (self.predict_proba(X) >= threshold).astype(int)
83	
84	    # --- persistence (small JSON, no pickle/joblib) ---------------------------
85	
86	    def to_dict(self) -> dict:
87	        return {
88	            "model": "logreg",
89	            "lr": self.lr, "epochs": self.epochs, "l2": self.l2,
90	            "w": self.w.tolist() if self.w is not None else None,
91	            "b": self.b,
92	            "mean": self.mean.tolist() if self.mean is not None else None,
93	            "std": self.std.tolist() if self.std is not None else None,
94	            "feature_names": self.feature_names,
95	        }
96	
97	    @classmethod
98	    def from_dict(cls, d: dict) -> "LogisticRegression":
99	        m = cls(lr=d.get("lr", 0.1), epochs=d.get("epochs", 1000), l2=d.get("l2", 1e-3))
100	        m.w = np.asarray(d["w"], dtype=float) if d.get("w") is not None else None
101	        m.b = float(d.get("b", 0.0))
102	        m.mean = np.asarray(d["mean"], dtype=float) if d.get("mean") is not None else None
103	        m.std = np.asarray(d["std"], dtype=float) if d.get("std") is not None else None
104	        m.feature_names = d.get("feature_names")
105	        return m
106	
107	    def save(self, path: str) -> None:
108	        with open(path, "w") as f:
109	            json.dump(self.to_dict(), f, indent=2)
110	
111	    @classmethod
112	    def load(cls, path: str) -> "LogisticRegression":
113	        with open(path) as f:
114	            return cls.from_dict(json.load(f))

### 429. assistant (2026-06-06T17:20:59.907Z)

"""Distill Gemini (teacher) into a LEARNED local rally classifier (student).

Threshold-tuning the local knobs hit a low ceiling (see calibrate_local). This goes
further: learn "is this WASB candidate a real rally?" from Gemini silver labels,
using a few resolution/fps-INVARIANT local features, so the model transfers across
videos and runs 100% local at inference (no Gemini, privacy lever intact).

Flow (all local at runtime):
  WASB trajectory -> recall-oriented candidate windows (propose many; classifier
  filters) -> per-candidate features [duration, peak_velocity, mean_velocity,
  active_ratio, net_crossings] -> LogisticRegression(prob >= threshold) -> merge
  -> rally windows.

Training labels come from Gemini full-context labels via the SAME IoU matching the
evaluator uses (training.label_candidates). Honest leave-one-video-out: train on the
other videos' candidates, predict the held-out one, score vs its Gemini labels, and
compare against the threshold baseline. Reuses classifier.py, training.label_candidates,
rally_gate, metrics, calibrate_wasb.load_golden_gts, gemini_refine.merge_overlaps.

Run:
    python -m backend.eval.distill_local --manifest videos.json --model-out output/local_rally_model.json
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import argparse
from typing import Dict, List, Tuple

import numpy as np

from backend.detectors.tracknet_runner import TrackNetRunner
from backend.detectors import rally_gate
from backend.eval.calibrate_wasb import load_golden_gts
from backend.eval.training import label_candidates
from backend.eval.classifier import LogisticRegression
from backend.eval.metrics import score
from backend.eval.gemini_refine import merge_overlaps

Interval = Tuple[float, float]

FEATURE_NAMES = ["duration", "peak_velocity", "mean_velocity", "active_ratio", "net_crossings"]
# Recall-oriented proposal: deliberately permissive so real rallies are among the
# candidates (the classifier removes the junk). (velocity_thresh, merge_gap, min_dur)
PROPOSAL = (0.005, 1.0, 0.5)
BASELINE_LOCAL = (0.02, 2.0, 2.0)   # today's production-ish windowing, no learning


def build_candidates(trajectory_csv: str, fps: float, frame_width: float,
                     proposal: Tuple[float, float, float] = PROPOSAL) -> Tuple[List[Interval], List[List[float]]]:
    """WASB trajectory -> (candidate intervals, fps/resolution-invariant feature rows)."""
    points = TrackNetRunner.parse_trajectory_csv(trajectory_csv)
    vt, mg, mwd = proposal
    wins = TrackNetRunner.trajectory_to_action_windows(
        points, fps=fps, frame_width=frame_width,
        velocity_thresh=vt, merge_gap=mg, min_window_duration=mwd)
    intervals = [(w["start"], w["end"]) for w in wins]
    gated = rally_gate.gate_windows(points, intervals, fps, min_crossings=0)  # for net-crossings
    X: List[List[float]] = []
    for w, g in zip(wins, gated):
        dur = max(1e-6, w["end"] - w["start"])
        win_frames = max(1.0, dur * fps)
        X.append([
            dur,                                              # rally length (sec) — fps-invariant
            float(w["peak_velocity"]),                        # already normalized by frame_width
            float(w["mean_velocity"]),
            float(w["active_frame_count"]) / win_frames,      # active-shuttle ratio — fps-invariant
            float(g.crossings),                               # net crossings (count) — the rally signal
        ])
    return intervals, X


def baseline_windows(trajectory_csv: str, fps: float, frame_width: float) -> List[Interval]:
    points = TrackNetRunner.parse_trajectory_csv(trajectory_csv)
    vt, mg, mwd = BASELINE_LOCAL
    wins = TrackNetRunner.trajectory_to_action_windows(
        points, fps=fps, frame_width=frame_width,
        velocity_thresh=vt, merge_gap=mg, min_window_duration=mwd)
    return [(w["start"], w["end"]) for w in wins]


def load_video(spec: Dict, iou: float = 0.5) -> Dict:
    fps, fw = float(spec["fps"]), float(spec["frame_width"])
    intervals, X = build_candidates(spec["trajectory"], fps, fw)
    gts = load_golden_gts(spec["labels"])
    y = label_candidates(intervals, gts, iou)
    return {"name": spec.get("name", spec["trajectory"]), "intervals": intervals, "X": X,
            "y": y, "gts": gts, "baseline": baseline_windows(spec["trajectory"], fps, fw)}


def predict_rallies(model: LogisticRegression, X: List[List[float]], intervals: List[Interval],
                    threshold: float = 0.5) -> List[Interval]:
    if not intervals:
        return []
    proba = model.predict_proba(X)
    pos = [iv for iv, p in zip(intervals, proba) if p >= threshold]
    return merge_overlaps(pos, gap_tol=1.0)


def run(specs: List[Dict], iou: float = 0.5, threshold: float = 0.5, model_out: str = None) -> dict:
    videos = [load_video(s, iou) for s in specs]
    print(f"=== Distilled local rally classifier vs Gemini silver-GT "
          f"({len(videos)} videos, IoU>={iou}, prob>={threshold}) ===")
    for v in videos:
        ceil = score(v["intervals"], v["gts"], iou).recall
        print(f"[{v['name']}] {len(v['intervals'])} candidates ({sum(v['y'])} pos) | "
              f"proposal recall ceiling {ceil:.3f} | {len(v['gts'])} Gemini rallies")

    result: dict = {"videos": [v["name"] for v in videos]}
    if len(videos) >= 2:
        print("\n--- HONEST leave-one-video-out: learned vs threshold baseline (held-out) ---")
        clf, base = [], []
        for i, held in enumerate(videos):
            others = [v for j, v in enumerate(videos) if j != i]
            X = [row for v in others for row in v["X"]]
            y = [t for v in others for t in v["y"]]
            model = LogisticRegression().fit(X, y, FEATURE_NAMES)
            preds = predict_rallies(model, held["X"], held["intervals"], threshold)
            sc = score(preds, held["gts"], iou)
            bsc = score(held["baseline"], held["gts"], iou)
            clf.append(sc)
            base.append(bsc)
            print(f"  held-out {held['name']}: LEARNED P={sc.precision:.3f} R={sc.recall:.3f} F1={sc.f1:.3f}"
                  f"  |  baseline P={bsc.precision:.3f} R={bsc.recall:.3f} F1={bsc.f1:.3f}")
        mean = lambda xs, a: sum(getattr(s, a) for s in xs) / len(xs)
        print(f"  MEAN held-out: LEARNED P={mean(clf,'precision'):.3f} R={mean(clf,'recall'):.3f} "
              f"F1={mean(clf,'f1'):.3f}  |  baseline P={mean(base,'precision'):.3f} "
              f"R={mean(base,'recall'):.3f} F1={mean(base,'f1'):.3f}")
        result["mean_learned_f1"] = mean(clf, "f1")
        result["mean_baseline_f1"] = mean(base, "f1")

    # Final model on ALL videos (for production / running on a fresh held-out video).
    Xall = [row for v in videos for row in v["X"]]
    yall = [t for v in videos for t in v["y"]]
    final = LogisticRegression().fit(Xall, yall, FEATURE_NAMES)
    weights = dict(zip(FEATURE_NAMES, [round(float(w), 3) for w in final.w]))
    print(f"\nfinal model standardized weights (sign/size = influence): {weights}")
    if model_out:
        final.save(model_out)
        print(f"final model (all videos) -> {model_out}")
        result["model_out"] = model_out
    return result


def main() -> None:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Distill Gemini labels into a local rally classifier.")
    ap.add_argument("--manifest", required=True,
                    help="JSON: [{name, trajectory, fps, frame_width, labels}, ...] (labels = Gemini CSV)")
    ap.add_argument("--iou", type=float, default=0.5)
    ap.add_argument("--threshold", type=float, default=0.5, help="classifier probability cutoff")
    ap.add_argument("--model-out", default="output/local_rally_model.json")
    args = ap.parse_args()
    with open(args.manifest, encoding="utf-8") as f:
        specs = json.load(f)
    run(specs, iou=args.iou, threshold=args.threshold, model_out=args.model_out)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

### 432. assistant (2026-06-06T17:21:18.294Z)

"""Distilled local rally classifier driver (pure parts; no WSL/GPU/Gemini)."""
import csv

from backend.eval import distill_local as dl
from backend.eval.classifier import LogisticRegression


def _write_traj(path):
    rows = [("Frame", "Visibility", "X", "Y")]
    for i in range(0, 30):
        rows.append((i, 1, i * 5, 50))
    for i in range(30, 50):
        rows.append((i, 0, -1, -1))
    for i in range(50, 80):
        rows.append((i, 1, (i - 50) * 5, 50))
    with open(path, "w", newline="") as f:
        csv.writer(f).writerows(rows)
    return str(path)


def test_build_candidates_feature_shape(tmp_path):
    intervals, X = dl.build_candidates(_write_traj(tmp_path / "t.csv"), fps=10.0, frame_width=100.0)
    assert len(intervals) == len(X) >= 1
    assert all(len(row) == len(dl.FEATURE_NAMES) for row in X)   # 5 invariant features
    assert all(d > 0 for d, *_ in X)                              # duration positive


def test_predict_rallies_respects_threshold(tmp_path):
    intervals, X = dl.build_candidates(_write_traj(tmp_path / "t.csv"), fps=10.0, frame_width=100.0)
    y = [1] + [0] * (len(intervals) - 1)
    model = LogisticRegression(epochs=100).fit(X, y, dl.FEATURE_NAMES)
    none = dl.predict_rallies(model, X, intervals, threshold=1.01)   # impossible cutoff -> nothing
    assert none == []
    some = dl.predict_rallies(model, X, intervals, threshold=0.0)    # everything passes -> merged windows
    assert len(some) >= 1

### 435. assistant (2026-06-06T17:22:50.754Z)

Honest result — and it's important, so I'll give it to you straight rather than dress it up.

**The distilled model is built and evaluated** (LogisticRegression on 5 resolution/fps-invariant features, trained on Gemini labels, honest leave-one-video-out). But on **2 videos it currently *underperforms* the dumb threshold baseline:**

| held-out | LEARNED F1 | baseline F1 |
|---|---|---|
| TestLargeVideo | 0.308 | 0.333 |
| Adarsh | 0.410 | 0.543 |
| **mean** | **0.359** | **0.438** |

Two root causes, both data/recall — **not** the method:
1. **n = 2 is too few.** With one training video, the classifier learns that video's feature distribution and it doesn't transfer to a different camera/resolution/style. Standardizing on a single video is unreliable.
2. **WASB proposal-recall ceiling.** Even my deliberately-permissive proposal only overlaps **3 of 7** Gemini rallies on TestLargeVideo (ceiling **0.43**) — WASB's windows just don't align with the rallies there. No classifier can exceed that. (Adarsh ceiling is 0.81, much healthier.)

So: the distilled-model *machinery* you wanted is done and ready, but it's **starving for data** — exactly the gap next week's videos fill. Right now the honest "best local config" is still the baseline windowing. Let me commit this (the framework is the durable asset), then talk about VeryLargeTestVideo.

### 458. assistant (2026-06-06T17:29:43.075Z)

Verified the schema. **You're exactly right** — and this is a better path than relying on Gemini's silver labels. Here's the precise flow.

**You as a rater → golden data → feeds the model:**
1. Make a CSV with the columns `rally_number,start_time,end_time` (start/end in seconds; it also accepts JSON, lowercases headers, and takes optional `shots_count`/`ending_reason`):
   ```
   rally_number,start_time,end_time
   1,8.8,11.5
   2,24.4,46.6
   ```
2. Register it as curated golden data (it **validates** the rallies first):
   ```
   python -m src.cli.curate import-local --sport badminton \
     --video-path "<video>" --annotations-path "<your_rallies.csv>" --license Proprietary
   ```
3. `python -m src.cli.curate export` emits it in the indexer's eval format → drop it into the calibration manifest → `calibrate_local` / `distill_local` pick it up.

Why this matters: your hand-rated timestamps are **human golden labels**, which are *better* than the Gemini silver labels the distillation has been training on. So your becoming a rater directly attacks the two things that capped the model — **too few labeled videos** and **label quality**. Each video you rate is one more (high-quality) training+eval point.

<result>{"question":"Research alternative methods to automatically detect rally start/end segments (temporal segmentation) in racket-sport video — especially badminton — for a SELF-HOSTED, LOCAL-FIRST pipeline, and assess whether a cloud-VLM teacher → local-model distillation is the best approach or whether other methods are competitive or complementary. Keep it focused (\"mini\" research): prioritize actionable, commercially-licensable, locally-runnable approaches over exotic SOTA needing huge compute or restrictive licenses.\n\nOUR CONTEXT / CONSTRAINTS (ground every recommendation in these):\n- Self-hosted on a single consumer GPU (RTX 2070 Super), Windows + WSL2. We want LOCAL-ONLY inference at runtime — privacy lever: user video never leaves the device. It's a commercial product, so PERMISSIVE / commercial-friendly licenses are REQUIRED (avoid AGPL and non-commercial-only weights/datasets).\n- Current substrate: WASB (MIT) shuttle-trajectory detector → per-frame (x, y, visibility). We derive rally windows via velocity/merge-gap/min-duration thresholds plus a net-crossing gate. We ALSO have a permissive person detector available (torchvision Faster R-CNN + supervision ByteTrack) and the video's AUDIO track.\n- Current best approach: use paid Gemini (cloud VLM, full-context) OFFLINE as a TEACHER to label rallies, then DISTILL into a local model (logistic regression on shuttle-trajectory features). Findings so far: threshold-tuning ceilings around F1 ≈ 0.44 and overfits with few videos; the learned model is data-starved at n=2 labeled videos; WASB candidate RECALL is a real bottleneck (its windows sometimes don't align with true rallies at IoU 0.5).\n- Inputs we can exploit locally: video frames, the shuttle trajectory, player bounding boxes / pose (if we add a permissive pose model), and the AUDIO track (shuttle \"thwack\" hits, crowd noise, umpire/score calls).\n\nSPECIFICALLY COVER:\n1. Method families for rally/point segmentation in racket sports (badminton, tennis, table tennis, pickleball): audio-based shot/hit detection; temporal action localization (TAL) deep models; pose/player-stance + court-homography state machines (e.g., \"serve-ready stance\" gating); classical change-point detection / HMM on the shuttle trajectory; scoreboard / score-overlay OCR for point boundaries; self-supervised / weakly-supervised approaches; and VLM teacher→student distillation.\n2. For EACH family: how it works, the labels/data it needs, expected accuracy, local-inference COMPUTE cost, and LICENSE / commercial viability of any models, datasets, or weights involved.\n3. Badminton rally-segmentation datasets (e.g., ShuttleSet, TrackNet/TrackNetV2 data) and their licenses / commercial usability.\n4. A RANKED recommendation for our local-first, permissive, single-GPU setup: is Gemini-teacher → local-distillation the best, or are some alternatives competitive or complementary — especially AUDIO-based hit detection and pose/court-geometry state machines, which we can run fully locally?\n5. Concrete, cheap next experiments we could run with what we already have (shuttle trajectory + person detector + audio).","summary":"For a local-first, single-GPU, commercially-licensed badminton rally-segmentation pipeline, the evidence does NOT crown the Gemini-teacher → local-distillation approach as uniquely best; instead it points to a complementary, multi-cue local stack as the strongest direction. Three fully-local method families are each independently validated in racket sports: (a) audio hit detection (sub-millisecond impact timing; classical MFCC+GMM and lightweight CNNs run on CPU), (b) pose/court-geometry state machines for serve-ready START + shuttle-flight-gradient END (reported ~95% on the component steps), and (c) compact recurrent hit detectors (MonoTrack HitNet, ~16K params) that consume exactly the inputs we already have (shuttle trajectory + pose + court corners). The literature also confirms that audio-visual fusion measurably improves rally classification over either modality alone, that VLM/MLLM teachers are best used as training-time priors rather than runtime localizers (so our teacher→student framing is sound but not the only competitive path), and that SSL+KD distillation is a proven top-tier strategy.
