### 479. user (2026-06-06T17:44:10.596Z)

=== SUMMARY ===
 For a local-first, single-GPU, commercially-licensed badminton rally-segmentation pipeline, the evidence does NOT crown the Gemini-teacher → local-distillation approach as uniquely best; instead it points to a complementary, multi-cue local stack as the strongest direction. Three fully-local method families are each independently validated in racket sports: (a) audio hit detection (sub-millisecond impact timing; classical MFCC+GMM and lightweight CNNs run on CPU), (b) pose/court-geometry state machines for serve-ready START + shuttle-flight-gradient END (reported ~95% on the component steps), and (c) compact recurrent hit detectors (MonoTrack HitNet, ~16K params) that consume exactly the inputs we already have (shuttle trajectory + pose + court corners). The literature also confirms that audio-visual fusion measurably improves rally classification over either modality alone, that VLM/MLLM teachers are best used as training-time priors rather than runtime localizers (so our teacher→student framing is sound but not the only competitive path), and that SSL+KD distillation is a proven top-tier strategy. The single most actionable near-term lever, given our current substrate and the WASB recall bottleneck, is to add a fully-local AUDIO hit-detection cue and fuse it with the shuttle trajectory; pose/court-geometry serve-gating is the strongest second add. A major dataset caveat: ShuttleSet and the broadcast-CNN approaches are derived from BWF broadcast footage with replays/score graphics/director cuts, and their high reported numbers are per-frame classification, not IoU boundary accuracy — they will not transfer cleanly to single-fixed-camera amateur video.

=== 12 VERIFIED FINDINGS ===

[1] conf=high vote=2-1 and 3-0
CLAIM: Pose + court-geometry state machines are a viable FULLY-LOCAL alternative for rally boundaries: serve-ready/serving stance (court-detail + pose features, classified by BiLSTM/CNN) marks rally START, and shuttlecock-flight gradient analysis marks rally END. Inputs are hand-engineered 12-frame x 26-feature (distance/angle/gradient/movement) matrices, not raw frames — i.e., cheap to run locally with no large model.
SRC: https://link.springer.com/chapter/10.1007/978-3-031-69073-0_15; https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s42979-025-03935-0

[2] conf=high vote=3-0
CLAIM: A compact recurrent hit detector (MonoTrack's HitNet) detects badminton hit events — which segment a rally into successive strokes — using a ~16K-parameter 2-layer GRU over a 12-frame sliding window of court corners + both players' poses + shuttle location. These are exactly the locally-available, permissive inputs our pipeline already has (shuttle trajectory + person/pose + court geometry), and the model is trivially runnable on our GPU.
SRC: https://ar5iv.labs.arxiv.org/html/2204.01899

[3] conf=high vote=3-0
CLAIM: Audio-based hit detection is a high-resolution, fully-local cue for racket-sport hit timing. A two-stage energy-peak + small-CNN pipeline detects ball-racket contacts with sub-millisecond timing (peaks within ~0.2 ms even with noise; peak-detection precision 0.98, recall 0.95-1.0), and a classical MFCC+GMM pipeline (39-D MFCC, per-class GMMs, likelihood-ratio confidence) is CPU-light and applicable to detecting shuttle 'thwack' hits.
SRC: https://arxiv.org/html/2409.11760v1; https://www.researchgate.net/publication/261119682_Detection_of_ball_hits_in_a_tennis_game_using_audio_and_visual_information

[4] conf=high vote=3-0 and 2-1
CLAIM: Audio-only hit detection is only as good as its post-processing: naive raw-frame probabilities give a poor F-score (~41-50), but adding a simple likelihood-ratio confidence measure raises audio-only F-score to ~68-77 by cutting false positives. A standalone XGBoost impact classifier hits 0.98 median accuracy on isolated samples, but the full on-court system drops to ~85% — real-world deployment degrades audio-only hit detection substantially.
SRC: https://www.researchgate.net/publication/261119682_Detection_of_ball_hits_in_a_tennis_game_using_audio_and_visual_information; https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11843912/

[5] conf=high vote=3-0
CLAIM: Audio + visual fusion measurably improves rally vs non-rally classification over either modality alone — supporting an audio cue we can run fully locally as competitive/complementary. In a tennis HMM, ball-hits + end-of-exchange applause are discriminative for rallies, and audio-visual fusion lifted rally RECALL to 83% (vs 54% visual-only, 63% audio-only) at near-unchanged precision. In soccer action spotting, late fusion gave best performance (+4.19% mAP spotting / +7.43% classification).
SRC: https://www.irisa.fr/metiss/gravier/biblio/03/kijak-icme03.pdf; https://arxiv.org/html/2505.03991v1

[6] conf=high vote=3-0 and 2-1
CLAIM: VLM/MLLM teacher -> local-student distillation is a SOUND but not uniquely-best strategy. The recommended paradigm uses the MLLM only as a source of semantic priors/guidance to enhance a conventional weakly-supervised localizer at TRAINING time — the MLLM is NOT the deployed runtime localizer — which exactly matches our offline-Gemini-teacher -> local-student framing. Separately, SSL + knowledge distillation (COMEDIAN, 73.10 tight mAP on SoccerNet) is a proven top-tier action-spotting direction, validating distillation as a real approach rather than a workaround.
SRC: https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.08466; https://arxiv.org/html/2505.03991v1

[7] conf=high vote=3-0
CLAIM: Broadcast-style visual segmenters report very high numbers but are BROADCAST-specific and report per-frame, not IoU, accuracy — a key transfer caveat for our single-fixed-camera amateur footage. A per-frame ResNet-18 CNN distinguishing broadcast rally frames from replays/close-ups hits F1 97.23 / Prec 98.06 / Rec 92.34 without any shuttle tracking, and a separate shot-angle-recognition trimming step hits 99% — but both rely on broadcast artifacts (replays, score graphics, director cuts) absent in self-recorded video, and both impose the SAME min-3s-rally / min-3s-gap heuristics our pipeline already uses.
SRC: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2308.12199; https://arxiv.org/pdf/2307.16000

[8] conf=high vote=3-0
CLAIM: ShuttleSet is the leading permissive-context badminton dataset (104 sets, 3,685 rallies, 36,492 strokes, 44 matches 2018-2021; 18 shot classes + hitting/player locations per stroke), giving stroke-level structure for training/eval — but it is derived from BWF broadcast footage, so it shares the broadcast-domain transfer caveat and its license must be confirmed before commercial use.
SRC: https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.04948; https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3580305.3599906

[9] conf=medium vote=3-0
CLAIM: Scoreboard/score-overlay OCR is a usable primary temporal-segmentation signal for point boundaries when a stable on-screen score exists: sample frames, OCR a fixed scorecard ROI, and declare an event when the recognized count increments vs the previous valid reading. It is fragile to occlusion/reformatting/absent overlays — and is largely INAPPLICABLE to our amateur single-camera footage, which typically has no score overlay.
SRC: https://arxiv.org/html/2510.18405

[10] conf=high vote=3-0
CLAIM: Frame-exact Precise Event Spotting (PES) tolerances (~1-5 frames / 33-200 ms) are far tighter than coarse Action Spotting (5-60 s) — but our rally start/end WINDOWING is closer to coarse AS, so we should NOT over-engineer for frame-exact precision when choosing methods.
SRC: https://arxiv.org/html/2505.03991v1; https://arxiv.org/abs/2207.10213

[11] conf=medium vote=synthesis
CLAIM: RANKED RECOMMENDATION for our local-first, permissive, single-GPU setup: Gemini-teacher -> local-distillation is a valid backbone but should be AUGMENTED, not relied on alone. Highest-leverage, cheapest local additions in order: (1) AUDIO hit detection (MFCC+GMM or energy-peak+small-CNN) fused with the shuttle trajectory — directly attacks the WASB recall bottleneck using inputs we already have; (2) pose/court-geometry serve-ready gating for rally START + shuttle-flight-gradient for END; (3) a compact HitNet-style GRU on pose+trajectory+court as a learned segmentation primitive. All are MIT/permissive-compatible and CPU/GPU-light. Use Gemini purely as an offline TEACHER providing priors/labels, never at runtime.
SRC: https://www.irisa.fr/metiss/gravier/biblio/03/kijak-icme03.pdf; https://arxiv.org/html/2409.11760v1

[12] conf=medium vote=synthesis
CLAIM: CONCRETE CHEAP NEXT EXPERIMENTS with what we already have (shuttle trajectory + person detector + audio): (E1) Extract the audio track, run energy-based peak detection (high-pass + EMA threshold) to get candidate impact timestamps, and overlay hit density on existing WASB windows — measure whether audio hit-clusters recover the rally windows WASB misses (recall bottleneck) with zero new training. (E2) Add likelihood-ratio-style confidence post-processing to suppress audio false positives, since raw peaks alone are poor (~41-50 F) but LR post-processing reaches ~68-77 F. (E3) Build a tiny audio+trajectory feature fusion (add audio-hit-rate / time-since-last-hit / applause-energy as features to the existing logistic-regression distillation student) and re-eval at IoU 0.5. (E4) Use the person detector to add a coarse serve-ready-stance / both-players-in-court gate at window boundaries (proxy for the pose state machine) without a full pose model.
SRC: https://arxiv.org/html/2409.11760v1; https://www.researchgate.net/publication/261119682_Detection_of_ball_hits_in_a_tennis_game_using_audio_and_visual_information

=== CAVEATS ===
DOMAIN-TRANSFER is the dominant caveat: nearly every high accuracy figure comes from BWF/tennis/soccer BROADCAST footage (replays, score graphics, director cuts, multi-camera) or curated lab audio. Our target is single-fixed-camera amateur video with no replays and likely no score overlay, so the broadcast-CNN (97-99%) and scoreboard-OCR methods will degrade or be inapplicable, and audio results (mostly tennis/table-tennis with heavier/louder balls) face a weaker-SNR shuttle 'thwack' transfer problem. METRIC MISMATCH: the headline numbers are almost all PER-FRAME classification or per-component accuracy, NOT the IoU-0.5 boundary F1 our project is judged on — direct comparison to our ~0.44 ceiling is invalid (one such comparison claim was refuted 0-3). VOTE/EVIDENCE WEAKNESS: claims about pose-state-machine headline accuracy, audio-CNN superiority over classical methods, and automatic-audio reliability had split or refuted votes; the cloud-VLM->tiny-student 'is best' framing is NOT directly evidenced (COMEDIAN's KD is self-distillation, not VLM->student). LICENSE GAP: ShuttleSet's specific commercial-license terms and MonoTrack/HitNet weight licenses were not confirmed in the surviving claims and MUST be verified before any commercial use. CROSS-SPORT EXTRAPOLATION: fusion-lift magnitudes vary wildly (+0.19 to +7.43 mAP depending on model), so predicted gains for badminton are hypotheses, not guarantees. TIME-SENSITIVITY: action-spotting SOTA (ASTRA, T-DEED) moves fast, so 'top-tier' rankings are as-of-2024-2025.

=== OPENQUESTIONS ===
[
 "What is ShuttleSet's exact license, and is it (and any pretrained TrackNet/WASB/MonoTrack-HitNet weights) cleared for COMMERCIAL use? This is a hard product requirement and was not confirmed in the surviving claims.",
 "How well does audio shuttle-'thwack' detection actually work on REAL amateur single-camera badminton audio (weaker/quieter impacts, crowd/gym reverb) \u2014 does the tennis/table-tennis ~73-77 F audio-only ceiling hold, degrade, or improve for badminton?",
 "On OUR footage and at IoU 0.5, does adding audio (and/or a pose serve-gate) actually fix the WASB candidate-RECALL bottleneck, or does WASB simply miss the underlying trajectory regardless of how rally windows are derived?",
 "Given we are data-starved (n=2 labeled videos), what is the most label-efficient path: more Gemini-teacher labels, weak-supervision from audio/pose heuristics, or a small hand-labeled golden set \u2014 and which yields the biggest IoU-0.5 lift per labeling hour?"
]

### 481. assistant (2026-06-06T17:45:19.419Z)

# Rally-detection methods — research (2026-06-06)

Deep-research survey (23 sources, 110 claims → 25 adversarially verified, 6 refuted, 12 final findings)
on alternative ways to find rally start/end in racket-sport video, for our **local-first, permissive,
single-GPU** pipeline. Question: is Gemini-teacher → local-distillation the best, or are there competitive/
complementary methods?

## Verdict
Teacher→student distillation is **sound but NOT uniquely best**. The evidence points to a **complementary
multi-cue LOCAL stack**. VLM/MLLM teachers are validated specifically as **training-time priors**, not runtime
localizers — which is exactly our offline-Gemini framing. The single highest-leverage near-term lever is to
**add a fully-local AUDIO hit-detection cue and fuse it with the shuttle trajectory**; pose/court serve-gating
is the strong #2.

## Three fully-local, permissive, competitive method families
1. **Audio hit detection (shuttle "thwack") — top cheap lever.** Energy-peak + small-CNN gives sub-ms impact
   timing (peak precision ~0.98, recall ~0.95+); classical MFCC+GMM is CPU-light. ⚠ Only as good as its
   post-processing: raw peaks ~41–50 F-score → **likelihood-ratio confidence raises audio-only to ~68–77**;
   fully-automatic on-court systems degrade (~85%, and one tennis system hit 46% precision). Mostly validated
   on tennis/table-tennis (louder balls) → shuttle SNR transfer is an open risk.
2. **Pose + court-geometry state machine** (= our "gate B" idea, validated). Serve-ready stance (court+pose
   features, BiLSTM/CNN over a 12×26 hand-engineered matrix) → rally **START**; shuttle-flight gradient
   (≥80% consecutive no-motion frames) → rally **END**. Cheap (engineered features, no big model).
3. **Compact HitNet GRU** (MonoTrack, ~16K params, 2-layer GRU over 12-frame window of court corners + both
   players' poses + shuttle xy) — consumes exactly our available inputs; trivially runnable. Detects *strokes*
   (a segmentation primitive), not whole rallies.

## Key supporting evidence
- **Audio+visual fusion beats either alone:** tennis HMM rally **recall 83% (fused) vs 54% visual / 63% audio**
  at ~unchanged precision; soccer late-fusion +4–7% mAP. This is the core case for adding audio.
- **Distillation is legit:** MLLM4WTAL uses the MLLM as a training-time prior only; SSL+KD (COMEDIAN) is
  top-tier action-spotting. Our teacher→student is a valid backbone.
- **Our windowing is "coarse action spotting" (5–60 s tolerance), not frame-exact PES** — don't over-engineer
  for frame precision.

## ⚠ Caveats (honest)
- **Domain transfer:** nearly all high numbers come from **BWF/tennis BROADCAST** footage (replays, score
  graphics, director cuts) or curated lab audio. Our target is single-fixed-camera amateur video → broadcast
  CNNs (97–99%) and scoreboard-OCR are inapplicable/degrade.
- **Metric mismatch:** headline numbers are **per-frame / per-component accuracy, NOT IoU-0.5 boundary F1**.
  The "~95% beats your 0.44" comparison was **refuted (0-3)** — not comparable.
- **License gap (hard product req):** ShuttleSet, MonoTrack/HitNet weights, and WASB *weights* commercial
  terms were NOT confirmed — must verify before commercial use. (WASB code = MIT; weights = academic-trained.)
- Scoreboard/score-overlay OCR: usable only when a stable on-screen score exists → mostly N/A for amateur clips.

## Datasets
- **ShuttleSet** — 104 sets / 3,685 rallies / 36,492 strokes / 44 BWF matches (2018–21), 18 shot classes +
  hitting/player locations. Stroke-level structure for train/eval, but broadcast-domain + license TBD.

## Ranked recommendation (local-first, permissive, single-GPU)
Gemini-teacher → local-distillation is a valid **backbone to AUGMENT, not rely on alone**. Cheapest local adds,
in order: **(1) audio hit detection fused with trajectory** (attacks the WASB recall bottleneck with inputs we
already have); **(2) pose/court serve-ready START + flight-gradient END gating**; **(3) a compact HitNet-style
GRU** on pose+trajectory+court. All MIT/permissive-compatible and CPU/GPU-light. Keep Gemini offline-only.

## Concrete cheap next experiments (with what we already have)
- **E1 — audio recall probe:** extract audio → energy-peak detection (high-pass + EMA threshold) → candidate
  impact timestamps → overlay hit density on existing WASB windows → does audio recover rallies WASB misses?
  (zero new training; directly tests the recall bottleneck.)
- **E2 — audio FP suppression:** add likelihood-ratio confidence (raw peaks ~41–50 F → ~68–77 F).
- **E3 — fuse into the distillation student:** add audio-hit-rate / time-since-last-hit / applause-energy as
  features to the logistic-regression student → re-eval at IoU 0.5.
- **E4 — pose-free serve gate:** use the existing person detector for a coarse "both players settled in court"
  gate at window boundaries (proxy for the pose state machine, no pose model needed).

## Open questions
1. ShuttleSet / MonoTrack-HitNet / WASB-weights **commercial license** terms (hard requirement).
2. Does shuttle-"thwack" audio detection hold on **real amateur single-camera** badminton (weaker SNR, gym
   reverb), or degrade below the tennis ~73–77 F audio-only ceiling?
3. On OUR footage at IoU 0.5, do audio/pose cues actually fix the **WASB recall** bottleneck — or does WASB
   miss the underlying trajectory regardless of how windows are derived?
4. Most label-efficient path given n=2: more Gemini labels vs audio/pose weak-supervision vs a small
   hand-labeled golden set — biggest IoU-0.5 lift per labeling hour?

## Sources (primary)
Springer 978-3-031-69073-0_15 (+ SN CompSci 6(5):447) · MonoTrack arXiv:2204.01899 · arXiv:2409.11760 (audio
peaks) · Kijak tennis AV-HMM (irisa) · ShuttleSet arXiv:2306.04948 · broadcast-CNN arXiv:2308.12199 /
2307.16000 · MLLM4WTAL arXiv:2411.08466 · action-spotting survey arXiv:2505.03991 · WASB-SBDT (github nttcom)
· CoachAI license (github wywyWang).

## Research posted — rally-detection methods

Full cited report saved to [docs/RALLY_DETECTION_RESEARCH.md](docs/RALLY_DETECTION_RESEARCH.md) (23 sources, 110 claims → 25 adversarially verified, 6 refuted). The headline:

**Verdict: teacher→student is sound but *not* uniquely best.** The literature actually validates our exact framing (VLM as an *offline training-time prior*, not a runtime localizer) — but it says the strongest direction is to **augment** it with a complementary **fully-local multi-cue stack**, not rely on distillation alone.

**Three local, permissive, competitive cues — ranked by leverage for us:**
1. **🔊 Audio hit detection (shuttle "thwack") — the #1 cheap lever.** We're ignoring the audio track entirely, and it's a high-resolution rally signal. The killer evidence: in a tennis system, audio+visual **fusion lifted rally recall to 83% vs 54% visual-only** — and *recall* is exactly our WASB bottleneck. CPU-light (MFCC+GMM or energy-peak+small-CNN). Caveat: needs likelihood-ratio post-processing (raw peaks are weak, ~41–50 F → ~68–77 F).
2. **🧍 Pose/court serve-gate** — this is *literally our "gate B" idea, validated*: serve-ready stance → START, shuttle-flight-gradient → END. We can prototype a cheap version with the person detector we already have (no pose model).
3. **🧠 Compact HitNet GRU** (~16K params, MonoTrack) on pose+trajectory+court — a learned stroke primitive.

**Honest caveats it surfaced (important):**
- Almost every flashy number (95–99%) is from **broadcast** footage with replays/score-graphics and is **per-frame accuracy, not IoU boundary F1** — it will **not** transfer cleanly to your single-camera amateur clips. (The "95% beats your 0.44" claim was explicitly *refuted* as a metric mismatch.)
- **License gap (hard req):** ShuttleSet, MonoTrack/HitNet weights, and even the WASB *weights* commercial terms are unconfirmed — must verify before commercial use.

**Cheapest next experiments (zero/low new training, all in the doc):** E1 — extract audio, energy-peak the impacts, overlay hit-density on WASB windows and measure whether audio *recovers the rallies WASB misses* (directly tests the recall bottleneck). E3 — add audio features (hit-rate, time-since-last-hit, applause-energy) to the distillation student and re-eval at IoU 0.5.

Net: when we resume, **adding audio is the highest-ROI move**, and your **pose serve-gate instinct was right**. Both run fully local.

The **VLC annotator** workflow (`w6nwcpw33`) is still building — I'll post the plugin + install steps when it lands, then we're parked for your rating. (docs/RALLY_DETECTION_RESEARCH.md is uncommitted on master — say the word and I'll commit it.)

### 491. user (2026-06-06T17:47:52.499Z)

1	# Badminton Rally Annotator (VLC extension) -- install & usage
2	
3	## 1. Install (Windows)
4	1. Create the folder if it does not exist:
5	   `C:\Users\<you>\AppData\Roaming\vlc\lua\extensions\`
6	   (i.e. `%APPDATA%\vlc\lua\extensions\`).
7	2. Copy `rally_annotator.lua` into that folder.
8	3. In VLC: **Tools -> Plugins and extensions -> Reload extensions**
9	   (or just restart VLC). Requires VLC 3.0.x (tested target 3.0.x).
10	4. Enable it from the **View** menu -> **Badminton Rally Annotator**.
11	   The dialog appears (it stays open while you watch).
12	
13	> Tip: if you run the `timeosd` interface
14	> (`%APPDATA%\vlc\lua\intf\timeosd.lua`) you also get a live on-screen clock
15	> while you mark -- the two work together.
16	
17	## 2. Rate workflow
18	1. Open the badminton video in VLC and start playback. Pause/scrub freely.
19	2. When a rally begins, click **Mark START** (snapshots the current time).
20	3. Pick the point-stop reason in the **Ending reason** dropdown
21	   (winner / forced_error / unforced_error / service_fault / let / other).
22	4. When the rally ends, click **Mark END** -- one CSV row is appended
23	   immediately. The dropdown value at that moment is what gets written.
24	5. Made a mistake? **Undo last** disarms an uncommitted START, or removes the
25	   last written row (one level of undo).
26	6. The status panel shows: last action, current time, whether a START is armed,
27	   rallies written, and the **CSV output path**.
28	
29	**Output path:** written next to the video as `<video-stem>.rallies.csv`
30	when VLC can resolve the file path; otherwise it falls back to your home dir
31	(`%USERPROFILE%\rally_labels.csv`). The exact path is always shown in the dialog.
32	
33	**CSV format:** header `rally_number,start_time,end_time,ending_reason`,
34	times in **decimal seconds**. Re-enabling the extension keeps appending to the
35	same file (it does not truncate) and **continues** rally numbering from the
36	highest number already in the file, so you never get duplicate `rally_number`s.
37	
38	## 3. Ingest with the in-repo loaders
39	The CSV is consumed unchanged by this repo's golden-set tooling, which reads
40	`start_time`/`end_time` (decimal seconds) via `csv.DictReader` and drops any
41	row with `end <= start`:
42	
43	```
44	# Per-video WASB calibration against your labels:
45	python -m backend.eval.calibrate_wasb --trajectory full_traj.csv \
46	  --labels "C:\path\to\match.rallies.csv" --fps 59.94 --frame-width 1920
47	
48	# Slice rally clips out of the match to eyeball boundaries:
49	python -m backend.tools.rally_slicer --video "C:\path\to\match.mp4" \
50	  --labels "C:\path\to\match.rallies.csv" --rallies all
51	```
52	
53	> **Other repo:** the separate **sports-data-collector** repo exposes an
54	> `import-local` command for the same CSV; that command does **not** live in
55	> this repository, so run it from the collector checkout:
56	> `python -m src.cli.curate import-local --sport badminton --video-path ... --annotations-path match.rallies.csv`.
57	
58	## 4. Notes & gotchas
59	- VLC's `time` is in MICROSECONDS; the extension divides by 1e6 -> seconds.
60	- It does not auto-react to pause on purpose (that callback is broken in VLC 4.0
61	  and flaky on macOS). You explicitly click START/END, which is more precise.
62	- Keep `ending_reason` to the 6 dropdown values -- downstream QA enforces the
63	  closed vocabulary; the loaders carry it verbatim and do not validate it.
64	- A rally must be > 0s: if END is at/before START it is either rejected (equal)
65	  or swapped to a positive interval (END earlier than START), keeping the file
66	  free of degenerate rows the loaders would drop anyway.
67	- The status panel is a rich-text (HTML) widget, so multi-line output renders
68	  reliably across VLC 3.x Qt builds.
69

## 🎬 VLC Rally Annotator — ready to use

**Built, verified (high confidence), and installed.** Restart VLC (or **Tools → Plugins and extensions → Reload extensions**), then enable it from the **View menu → "Badminton Rally Annotator"**.

**Rate workflow:**
1. Open a badminton video, play/scrub freely.
2. At a rally's first serve → **Mark START** (snapshots the exact time).
3. Pick the **Ending reason** in the dropdown (winner / forced_error / unforced_error / service_fault / let / other).
4. At rally end → **Mark END** → one row is appended instantly. **Undo last** fixes mistakes (one level).

**Output:** `<video-stem>.rallies.csv` next to the video, columns `rally_number,start_time,end_time,ending_reason` in decimal seconds. Re-enabling keeps appending and **continues numbering** (no duplicate IDs — that was a bug the verify pass caught and fixed). The dialog always shows the live path + rally count.

**It ingests unchanged** into:
- `python -m backend.eval.calibrate_wasb --labels <your>.rallies.csv ...` (calibration/distillation)
- `python -m backend.tools.rally_slicer --labels <your>.rallies.csv --rallies all` (eyeball clips)
- the collector's `import-local` (separate repo)

**Honest caveat:** it's verified against the real VLC 3.x Lua API and your CSV loaders, but I can't click through VLC myself — so the first launch is your smoke test. If a button misbehaves, paste the error and I'll fix the v1.1. (Deliberately button-driven, not auto-on-pause — that VLC callback is flaky on macOS / broken in VLC 4, and clicking lets you scrub to the exact frame first.)

**If the grid dialog feels cramped:** the workflow flagged two stronger alternatives — a python-vlc+Tkinter app with global hotkeys (S/E/1–6/U), or an HTML5 page (zero-install, shareable with remote raters like Roora). Say the word and I'll build either.

### 497. user (2026-06-07T01:19:21.209Z)

1	# Architecture Decision Records
2	
3	## ADR-001: Precise shuttle tracking via self-hosted TrackNetV4/WASB on WSL2
4	
5	**Date:** 2026-05-25
6	**Status:** Accepted
7	
8	### Context
9	
10	The pipeline uses an AI multimodal provider (Gemini) for *semantic* rally segmentation.
11	We need **precise frame-level detection of the moving shuttlecock** (and, generically,
12	balls for other sports) to produce higher-quality candidate rallies and a fine-tuning
13	signal.
14	
15	We evaluated whether a commercially available model/API could do this. Findings:
16	
17	- **Multimodal LLM APIs (Gemini, Claude, GPT-class) are the wrong tool for precise
18	  localization.** They reason well about *what* is happening (rally vs. dead time) but
19	  cannot reliably track a tiny, fast (~up to 400 km/h), motion-blurred shuttle frame by
20	  frame.
21	- The precision leaders are **purpose-built trajectory models**, which are open source and
22	  self-hosted, not big-vendor APIs:
23	  - **TrackNetV4** (TensorFlow) — badminton-specific, heatmap + motion-attention,
24	    highest reported shuttle accuracy; ships `src/predict.py` producing per-frame (X, Y)
25	    coordinate CSV. https://github.com/AR4152/TrackNetV4
26	  - **WASB** (PyTorch) — multi-sport strong baseline (badminton/tennis/volleyball/…),
27	    fits our `SportProfile` abstraction; needs a custom-video inference wrapper.
28	    https://github.com/nttcom/WASB-SBDT
29	- True turnkey commercial APIs (Roboflow hosted models) exist but cap below a tuned
30	  TrackNet; enterprise systems (Hawk-Eye, SkillCorner, TRACAB) are not API-accessible.
31	
32	### Decision
33	
34	1. Adopt **self-hosted TrackNetV4** as the primary precise shuttle detector, with **WASB**
35	   as the multi-sport fallback.
36	2. Run these models under **WSL2 (Ubuntu)**, **not** native Ubuntu. The entire indexer
37	   already runs on Windows; WSL2 keeps a single dev environment and quarantines the
38	   TensorFlow/PyTorch dependencies. Native Ubuntu would require dual-boot and migrating
39	   the whole project for marginal gain.
40	3. Integrate via a **subprocess adapter**: a detector module that shells out to the WSL
41	   `predict.py`, reads the coordinate CSV, and converts trajectory → candidate rallies
42	   feeding the existing stitching flow. This keeps the TF dependency fully isolated from
43	   the main Python environment.
44	
45	### Consequences
46	
47	- **WSL I/O caveat:** `/mnt/c` access is slow. Stage match videos inside the WSL
48	  filesystem (e.g. `~/clips/`) before inference rather than reading large mp4s across the
49	  Windows↔WSL boundary.
50	- TrackNetV4 (TensorFlow) and WASB (PyTorch) live in **separate conda envs**.
51	- Next step after integration: manual video annotation → fine-tune the detector.
52	
53	---
54	
55	## ADR-002: Use the sports-data-collector's rally annotations as a golden eval + temporal-segmenter training set
56	
57	**Date:** 2026-05-26
58	**Status:** Accepted
59	
60	### Context
61	
62	A sibling project, **sports-data-collector**, curates sports videos and stores per-rally
63	`[start, end)` timestamps (plus stroke counts, scores) in an SQLite DB, with a
64	commercial-license flag per source. We asked whether this data could improve the
65	indexer's rally detection, and for which task.
66	
67	Key findings:
68	
69	- The annotations are **temporal-only** (when rallies start/end). They do **not** contain
70	  per-frame shuttle `(x, y)` coordinates, so they **cannot train the shuttle detector**
71	  (TrackNetV4/WASB — see ADR-001), which needs coordinate labels.
72	- They are an excellent **golden evaluation set** and a **training signal for the
73	  *temporal* rally segmenter** (active vs. dead play over time) — the stage that is today
74	  a hand-tuned heuristic, not a learned model.
75	- The collector's five sports tables (`badminton_rallies`, `tennis_points`,
76	  `cricket_deliveries`, `pickleball_rallies`, `tabletennis_rallies`) all share a
77	  `(video_id, ordinal, start_time, end_time)` shape, and the indexer already has
78	  `sport_registry` / `segmenter_registry`. So a *generic* `(start, end)` bridge serves
79	  every sport.
80	- The two repos key videos differently: the collector by a human `video_id`, the indexer
81	  by **file MD5**. A re-encode/re-download changes the bytes, hence the MD5.
82	
83	### Decision
84	
85	1. Treat the collector's rally timestamps as (a) the **golden eval set** for rally
86	   *segmentation* and (b) the **labels** for training a temporal segmenter — explicitly
87	   **not** as shuttle-detector training data.
88	2. Bridge the repos with a sport-agnostic **export** (`curate export` in the collector):
89	   per-video `start,end` CSVs (the indexer's eval format) + a `manifest.json` that pairs
90	   each CSV with its video file. Gate on `is_commercial` so only commercially usable data
91	   flows through.
92	3. Honour the **MD5 keying** contract: evaluation/processing must run on the exact video
93	   file recorded in the manifest; acquisition (ADR-003) therefore precedes processing.
94	
95	### Consequences
96	
97	- The collector becomes the *producer* of labels; the indexer the *consumer* (processing +
98	  scoring + training). Neither takes a hard dependency on the other beyond the manifest.
99	- Scoring is free and deterministic (reads the DB; no API). See `docs/EVALUATION.md`.
100	- Only invest in frame-level coordinate labels (for the detector) **if** evaluation shows
101	  *detection*, not *segmentation*, is the bottleneck.
102	
103	---
104	
105	## ADR-003: Video acquisition — always best resolution, upgrade in place, authenticated cookies
106	
107	**Date:** 2026-05-26
108	**Status:** Accepted
109	
110	### Context
111	
112	The collector's initial crawl downloaded videos with `yt-dlp -f worstvideo` (256×144),
113	mislabelled as 1080p. The indexer needs ≥720p MP4 for meaningful YOLO/shuttle detection.
114	Re-acquiring full-resolution sources surfaced several hard-won realities:
115	
116	- The ShuttleSet corpus **tops out at 1080p** (no 4K); "best available" resolves to 1080p.
117	- Authenticating with a **YouTube Premium** account is required for reliable, unthrottled
118	  downloads — anonymous downloads are throttled (~1.3 MiB/s) and **non-deterministic**
119	  (identical commands variously yielded 1080p DASH, 1080p HLS, or a 144p fallback).
120	- **Chrome** cookies can't be read on Windows (app-bound encryption / DPAPI, yt-dlp
121	  #10927); **Firefox** cookies read cleanly even while open.
122	- The `web`/`web_safari` player clients are forced into **SABR streaming**, which strips
123	  download URLs and silently drops to 144p (yt-dlp #12482). yt-dlp's `default` client
124	  serves real downloadable 1080p DASH with auth cookies.
125	- Bulk bursts trip YouTube **rate-limiting** (a 22-video burst failed all at once, though
126	  each worked in isolation).
127	
128	### Decision
129	
130	1. **Always fetch the best resolution available** (`bv*+ba/b -S res,...`, merged to MP4);
131	   ffprobe the result and store the *actual* width/height/fps — never assume.
132	2. **Upgrade in place, never clean-slate:** the `fetch` command re-downloads a video and
133	   updates only `local_path`/`resolution`/`fps`, preserving every rally annotation and
134	   curation status (deleting would cascade-delete the valuable labels).
135	3. Use **authenticated cookies** (`--cookies-from-browser firefox` / `--cookies file`) and
136	   the **`default` player client**; enable the EJS challenge solver (deno).
137	4. **Throttle bulk runs** (`--sleep` + per-video `--retries`/backoff) and treat a
138	   below-min-height download as a **failure** (discard, don't commit) so a flaky low-res
139	   result never overwrites a good entry.
140	
141	### Consequences
142	
143	- All 22 fetchable ShuttleSet matches are now true 1080p (one match, shuttleset_12, has no
144	  source URL in the catalog and remains video-less but keeps its annotations).
145	- Acquisition is reproducible and resumable; the ~26 GB corpus is kept outside git.
146	- Future non-YouTube or 4K sources are supported by the same code (optional `max_height`).
147	
148	---
149	
150	## ADR-004: Add an offline, API-free *learned* rally segmenter as a first-class mode
151	
152	**Date:** 2026-05-26
153	**Status:** Proposed (substrate pending data)
154	
155	### Context
156	
157	The default high-quality path (`yolo_hybrid`) refines YOLO candidate windows with **Gemini**,
158	which costs money and requires network access. With a now-1080p, commercially-licensed,
159	rally-annotated corpus (ADR-002/003), we can learn the refinement step offline instead.
160	
161	Crucially, the **API-free signal already exists and is already persisted**: `yolo_hybrid`
162	Stage 1 is pure YOLO (player tracking → per-window motion features: peak/mean velocity,
163	active-frame count, track-id count) stored in `candidate_segments.stats`. Gemini is only
164	the Stage-2 refiner. So the rally annotations can **train a classifier to replace Gemini**,
165	with no external API and **no new labeling**.
166	
167	Three candidate substrates were weighed:
168	
169	- **Tune `motion` thresholds** on the annotations — trivial, fully offline, but a low
170	  ceiling (pixel motion can't separate rally from crowd/pan/replay). Use as a *floor*.
171	- **YOLO features → trained classifier** — reuses already-stored, semantically meaningful
172	  player-motion features; medium-high ceiling; moderate effort. *Recommended primary.*
173	- **WASB shuttle-motion → rally detector** — highest ceiling (the shuttle defines play),
174	  fully offline but heaviest (needs the WSL2 WASB runner, ADR-001); can't train WASB
175	  itself (no xy labels), only a rally layer on its output.
176	
177	### Decision
178	
179	1. Build an **offline, API-free learned segmenter** as a new registry mode (e.g.
180	   `yolo_learned`): label detector windows by overlap with GT rallies, train a lightweight
181	   classifier on the persisted features, emit final segments with **zero API calls**, and
182	   score it with the existing harness against `motion` and a Gemini reference.
183	2. **Choose the substrate from data, not assumption.** A learned refiner can only sharpen
184	   the windows the detector found, so the deciding metric is the detector's **candidate-stage
185	   recall** vs. the annotations: high recall → build on YOLO features; low recall → bring in
186	   WASB's shuttle signal to raise the ceiling.
187	3. Keep the Gemini-backed `yolo_hybrid` as an **upper-bound reference**, not a production
188	   dependency.
189	
190	### Consequences
191	
192	- The final tool gains a **self-contained, offline, free** rally mode trained on its own
193	  curated data — runnable air-gapped, improvable as more matches are annotated.
194	- The approach is **sport-generic**: the same label→feature→classifier machinery extends to
195	  tennis/cricket once those have annotations.
196	- The full paid 22-video Gemini evaluation is **not** run; spend is limited to a single
197	  reference video.
198	
199	### Update (2026-05-26): Phase 3 evidence — substrate is WASB, not YOLO
200	
201	The deciding candidate-recall measurement is in. On shuttleset_8 (64 GT rallies), pure
202	YOLO Stage-1 produced 181 candidate windows with:
203	
204	- **Recall @ IoU 0.5 = 0.05**, but recall @ IoU 0.1 = 0.53 and @ IoU 0.01 = 0.72.
205	- **72%** of rallies have *some* candidate overlap, yet the **median best coverage of a
206	  rally by any candidate is only ~20%**.
207	- Post-hoc **merging adjacent candidates does not help** (recall stays ~0.05 and degrades
208	  as the merge gap grows — windows overshoot rather than align).
209	
210	Interpretation: YOLO isn't *blind* to rallies, but its windows are **badly misaligned** —
211	because the **broadcast camera tracks the players**, their on-screen (pixel) velocity dips
212	*during* rallies and spikes on pans/cuts/replays. Player-pixel-motion is therefore a poor
213	rally proxy on broadcast footage (it was designed for *static* GoPro recordings). A
214	classifier that only *filters* these windows is capped at ~0.25 recall — below any useful
215	bar — and no simple boundary post-processing recovers it.
216	
217	**Decision update:** per this ADR's data-driven rule (low candidate recall → bring in the
218	shuttle signal), the offline segmenter's substrate is **WASB shuttle-motion** (ADR-001),
219	not YOLO player-motion. The shuttle's motion/presence is camera-invariant and directly
220	defines active play. The `motion` segmenter is likewise unusable here (0 TP — moving-camera
221	pixel motion is everywhere).
222	
223	**Caveats / next:** this is one video; the mechanism (camera tracking) generalizes but
224	should be confirmed on 2–3 more via a *free, local, candidates-only* YOLO pass (no Gemini).
225	Processing is also slow (~50 min per 1080p video) — the detection pass likely needs reduced
226	resolution and/or cached detector outputs to be practical across 22 matches. The
227	substrate-agnostic training-data + classifier layers already built still apply: they will
228	consume **WASB-derived** windows/features instead of YOLO's.
229	
230	---
231	
232	## ADR-005: Permissive Dependency Stack for the Hosted Monetization Path
233	
234	**Date:** 2026-05-30
235	**Status:** Accepted
236	
237	### Context
238	
239	The project will be monetized as a **closed-source hosted API** (users upload videos to a cloud GPU VM and get highlights). Because we never distribute code/binaries, GPL/LGPL distribution copyleft is not triggered — but **AGPL §13** (network clause) and **metered-API terms** are, and **video-codec patent royalties** are a separate concern from software licenses. A deep-research audit (2026-05-30, 25 adversarially-verified claims against primary sources — see `docs/MONETIZATION_AUDIT.md`) identified the blockers and clean alternatives. This ADR also **resolves the open licensing question in ADR-001/ADR-004** about WASB.
240	
241	### Decision
242	
243	1. **Drop `ultralytics`.** Both the library and the `yolov8n.pt` weights are **AGPL-3.0**; §13 makes them a blocker for a closed-source hosted service. Swapping the codebase while keeping the `.pt` does **not** escape — the weights are AGPL too. Replace player/motion-gating detection with a permissive detector: **torchvision Faster R-CNN / RetinaNet (BSD, default — zero new deps, person = COCO class 0)**, escalating to **YOLOX** or **RT-DETRv2 (Baidu repo, Apache-2.0)** if more speed/accuracy is needed. **Ultralytics Enterprise License** is a paid fallback only (quote-only pricing).
244	2. **Shuttle tracking stays permissive.** **WASB-SBDT and TrackNetV3 are MIT (CLEAR for commercial use)** — this resolves the licensing unknown flagged in ADR-001/ADR-004. **MonoTrack (Adobe Research, noncommercial) and YOLO-NAS weights (Deci, noncommercial) are banned.** Train our own checkpoints (or confirm per-checkpoint terms), since distributed research weights/datasets aren't covered by the MIT *code* grants.
245	3. **LLM step — keep all three strategies open:** (A) eliminate via an offline temporal segmenter (**ActionFormer, MIT** — the Phase-4 direction), (B) self-host **Qwen2.5-VL-7B/32B (Apache-2.0)** or **InternVL2.5 (MIT)**, or (C) keep **Gemini 2.5 Flash on the paid tier** (required — the free tier trains on and human-reviews uploaded video). The `google-genai` SDK itself is Apache-2.0 (clear).
246	4. **Emit royalty-free output.** Standardize highlight output on **AV1 (+ Opus)** to avoid AVC/HEVC encode-side patent exposure; provision an **Ada-class GPU (L4/L40)** for hardware AV1 (NVENC), falling back to **SVT-AV1 (CPU, BSD-3-Clause-Clear)** on T4/A10/A100.
247	
248	### Consequences
249	
250	- Eliminates the only two hard legal blockers; the rest of the stack (torch, opencv, fastapi, numpy, lapx, ffmpeg-as-subprocess, google-genai SDK) is already permissive.
251	- Detector swap is contained to `backend/indexer/yolo_hybrid.py` (the `from ultralytics import YOLO` load + the `model.track(...)` call) plus `config.json` knobs and `tests/test_yolo_hybrid.py` mocks. Stage-1 feature outputs (velocity/active-frame/track-id stats) and Stages 2–4 are detector-agnostic and unchanged.
252	- We now own the frame loop + tracking (ultralytics did both internally). Use a permissive ByteTrack (e.g. `supervision`, MIT, or the MIT ByteTrack repo) on top of `lapx` (already a BSD dep).
253	- Residual items needing legal sign-off: exact codec-pool royalty thresholds (AVC/HEVC decode of uploads), Ultralytics Enterprise pricing, and per-checkpoint weight licenses — tracked in `docs/MONETIZATION_AUDIT.md` §7.
254	- The `yolo_hybrid` registry key is now a slight misnomer (no YOLO); keep it for back-compat or rename in a follow-up.
255	
256	---
257	
258	## ADR-006: Golden-Set Generation via Vendor Bake-off + a Pluggable Annotation Provider
259	
260	**Date:** 2026-05-30
261	**Status:** Accepted (process v1; vendor shortlist pending research)
262	
263	### Context
264	
265	The product needs **golden-set ground truth** (hand-verified rally `[start,end)` boundaries) to (a) measure pipeline quality and (b) run **on-demand / surprise regression tests** against the production tool. Two forces shape the design: the hosted-API monetization goal raises a "who views the user's video" concern (ADR-005 / MONETIZATION_AUDIT.md), and the founder is **Bangalore-based** and prefers **local/India vendors**. We already own a deterministic interval scorer (`backend/eval/metrics.py`) and a growing collection of **licensed/owned** match videos.
266	
267	Decisions locked with the owner: **licensed/owned video only** (no user uploads to vendors) · **rally `[start,end)` annotation depth** · **tiered turnaround** (fast automated + slow human) · **vendor bake-off** to choose · **Bangalore/India-local preference**.
268	
269	### Decision
270	
271	1. **One scorer, three jobs.** A vendor's `[start,end)` output is structurally identical to a prediction, so `metrics.py` grades product-vs-truth, vendor-vs-key, and annotator-vs-annotator (IAA) with no new scoring code.
272	2. **Select vendors by measured quality-per-dollar**, via a bake-off: write a precise rally-definition guideline, build a small internal gold key (5–10 licensed clips), send the same clips to 2–3 vendors (favoring Bangalore/India), and grade every submission with our own harness. The numbers are the decision.
273	3. **Pluggable `AnnotationProvider` registry** mirroring the existing registry pattern: `FileProvider` (today's manual/ShuttleSet flow), `HumanVendorProvider` (slow tier — authoritative truth), `AutomatedProvider` (fast tier — independent model oracle).
274	4. **Regression = existing harness + one new component.** `regress(video)` calls a provider for ground truth, runs the production pipeline, and scores with the unchanged `metrics.py` against a per-video `baseline.json`.
275	5. **Privacy by construction.** Golden sets use only licensed/owned footage; all third-party video egress flows through the single provider boundary where DPA / no-reuse / delete-on-delivery / access-logging controls live.
276	
277	### Consequences
278	
279	- Eliminates the "human views user video" concern for golden-set/regression work, because user uploads never enter the pipeline.
280	- **The oracle problem is a first-class caveat:** the fast-tier `AutomatedProvider` must be a *different model lineage* than production (else shared blind spots make the test falsely green); fast-tier alarms escalate to the slow human tier before blocking a release.
281	- New docs: `docs/GOLDEN_SET_VENDORING.md` (the full process v1) and `docs/VIDEO_RECORDING_GUIDELINES.md` (a tracked draft: capture-quality thresholds to be derived empirically from precision/recall, since input quality bounds output quality — cf. ADR-004's broadcast-camera finding).
282	- The vendor shortlist itself is **pending a primary-source research pass** (favoring India/Bangalore) and is intentionally left blank until verified — no un-sourced vendor claims.
283	- **Honest limitation:** a licensed/owned corpus may not fully represent real user capture, so regression greens are strong evidence, not guarantees — the recording-guidelines work aims to narrow that gap.
284	
285	### Update (2026-06-02): shortlist researched; Roora selected for the initial pilot
286	
287	- The **vendor shortlist** is filled in (GOLDEN_SET_VENDORING.md §6, primary-source pass 2026-05-30).
288	- The founder selected **Roora ML Pvt Ltd** (HSR Layout, Bengaluru) for the **initial paid pilot**, diverging from the doc's iMerit/Taskmonk default toward a Bangalore-local, startup-friendly shop. Roora's existence, image/video/temporal capability, and location are verified (rooragrp.com); **pilot quality is still to be measured** — run as a **single-vendor pilot first** (the pilot *is* the calibration), not a multi-vendor bake-off.
289	- **Step 0 — the annotation guideline — is authored** (the highest-leverage artifact): collector repo `docs/annotation/` — `ROORA_BRIEF.md` (rally CSV schema), `ANNOTATION_GUIDE.md` (illustrated guide), plus intake-manifest and NDA templates.
290	- **Pilot scope is 3 sports** (badminton, pickleball, table tennis); the collector's `import-local` ingestion for all three is implemented (collector ADR-001).
291	- **Still pending:** the internal **gold key** (5–10 expert-labeled clips) and the vendor-dependent **GS-3 `HumanVendorProvider`** — GS-1/GS-2/GS-4 (FileProvider, regression runner, automated-oracle scaffold) have already landed (see GOLDEN_SET_IMPLEMENTATION.md).
292

### 498. user (2026-06-07T01:19:21.771Z)

1	# Video Recording Guidelines (DRAFT — thresholds pending empirical data)
2	
3	**Status:** 🚧 Draft / placeholder · **Date:** 2026-05-30
4	**Why this exists:** highlight quality is bounded by *input* quality. To get reliably good auto-generated highlights, the source video must be recorded a certain way (camera height, court position, resolution, frame rate, stability, framing). We want to (1) **derive** the minimum acceptable capture spec **empirically** from the precision/recall of the solutions we build, and (2) **publish user-facing best practices** so a user knows how to record footage that the product can index well.
5	
6	> **This is a tracked thought, not a finished standard.** The exact numeric thresholds below are **placeholders** to be replaced with values measured against our golden set. See "How we'll set the real numbers" (§3). Captured now (per the team's working agreement) so it isn't lost; to be revisited.
7	>
8	> **Update (2026-06-02):** the **frame-rate** and **camera-geometry** rows now carry **literature-backed starting values** (cited inline). They remain *starting points* to confirm empirically per §3. The golden-set **corpus** diversity spec (what footage to collect, not just how users should record) is now in [GOLDEN_SET_PHASE1_VIDEOS.md](GOLDEN_SET_PHASE1_VIDEOS.md).
9	
10	---
11	
12	## 1. Why input capture matters (grounded in what we already know)
13	
14	ADR-004 found a concrete, capture-dependent failure mode: on **broadcast** footage the camera *tracks the players*, so player pixel-motion dips during rallies and spikes on pans/replays — making player-motion a poor rally proxy. The lesson generalizes: **the pipeline's accuracy is conditional on how the video was shot.** A **static, full-court** camera (typical of a fixed tripod recording) behaves very differently from broadcast — and is likely much friendlier to both the motion/detector stages and the shuttle-tracking substrate (WASB/TrackNet, ADR-001).
15	
16	So capture guidance isn't cosmetic — it directly moves precision/recall.
17	
18	---
19	
20	## 2. The dimensions that likely matter (hypotheses to test)
21	
22	| Dimension | Why it plausibly affects P/R | Placeholder guidance (UNVERIFIED) |
23	|---|---|---|
24	| **Resolution** | Shuttle is tiny & fast; too few pixels → detector misses it | ≥ **1080p** preferred; **720p** likely floor; 2K/4K may help shuttle tracking but ↑ compute/cost |
25	| **Frame rate** | Motion blur; boundary precision; fast-ball detection | ≥ **30 fps** badminton/pickleball; **≥ 60 fps table tennis** — 30 fps collapses fast-ball detection (**22.5% → 93.2%** at 60 fps; 120 ≈ 60, [Nature Sci Rep](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41598-023-42966-6)) |
26	| **Camera position** | Static full-court view avoids the broadcast camera-tracking trap (ADR-004) | **Fixed tripod**, elevated, behind/above one baseline, whole court in frame |
27	| **Camera height & angle** | Occlusion of players/shuttle; court keystoning | **3–5 m** elevated (~4.5 m), **~30° down-tilt**, behind one baseline, whole court + net headroom ([badminton-CV setup](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10747833/)) |
28	| **Framing / margins** | Cropping the court or players breaks detection & relevance check | Entire court + a margin; players never leave frame |
29	| **Stability** | Handheld jitter inflates motion signals | Tripod / fixed mount; no panning or zoom during play |
30	| **Lighting / exposure** | Blur & contrast affect detection and the existing blur/relevance validators | Even indoor lighting; avoid backlight/glare; minimize motion blur |
31	| **Court contrast / background** | Relevance check uses court color; busy crowds add false motion | Clear court markings; minimal moving background where possible |
32	| **Codec / container** | Pipeline already validates MP4 + decodes H.264/HEVC | MP4 (H.264/HEVC) as today; align with MONETIZATION_AUDIT.md codec notes |
33	
34	These map onto checks that **already exist** in `backend/validators/` (resolution, fps, blur, scene-cut, court relevance) — so several can be enforced/measured programmatically rather than only advised.
35	
36	---
37	
38	## 3. How we'll set the REAL numbers (empirical plan)
39	
40	Replace every placeholder above with a value derived from data, using the golden-set harness:
41	
42	1. Take golden-set clips (GOLDEN_SET_VENDORING.md) spanning a **range** of each dimension — e.g. the same match re-encoded at 480p/720p/1080p/4K, or clips at different camera heights.
43	2. Run the production pipeline; score with `backend/eval/metrics.py`.
44	3. Plot **precision/recall vs. each dimension** → find the knee where quality collapses.
45	4. Set the **published minimum** at (knee + safety margin); set a **"recommended"** tier above it.
46	5. Re-derive when the pipeline changes materially (new detector/segmenter shifts the knees).
47	
48	This turns "record in 1080p" from an assertion into a measured threshold — and lets us tell users *why*.
49	
50	---
51	
52	## 4. Two surfaces for the output
53	
54	1. **User-facing best-practices guide** — short, friendly, illustrated: "Put the camera here, this high, record at ≥X, keep it still." The goal is that a user can record once and get great results.
55	2. **Programmatic pre-flight check** — extend `backend/validators/` to *reject or warn* on out-of-spec uploads at ingest (resolution/fps/blur/framing), so the product fails fast with actionable feedback instead of producing poor highlights silently.
56	
57	---
58	
59	## 5. Open questions
60	1. Which dimension dominates — resolution, camera staticness, or height? (Run the ablations in §3 to rank them.)
61	2. Do we **enforce** minimums (reject uploads) or only **warn**? Likely warn first, enforce once thresholds are trustworthy.
62	3. Single fixed-camera spec, or several supported "recording profiles" (e.g. baseline-elevated vs. side-elevated)?
63	4. How does the owned/licensed golden corpus need to grow to *cover* the capture conditions real users will actually use? (Feeds GOLDEN_SET_VENDORING.md §8.)
64

### 501. assistant (2026-06-07T01:20:51.319Z)

# Audio-based rally detection — detailed plan

**Status:** Accepted direction (ADR-007) · experiments gated on golden eval labels · **Date:** 2026-06-07
**Decision in one line:** add a fully-local **audio "thwack" hit-detection cue, fused with the WASB shuttle
trajectory**, as the next rally-detection lever — because it directly attacks our recall bottleneck using an
input we currently throw away. Pose/stance detection is the parked #2 (see ADR-007 / [research](RALLY_DETECTION_RESEARCH.md)).

## 1. Why audio (grounded in the research)
- **#1 cheap local lever** in the verified survey (`docs/RALLY_DETECTION_RESEARCH.md`). Audio+visual **fusion
  lifts rally RECALL** — tennis HMM: 83% fused vs 54% visual-only / 63% audio-only at ~unchanged precision —
  and recall is exactly where WASB-derived windows fail us (IoU-0.5 candidate recall ceilinged at ~0.43 on
  TestLargeVideo).
- **Fully local, permissive, CPU-light** (classical MFCC/energy methods; no restrictive weights) → keeps the
  privacy lever and adds ~zero marginal cost.
- **Uses an input we currently ignore.** Our GoPro clips already carry audio.

## 2. What we detect, and the rally model
- **Hit event** = racket–shuttle contact ("thwack"), a short broadband transient.
- **Rally** = a temporally-clustered run of hits with rhythmic cadence (a shot roughly every ~0.4–2 s,
  alternating ends). **Dead time** = no/sparse hits. A **single isolated hit** = the between-point service
  feed/toss — i.e. exactly the false-fire pattern we've been fighting. So hit *clusters*, not single hits,
  define rallies.

## 3. The GoPro-audio reality (honest, and the key risk)
All clips provided so far have audio (GoPro records it by default). BUT: a shuttle "thwack" is **much quieter**
than a tennis/table-tennis impact (lighter projectile), so the **SNR transfer is the dominant open risk** — the
research's audio numbers come from louder sports. Gym reverb, wind, and crowd add noise. **Mitigations:**
1. **E1 (below) is an explicit GO/NO-GO probe on the real GoPro audio** before we build anything heavy.
2. Make **"record with audio enabled, mic unobstructed"** a capture requirement (added to
   `docs/VIDEO_RECORDING_GUIDELINES.md`).

## 4. Architecture (all local, CPU-light)
- **Stage 0 — Extract:** `ffmpeg` → mono WAV (~16–22 kHz; keep HF — clicks are broadband). Cached per video,
  keyed like the frame/trajectory caches.
- **Stage 1 — Onset/peak candidates (NO training):** band-emphasis/high-pass → short-time energy + spectral
  flux → adaptive (EMA/median) threshold → peak-pick → `(timestamp, strength)` candidate impacts. (Research E1.)
- **Stage 2 — Hit confidence / FP suppression:** a likelihood-ratio confidence (research: raw peaks ~41–50 F →
  ~68–77 F) and optionally a tiny MFCC-window classifier (GMM/logreg, hit-vs-not), bootstrapped from the
  user's golden rally spans (hits inside rallies = positives). (Research E2.)
- **Stage 3 — Hits → rally structure:** cluster impacts by inter-hit gap; a cluster with ≥K hits and plausible
  cadence = a rally; **START ≈ first hit (the serve), END ≈ last hit + a short tail**; drop isolated single
  hits (the feed).
- **Stage 4 — Fuse with the WASB trajectory:**
  - **Recall recovery (the main win):** propose a candidate rally wherever there's a dense hit-cluster but WASB
    proposed nothing.
  - **Precision / features:** add per-candidate audio features — `hit_count`, `hit_rate`, inter-hit-interval
    mean/std (cadence regularity), `time_since_last_hit`, end-of-window energy — to the existing
    `distill_local` student (Research E3).
  - **Joint rule:** a rally needs shuttle-motion **and** a hit-cluster (precision), OR an audio cluster
    recovers a WASB miss (recall).
- **Stage 5 — Eval:** vs **golden labels** (the rater CSVs from the VLC annotator) at IoU 0.5, leave-one-video-
  out; report recall lift + precision vs the WASB-only baseline and the trajectory-only distilled model.

## 5. Experiments (sequenced — E1 is the gate)
| # | Experiment | Needs | Decides |
|---|---|---|---|
| **E1** | Extract audio → energy-peak hits → overlay hit-density on WASB windows **and** golden labels | audio + ≥1 golden-rated video | **GO/NO-GO**: do hit-clusters align with real rallies + recover WASB-missed ones on *real GoPro* audio? |
| **E2** | Add likelihood-ratio / MFCC confidence to suppress false peaks | E1 + a few labeled hits | F-score lift of the hit stream |
| **E3** | Add audio features to `distill_local`; re-eval IoU-0.5 LOVO | E1/E2 + ≥2 golden videos | does fused beat WASB-only + trajectory-only distilled? |
| **E4** | Use first/last hit to tighten rally start/end | E1 | boundary-IoU improvement |

## 6. Code & dependencies
- New module `backend/audio/hit_detector.py` (extract + onset/peak + confidence). Pure DSP helpers unit-tested
  on **synthetic click audio** (no fixtures); ffmpeg via subprocess (already a dep).
- Reuse: feed audio features into `backend/eval/distill_local.py` (pluggable `feature_fn`); score with the
  existing `metrics.py` / golden loaders.
- **Deps:** prefer **numpy + scipy (BSD)** for filtering/STFT (numpy already present; scipy is permissive and
  light). Avoid heavy/AGPL audio stacks. Any later small CNN = trained in-house (no restrictive weights).
- **Licensing:** all permissive — no new model weights for Stage 1. Consistent with ADR-005.

## 7. Risks & mitigations
- **Shuttle SNR (top risk):** E1 is the empirical gate; if NO-GO, audio is deferred, not forced.
- **No crowd applause** in amateur single-camera clips (a cue the literature leans on for end-of-point) → rely
  on **hit-cadence cessation**, not applause.
- **Mic occlusion / wind** → recording guideline (audio on, unobstructed, avoid aggressive wind-suppression).
- **A/V sync:** audio and frames share the same file timeline → no sync work needed.

## 8. Gating & sequencing
- All measurement is **gated on golden labels** (user rating via the VLC annotator → `import-local` / the
  in-repo `--labels` loaders). So: rate a few videos → **E1 go/no-go** → E2/E3.
- Roadmap order: **AUDIO (now)** → *[a TBD owner hunch — slot reserved]* → **POSE/STANCE (#2, parked;
  expected substantially harder)**.

## 9. Success criteria
- **E1:** audio hit-clusters recover a meaningful share of WASB-missed golden rallies on real GoPro audio
  (recall lift), with hit-clusters aligning to true rallies. Pass → proceed; fail → defer audio, revisit mic/setup.
- **E3:** the fused model's held-out (LOVO) F1 beats both the WASB-only baseline and the trajectory-only
  distilled student.

### 517. assistant (2026-06-07T01:23:53.699Z)

## 🔊 The audio plan — committed & documented (PR #32)

Everything's checked in: **ADR-007** (the decision), **`docs/AUDIO_RALLY_DETECTION_PLAN.md`** (the detailed plan), the **research report**, the **"audio always on" recording requirement**, and the **VLC annotator**. Here's the plan in plain terms.

**Core idea:** a rally is a *cluster of hits with rhythmic cadence*; dead time has no hits; a **single isolated "thwack" = the between-point service feed** (exactly our false-fire). So the audio doesn't replace WASB — it **fuses** with it, and its biggest job is **recall**: propose a rally wherever there's a hit-cluster but WASB found nothing (the gap that ceilinged us at ~0.43).

**Pipeline (all local, CPU-light, permissive — numpy+scipy):**
1. `ffmpeg` → mono WAV.
2. Onset/energy-peak detection (no training) → candidate impact timestamps.
3. Confidence/false-positive suppression (likelihood-ratio + a tiny MFCC classifier) — the research showed raw peaks are weak (~41–50 F) but post-processing lifts them to ~68–77.
4. Cluster hits → rally: **START ≈ first hit (serve), END ≈ last hit + tail**; drop lone hits.
5. Fuse with WASB: recall-recovery + audio features (`hit_count`, `hit_rate`, cadence regularity, `time_since_last_hit`) into the distilled student.

**On your GoPro-audio doubt — you're right to flag it, and it's the #1 risk.** A shuttle "thwack" is *much quieter* than a tennis/table-tennis impact, so SNR transfer is genuinely uncertain. That's why **E1 is an explicit GO/NO-GO probe on your real GoPro audio** before we build anything heavy: extract audio → peak-detect → overlay hit-density on a video you've rated → does it (a) align with real rallies and (b) recover the ones WASB misses? If E1 fails, we defer audio honestly rather than sink effort. Making **"record with audio on, mic unobstructed"** a camera-setup requirement (now in the guidelines) is the right move — it's a cheap insurance that protects the cue, and yes, please add it to your camera-setup doc.

**Sequencing locked:** **audio now → [your other hunch — slot reserved] → pose/stance (#2, parked, the harder one).**

**What unblocks E1:** one or two videos rated with the **VLC annotator** (so I have golden rally boundaries to score against). The moment you've rated a clip, I run E1 and we get a real answer on shuttle-audio efficacy.

When you're ready to share that hunch, I'm all ears — and whenever a rated CSV exists, ping me and I'll kick off E1. Open PR to merge: **#32**.

1	--[[ rally_annotator.lua  --  VLC Lua EXTENSION (v1.1)
2	
3	  Mark badminton rally START / END + a point-stop reason while watching, and
4	  append one CSV row per rally that this repo's golden-set loaders (and the
5	  separate sports-data-collector `import-local` command) ingest directly.
6	
7	  Output CSV columns (decimal SECONDS):
8	      rally_number,start_time,end_time,ending_reason
9	
10	  WHY a button-dialog (not an auto-pause hook):
11	    VLC 3.x can expose pause via the {"playing-listener"} capability
12	    (status_changed/playing_changed), but that callback is flaky on macOS
13	    (VLC #22778) and the input/meta listeners are broken in VLC 4.0 (#27558).
14	    A persistent dialog whose button callbacks SNAPSHOT the current playback
15	    time is portable, precise, and lets the rater pause/scrub freely before
16	    committing a mark. So we use NO listener capabilities.
17	
18	  UNITS: in VLC 3.x, vlc.var.get(input,"time") is in MICROSECONDS -> /1e6.
19	
20	  Install (Windows): copy this file to
21	      %APPDATA%\vlc\lua\extensions\rally_annotator.lua
22	  then VLC > Tools > Plugins and extensions > Reload extensions (or restart),
23	  then enable it from the View menu.
24	]]
25	
26	--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
27	-- Extension registration
28	--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
29	function descriptor()
30	  return {
31	    title       = "Badminton Rally Annotator",
32	    version     = "1.1",
33	    author      = "badminton-highlight-indexer",
34	    url         = "",
35	    shortdesc   = "Mark rally start/end + reason to a collector CSV",
36	    description = "Persistent dialog to mark rally START/END and a point-stop "
37	               .. "reason; appends rally_number,start_time,end_time,ending_reason "
38	               .. "(decimal seconds) to a CSV for the golden-set loaders.",
39	    capabilities = {}   -- button-click model: no listeners needed
40	  }
41	end
42	
43	--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
44	-- State
45	--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
46	local d                 -- the single dialog
47	local w_status          -- status label widget (HTML/rich-text)
48	local w_reason          -- ending_reason dropdown widget
49	local pending_start     -- number (seconds) or nil
50	local rally_count = 0   -- highest rally_number written to the CSV so far
51	local last_line_len = nil -- byte length of the last appended line (for Undo)
52	local out_path          -- resolved CSV path (computed once on activate)
53	
54	local HEADER = "rally_number,start_time,end_time,ending_reason\n"
55	
56	local REASONS = {
57	  "winner", "forced_error", "unforced_error", "service_fault", "let", "other"
58	}
59	
60	--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
61	-- Helpers
62	--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
63	
64	-- Current playback time in SECONDS, or nil if nothing is playing.
65	local function now_seconds()
66	  local input = vlc.object.input()
67	  if not input then return nil end
68	  local t_us = vlc.var.get(input, "time")   -- MICROSECONDS in VLC 3.x
69	  if not t_us then return nil end
70	  return t_us / 1000000.0
71	end
72	
73	-- mm:ss.mmm for display only.
74	local function fmt_clock(s)
75	  if not s then return "--:--" end
76	  if s < 0 then s = 0 end
77	  local m   = math.floor(s / 60)
78	  local sec = s - m * 60
79	  return string.format("%d:%06.3f", m, sec)
80	end
81	
82	-- Minimal HTML-escape so paths/messages render literally in the rich-text label.
83	local function esc(text)
84	  text = tostring(text or "")
85	  text = text:gsub("&", "&amp;")
86	  text = text:gsub("<", "&lt;")
87	  text = text:gsub(">", "&gt;")
88	  return text
89	end
90	
91	-- Best-effort path to the currently playing media file (decoded from URI).
92	local function current_media_path()
93	  local input = vlc.object.input()
94	  if not input then return nil end
95	  local item = vlc.input.item()           -- VLC 3.x: vlc.input.item()
96	  if not item then return nil end
97	  local uri = item:uri()                   -- item:uri()
98	  if not uri or uri == "" then return nil end
99	  -- file:///C:/dir/clip.mp4  ->  C:\dir\clip.mp4
100	  local p = uri
101	  p = p:gsub("^file:///", "")
102	  p = p:gsub("^file://", "")
103	  -- percent-decode (e.g. %20 -> space) BEFORE separator normalization
104	  p = p:gsub("%%(%x%x)", function(h) return string.char(tonumber(h, 16)) end)
105	  p = p:gsub("/", "\\")
106	  return p
107	end
108	
109	-- Resolve a sensible default output path: next to the video if we can, else
110	-- the user's home (USERPROFILE on Windows, HOME elsewhere).
111	local function resolve_out_path()
112	  local media = current_media_path()
113	  if media then
114	    local dir, stem = media:match("^(.*[\\/])([^\\/]-)%.?[^\\/%.]*$")
115	    if dir and stem and stem ~= "" then
116	      return dir .. stem .. ".rallies.csv"
117	    end
118	    -- media with no clean stem: drop it next to the file anyway
119	    local d2 = media:match("^(.*[\\/])")
120	    if d2 then return d2 .. "rally_labels.csv" end
121	  end
122	  local home = os.getenv("USERPROFILE") or os.getenv("HOME") or "."
123	  local sep = home:find("\\") and "\\" or "/"
124	  if home:sub(-1) ~= sep then home = home .. sep end
125	  return home .. "rally_labels.csv"
126	end
127	
128	local function file_exists(path)
129	  local fh = io.open(path, "r")
130	  if fh then fh:close(); return true end
131	  return false
132	end
133	
134	-- Scan an existing CSV and return the highest integer rally_number in it, so a
135	-- re-enabled session keeps numbering monotonic instead of restarting at 1
136	-- (which would write duplicate rally_number keys the loaders dedupe-overwrite).
137	local function max_existing_rally()
138	  local f = io.open(out_path, "r")
139	  if not f then return 0 end
140	  local maxn = 0
141	  for line in f:lines() do
142	    local first = line:match("^%s*([^,]+)")
143	    if first then
144	      local n = tonumber(first)
145	      if n and n == math.floor(n) and n > maxn then maxn = n end
146	    end
147	  end
148	  f:close()
149	  return maxn
150	end
151	
152	local function set_status(msg)
153	  if not w_status then return end
154	  local tail = pending_start
155	      and string.format("START armed @ %s", esc(fmt_clock(pending_start)))
156	      or  "START not set"
157	  -- add_html / rich-text label: use <br> for line breaks (\n is NOT honored by
158	  -- the Qt label) and escape the path so backslashes/specials render literally.
159	  w_status:set_text(string.format(
160	    "%s<br>Now: %s &nbsp;|&nbsp; %s<br>Rallies written: %d<br>CSV: %s",
161	    esc(msg), esc(fmt_clock(now_seconds())), tail, rally_count, esc(out_path)))
162	  if d then d:update() end
163	end
164	
165	--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
166	-- CSV append (header written once if the file is new/empty)
167	--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
168	local function append_row(n, start_s, end_s, why)
169	  local exists = file_exists(out_path)
170	  local f, err = io.open(out_path, "a")
171	  if not f then return false, ("cannot open CSV: " .. tostring(err)) end
172	  if not exists then
173	    f:write(HEADER)
174	  end
175	  local line = string.format("%d,%.3f,%.3f,%s\n", n, start_s, end_s, why)
176	  f:write(line)
177	  f:close()
178	  last_line_len = #line   -- track byte length so Undo can truncate it
179	  return true
180	end
181	
182	-- Remove the last appended data line by rewriting the file without its tail.
183	-- Lua has no ftruncate, so we read all, drop the tail, and rewrite.
184	local function remove_last_row()
185	  if not last_line_len then return false, "nothing to undo" end
186	  local f = io.open(out_path, "rb")
187	  if not f then return false, "CSV not found" end
188	  local data = f:read("*a") or ""
189	  f:close()
190	  if #data < last_line_len then return false, "CSV shorter than last row" end
191	  local kept = data:sub(1, #data - last_line_len)
192	  -- If only the header remains, blank the file entirely so a fresh header
193	  -- is rewritten next time.
194	  if kept == HEADER then
195	    kept = ""
196	  end
197	  local wf = io.open(out_path, "wb")
198	  if not wf then return false, "cannot rewrite CSV" end
199	  wf:write(kept)
200	  wf:close()
201	  last_line_len = nil   -- only one level of undo
202	  return true
203	end
204	
205	--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
206	-- Button callbacks (VLC calls these on its main loop; MUST be global)
207	--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
208	function mark_start()
209	  local t = now_seconds()
210	  if not t then set_status("No media playing -- cannot mark START."); return end
211	  pending_start = t
212	  set_status(string.format("START marked @ %s", fmt_clock(t)))
213	end
214	
215	function mark_end()
216	  local t = now_seconds()
217	  if not t then set_status("No media playing -- cannot mark END."); return end
218	  if not pending_start then
219	    set_status("No START armed -- press \"Mark START\" first.")
220	    return
221	  end
222	  local s, e = pending_start, t
223	  if e <= s then
224	    -- Tolerate the rater pressing END before START position: swap if it yields
225	    -- a positive interval; reject a zero/degenerate one (e == s).
226	    if e == s then
227	      set_status(string.format(
228	        "END (%s) equals START (%s) -- mark END later (rally must be > 0s).",
229	        fmt_clock(e), fmt_clock(s)))
230	      return
231	    end
232	    s, e = e, s
233	  end
234	  local _, reason = w_reason:get_value()   -- (id, text); we want the text
235	  if not reason or reason == "" then reason = "other" end
236	  local n = rally_count + 1
237	  local ok, err = append_row(n, s, e, reason)
238	  if not ok then set_status("WRITE FAILED: " .. tostring(err)); return end
239	  rally_count = n
240	  pending_start = nil
241	  set_status(string.format(
242	    "Rally #%d saved: %s -> %s  [%s]", n, fmt_clock(s), fmt_clock(e), reason))
243	end
244	
245	function undo_last()
246	  -- If a START is armed but not yet committed, just disarm it.
247	  if pending_start and not last_line_len then
248	    pending_start = nil
249	    set_status("Cleared armed START (no row written yet).")
250	    return
251	  end
252	  local ok, err = remove_last_row()
253	  if not ok then set_status("Undo: " .. tostring(err)); return end
254	  if rally_count > 0 then rally_count = rally_count - 1 end
255	  set_status(string.format("Undid last rally. %d remaining in CSV.", rally_count))
256	end
257	
258	function refresh_now()
259	  set_status("Refreshed.")
260	end
261	
262	--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
263	-- Dialog construction
264	--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
265	local function create_dialog()
266	  d = vlc.dialog("Badminton Rally Annotator")
267	
268	  d:add_label("Ending reason (used on Mark END):", 1, 1, 1, 1)
269	  w_reason = d:add_dropdown(2, 1, 2, 1)
270	  for i, v in ipairs(REASONS) do
271	    w_reason:add_value(v, i)   -- add_value(text, id)
272	  end
273	
274	  d:add_button("Mark START", mark_start, 1, 2, 1, 1)
275	  d:add_button("Mark END",   mark_end,   2, 2, 1, 1)
276	  d:add_button("Undo last",  undo_last,  3, 2, 1, 1)
277	
278	  d:add_button("Refresh time", refresh_now, 1, 3, 1, 1)
279	
280	  -- Rich-text label so multi-line status (with <br>) renders reliably.
281	  w_status = d:add_html("", 1, 4, 3, 2)
282	
283	  d:show()
284	  set_status("Ready. Play a video, then Mark START / Mark END per rally.")
285	end
286	
287	--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
288	-- Lifecycle
289	--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
290	function activate()
291	  pending_start = nil
292	  last_line_len = nil
293	  out_path      = resolve_out_path()
294	  rally_count   = max_existing_rally()   -- continue numbering an existing CSV
295	  create_dialog()
296	end
297	
298	function deactivate()
299	  if d then d:delete(); d = nil end
300	end
301	
302	function close()
303	  -- User X-ed the dialog window: tear down so VLC unticks us cleanly.
304	  deactivate()
305	  vlc.deactivate()
306	end
