### 524. assistant (2026-06-07T01:27:14.300Z)

--[[ rally_annotator.lua  --  VLC Lua EXTENSION (v1.2)

  Rally Annotator for NET-SEPARATED RACQUET SPORTS
  (badminton · tennis · table tennis · pickleball · padel)

  While watching a match in VLC, mark each rally's START / END and a point-stop
  reason, and append one CSV row per rally. Pause/scrub freely, then click — the
  callback snapshots the exact playback time. Output is a plain CSV that ingests
  directly into common rally-segmentation tooling.

  Output CSV columns (times in decimal SECONDS):
      rally_number,start_time,end_time,ending_reason,sport

  WHY a button-dialog (not an auto-pause hook):
    VLC 3.x can expose pause via the {"playing-listener"} capability
    (status_changed/playing_changed), but that callback is flaky on macOS
    (VLC #22778) and the input/meta listeners are broken in VLC 4.0 (#27558).
    A persistent dialog whose button callbacks SNAPSHOT the current playback
    time is portable, precise, and lets the rater pause/scrub before committing.
    So we declare NO listener capabilities.

  UNITS: in VLC 3.x, vlc.var.get(input,"time") is in MICROSECONDS -> /1e6.

  Install (Windows): copy this file to
      %APPDATA%\vlc\lua\extensions\rally_annotator.lua
    macOS:  ~/Library/Application Support/org.videolan.vlc/lua/extensions/
    Linux:  ~/.local/share/vlc/lua/extensions/
  then VLC > Tools > Plugins and extensions > Reload extensions (or restart),
  then enable it from the View menu.

  MIT licensed. https://github.com/avidullu/rally-annotator
]]

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- Extension registration
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
function descriptor()
  return {
    title       = "Rally Annotator",
    version     = "1.2",
    author      = "Avi Dullu",
    url         = "https://github.com/avidullu/rally-annotator",
    shortdesc   = "Mark rally start/end + reason to a CSV (net-separated racquet sports)",
    description = "Persistent dialog to mark rally START/END and a point-stop reason "
               .. "for badminton/tennis/table-tennis/pickleball/padel; appends "
               .. "rally_number,start_time,end_time,ending_reason,sport (decimal seconds) "
               .. "to a CSV next to the video.",
    capabilities = {}   -- button-click model: no listeners needed
  }
end

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- Config
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- Net-separated racquet sports share a forced/unforced-error point-stop taxonomy.
local REASONS = {
  "winner", "forced_error", "unforced_error", "service_fault", "let", "other"
}
local SPORTS = {
  "badminton", "tennis", "table_tennis", "pickleball", "padel"
}

local HEADER = "rally_number,start_time,end_time,ending_reason,sport\n"

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- State
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
local d                 -- the single dialog
local w_status          -- status label widget (HTML/rich-text)
local w_reason          -- ending_reason dropdown widget
local w_sport           -- sport dropdown widget
local pending_start     -- number (seconds) or nil
local rally_count = 0   -- highest rally_number written to the CSV so far
local last_line_len = nil -- byte length of the last appended line (for Undo)
local out_path          -- resolved CSV path (computed once on activate)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- Helpers
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

-- Current playback time in SECONDS, or nil if nothing is playing.
local function now_seconds()
  local input = vlc.object.input()
  if not input then return nil end
  local t_us = vlc.var.get(input, "time")   -- MICROSECONDS in VLC 3.x
  if not t_us then return nil end
  return t_us / 1000000.0
end

-- mm:ss.mmm for display only.
local function fmt_clock(s)
  if not s then return "--:--" end
  if s < 0 then s = 0 end
  local m   = math.floor(s / 60)
  local sec = s - m * 60
  return string.format("%d:%06.3f", m, sec)
end

-- Minimal HTML-escape so paths/messages render literally in the rich-text label.
local function esc(text)
  text = tostring(text or "")
  text = text:gsub("&", "&amp;")
  text = text:gsub("<", "&lt;")
  text = text:gsub(">", "&gt;")
  return text
end

-- Best-effort path to the currently playing media file (decoded from URI).
local function current_media_path()
  local input = vlc.object.input()
  if not input then return nil end
  local item = vlc.input.item()           -- VLC 3.x: vlc.input.item()
  if not item then return nil end
  local uri = item:uri()                   -- item:uri()
  if not uri or uri == "" then return nil end
  -- file:///C:/dir/clip.mp4  ->  C:\dir\clip.mp4
  local p = uri
  p = p:gsub("^file:///", "")
  p = p:gsub("^file://", "")
  -- percent-decode (e.g. %20 -> space) BEFORE separator normalization
  p = p:gsub("%%(%x%x)", function(h) return string.char(tonumber(h, 16)) end)
  p = p:gsub("/", "\\")
  return p
end

-- Resolve a sensible default output path: next to the video if we can, else
-- the user's home (USERPROFILE on Windows, HOME elsewhere).
local function resolve_out_path()
  local media = current_media_path()
  if media then
    local dir, stem = media:match("^(.*[\\/])([^\\/]-)%.?[^\\/%.]*$")
    if dir and stem and stem ~= "" then
      return dir .. stem .. ".rallies.csv"
    end
    local d2 = media:match("^(.*[\\/])")
    if d2 then return d2 .. "rally_labels.csv" end
  end
  local home = os.getenv("USERPROFILE") or os.getenv("HOME") or "."
  local sep = home:find("\\") and "\\" or "/"
  if home:sub(-1) ~= sep then home = home .. sep end
  return home .. "rally_labels.csv"
end

local function file_exists(path)
  local fh = io.open(path, "r")
  if fh then fh:close(); return true end
  return false
end

-- Scan an existing CSV and return the highest integer rally_number in it, so a
-- re-enabled session keeps numbering monotonic instead of restarting at 1.
local function max_existing_rally()
  local f = io.open(out_path, "r")
  if not f then return 0 end
  local maxn = 0
  for line in f:lines() do
    local first = line:match("^%s*([^,]+)")
    if first then
      local n = tonumber(first)
      if n and n == math.floor(n) and n > maxn then maxn = n end
    end
  end
  f:close()
  return maxn
end

local function set_status(msg)
  if not w_status then return end
  local tail = pending_start
      and string.format("START armed @ %s", esc(fmt_clock(pending_start)))
      or  "START not set"
  w_status:set_text(string.format(
    "%s<br>Now: %s &nbsp;|&nbsp; %s<br>Rallies written: %d<br>CSV: %s",
    esc(msg), esc(fmt_clock(now_seconds())), tail, rally_count, esc(out_path)))
  if d then d:update() end
end

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- CSV append (header written once if the file is new/empty)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
local function append_row(n, start_s, end_s, why, sport)
  local exists = file_exists(out_path)
  local f, err = io.open(out_path, "a")
  if not f then return false, ("cannot open CSV: " .. tostring(err)) end
  if not exists then
    f:write(HEADER)
  end
  local line = string.format("%d,%.3f,%.3f,%s,%s\n", n, start_s, end_s, why, sport)
  f:write(line)
  f:close()
  last_line_len = #line   -- track byte length so Undo can truncate it
  return true
end

-- Remove the last appended data line by rewriting the file without its tail.
local function remove_last_row()
  if not last_line_len then return false, "nothing to undo" end
  local f = io.open(out_path, "rb")
  if not f then return false, "CSV not found" end
  local data = f:read("*a") or ""
  f:close()
  if #data < last_line_len then return false, "CSV shorter than last row" end
  local kept = data:sub(1, #data - last_line_len)
  if kept == HEADER then kept = "" end   -- only the header left -> blank it
  local wf = io.open(out_path, "wb")
  if not wf then return false, "cannot rewrite CSV" end
  wf:write(kept)
  wf:close()
  last_line_len = nil   -- only one level of undo
  return true
end

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- Button callbacks (VLC calls these on its main loop; MUST be global)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
function mark_start()
  local t = now_seconds()
  if not t then set_status("No media playing -- cannot mark START."); return end
  pending_start = t
  set_status(string.format("START marked @ %s", fmt_clock(t)))
end

function mark_end()
  local t = now_seconds()
  if not t then set_status("No media playing -- cannot mark END."); return end
  if not pending_start then
    set_status("No START armed -- press \"Mark START\" first.")
    return
  end
  local s, e = pending_start, t
  if e <= s then
    if e == s then
      set_status(string.format(
        "END (%s) equals START (%s) -- mark END later (rally must be > 0s).",
        fmt_clock(e), fmt_clock(s)))
      return
    end
    s, e = e, s   -- END before START position: swap to a positive interval
  end
  local _, reason = w_reason:get_value()   -- (id, text); we want the text
  if not reason or reason == "" then reason = "other" end
  local _, sport = w_sport:get_value()
  if not sport or sport == "" then sport = "badminton" end
  local n = rally_count + 1
  local ok, err = append_row(n, s, e, reason, sport)
  if not ok then set_status("WRITE FAILED: " .. tostring(err)); return end
  rally_count = n
  pending_start = nil
  set_status(string.format(
    "Rally #%d saved: %s -> %s  [%s, %s]", n, fmt_clock(s), fmt_clock(e), reason, sport))
end

function undo_last()
  if pending_start and not last_line_len then
    pending_start = nil
    set_status("Cleared armed START (no row written yet).")
    return
  end
  local ok, err = remove_last_row()
  if not ok then set_status("Undo: " .. tostring(err)); return end
  if rally_count > 0 then rally_count = rally_count - 1 end
  set_status(string.format("Undid last rally. %d remaining in CSV.", rally_count))
end

function refresh_now()
  set_status("Refreshed.")
end

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- Dialog construction
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
local function create_dialog()
  d = vlc.dialog("Rally Annotator")

  d:add_label("Sport:", 1, 1, 1, 1)
  w_sport = d:add_dropdown(2, 1, 2, 1)
  for i, v in ipairs(SPORTS) do w_sport:add_value(v, i) end   -- add_value(text, id)

  d:add_label("Ending reason (used on Mark END):", 1, 2, 1, 1)
  w_reason = d:add_dropdown(2, 2, 2, 1)
  for i, v in ipairs(REASONS) do w_reason:add_value(v, i) end

  d:add_button("Mark START", mark_start, 1, 3, 1, 1)
  d:add_button("Mark END",   mark_end,   2, 3, 1, 1)
  d:add_button("Undo last",  undo_last,  3, 3, 1, 1)

  d:add_button("Refresh time", refresh_now, 1, 4, 1, 1)

  w_status = d:add_html("", 1, 5, 3, 2)   -- rich-text so multi-line (<br>) renders

  d:show()
  set_status("Ready. Pick the sport, play a video, then Mark START / Mark END per rally.")
end

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- Lifecycle
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
function activate()
  pending_start = nil
  last_line_len = nil
  out_path      = resolve_out_path()
  rally_count   = max_existing_rally()   -- continue numbering an existing CSV
  create_dialog()
end

function deactivate()
  if d then d:delete(); d = nil end
end

function close()
  deactivate()
  vlc.deactivate()
end

### 527. assistant (2026-06-07T01:27:42.807Z)

# Rally Annotator

A tiny **VLC plugin** to hand-label rally **start/end + point-stop reason** while you watch a match,
for **net-separated racquet sports** — badminton, tennis, table tennis, pickleball, padel.

You watch the video in VLC, pause/scrub freely, and click **Mark START** / **Mark END** (with a
reason). It writes one CSV row per rally — clean ground-truth labels for training/evaluating rally
(point) segmentation models, or just for cutting highlights.

```
rally_number,start_time,end_time,ending_reason,sport
1,8.800,11.500,winner,badminton
2,24.389,46.589,unforced_error,badminton
```

Times are **decimal seconds**. See [docs/CSV_FORMAT.md](docs/CSV_FORMAT.md).

## Why
Labeling rally boundaries is the slow, expensive prerequisite for any rally-detection model. Most tools
make you transcribe timestamps by hand. This lets a rater do it **inside the player they already use**,
pausing to the exact frame before each mark — turning "watch a match" into "produce golden labels."

## Install
1. Copy `vlc/rally_annotator.lua` into your VLC Lua **extensions** folder:
   - **Windows:** `%APPDATA%\vlc\lua\extensions\`
   - **macOS:** `~/Library/Application Support/org.videolan.vlc/lua/extensions/`
   - **Linux:** `~/.local/share/vlc/lua/extensions/`
   (create the `extensions` folder if it doesn't exist)
2. In VLC: **Tools → Plugins and extensions → Reload extensions** (or just restart VLC).
3. Enable it from the **View** menu → **Rally Annotator**. A small dialog opens and stays open while you watch.

Requires **VLC 3.0.x**. (VLC 4.0 changed the Lua input/listener API; targeting 3.x for now.)

## Use
1. Pick the **Sport** in the dropdown.
2. Play the match. When a rally begins, click **Mark START** (snapshots the current time — pause/scrub first for frame accuracy).
3. Choose the **Ending reason** (winner / forced_error / unforced_error / service_fault / let / other).
4. When the rally ends, click **Mark END** → one row is appended immediately.
5. **Undo last** removes an uncommitted START or the last written row (one level).
6. The status panel shows the current time, whether a START is armed, rallies written, and the **output CSV path**.

**Output:** `<video-stem>.rallies.csv` next to the video (falls back to your home dir if the path can't be
resolved). Re-opening the extension **continues** rally numbering from the existing file — no duplicate IDs,
no overwrite.

## Sports & taxonomy
Net-separated racquet sports share a forced/unforced-error point-stop taxonomy, so one tool covers them all:

| Sport | Notes |
|---|---|
| badminton | the reference sport |
| tennis | service_fault = fault/double-fault; let supported |
| table_tennis | let (net serve) supported; fast cadence |
| pickleball | service_fault for faults; treat "let" per local rules |
| padel | net-separated; rally boundaries as in tennis |

`ending_reason` ∈ `{winner, forced_error, unforced_error, service_fault, let, other}` — a shared vocabulary
across these sports. Keep to these values for clean downstream aggregation.

## Roadmap
- [ ] Validate v1.2 multi-sport build live in VLC across all five sports.
- [ ] Optional per-sport reason presets / hotkeys.
- [ ] Alternative front-ends for power users / remote raters: a `python-vlc` + Tk/Qt app with global keyboard
      shortcuts (S/E/1–6/U), and a zero-install HTML5 `<video>` page that exports the same CSV.
- [ ] Optional extra columns (e.g. `shots_count`, server/receiver) behind a toggle.

## Contributing
Issues and PRs welcome — especially live test reports per sport/OS and small UX fixes. The plugin is a single
self-contained Lua file (`vlc/rally_annotator.lua`) using only documented VLC 3.x Lua APIs.

## License
MIT — see [LICENSE](LICENSE).

### 531. assistant (2026-06-07T01:28:05.330Z)

# Output CSV format

One row per rally. Header is written once when the file is created.

```
rally_number,start_time,end_time,ending_reason,sport
1,8.800,11.500,winner,badminton
2,24.389,46.589,unforced_error,badminton
3,49.183,54.683,let,badminton
```

| Column | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `rally_number` | int | 1-based, monotonic; continues across re-opening the same CSV |
| `start_time` | float | rally start, **decimal seconds** from the video start |
| `end_time` | float | rally end, decimal seconds (always `> start_time`) |
| `ending_reason` | enum | `winner` / `forced_error` / `unforced_error` / `service_fault` / `let` / `other` |
| `sport` | enum | `badminton` / `tennis` / `table_tennis` / `pickleball` / `padel` |

Notes:
- **Decimal seconds**, not `mm:ss` — downstream loaders parse floats.
- Rows are appended live; **Undo last** truncates the last row (one level).
- Consumers should read by **column name** (the header is stable but column *order* may evolve; extra
  columns may be added later). Most CSV `DictReader`-style loaders already tolerate extra columns.

## Ingesting the labels

This CSV is intentionally compatible with common rally-segmentation tooling that reads
`rally_number,start_time,end_time` by header name and drops rows where `end <= start`:

```bash
# Generic: any tool that takes a labels CSV of start/end seconds.

# Example — score a detector against these labels, or slice clips by them
# (badminton-highlight-indexer loaders read --labels <this.csv> directly):
python -m backend.eval.calibrate_wasb --trajectory traj.csv --labels match.rallies.csv --fps 59.94 --frame-width 1920
python -m backend.tools.rally_slicer --video match.mp4 --labels match.rallies.csv --rallies all

# Example — register as curated golden data in sports-data-collector:
python -m src.cli.curate import-local --sport badminton --video-path match.mp4 --annotations-path match.rallies.csv
```

The `sport` column is self-documenting; tools that take a `--sport` flag can ignore it.

### 555. user (2026-06-07T01:45:37.103Z)

For the audio plumbing, I am trying to understand that whether we will integrate it in a way that we get audio as a separate signal for the entire video and then in code we try to fuse with WASB and make corrections to WASB models output, or we are going to feed the audio to WASB in a way that it uses audio signals to further refine its metrics which help us detect rallies better?

### 556. assistant (2026-06-07T01:46:37.180Z)

Great question — it's the key architectural fork, and the answer is firmly **Option A: audio is a separate whole-video signal, fused in *our* rally layer — we do not feed audio into WASB.** Here's the why, because it's grounded in what WASB actually is.

**WASB is a vision-only shuttle *detector*, not a rally detector.** It's a frozen, pretrained CNN (HRNet) that takes *image frames* → per-frame shuttle `(x, y, visibility)`. Two consequences:
- It has **no audio pathway**. Making it ingest audio means adding an audio encoder + fusion layers and **retraining the whole network** — which (a) needs **per-frame shuttle-coordinate labels paired with audio**, which we don't have (we only have rally `[start,end]` labels), and (b) forks the clean MIT model we rely on.
- **It doesn't decide rallies at all.** Rally detection is *our* layer on top of WASB's trajectory (`trajectory_to_action_windows` + gate + the distilled classifier). So "audio helping detect rallies better" already happens in our code — the only question is whether audio enters WASB (no) or our rally layer (yes).

And conceptually, audio answers a *different* question than WASB: WASB says **where** the shuttle is on screen; audio says **when** a hit happened. Audio doesn't make WASB localize the shuttle better — it's an independent rally-activity cue. So fusing them belongs at the layer that *combines cues into rallies*, not inside the shuttle detector.

**So the data flow is:**
```
frames ─► WASB (frozen, MIT) ─► shuttle trajectory ─┐
                                                     ├─► RALLY LAYER (our code) ─► rallies
audio  ─► hit detector (our code) ─► hit events ────┘   (fusion happens HERE)
```

Within that rally layer, "fusion" has **two complementary modes** (both in the plan):
1. **Feature-level fusion (primary — refines our rally metrics):** add audio features (`hit_rate`, inter-hit cadence/regularity, `time_since_last_hit`) alongside WASB-derived features (velocity, net-crossings) into the **one distilled classifier** that emits rallies. This is experiment **E3** — it's literally "use audio to make our rally metrics better."
2. **Decision-level recall-recovery (corrects WASB's output):** where WASB proposed nothing but there's a dense hit-cluster, propose a rally there. This is the "make corrections to WASB output" use — it directly attacks the recall bottleneck.

This also keeps the architecture **modular**: audio is a *peer cue* sitting beside the shuttle trajectory (and, later, pose), and WASB stays a swappable black box (we could drop in TrackNet without touching the audio path).

**The one scenario that resembles your Option B** isn't WASB at all — it's a *future* single end-to-end multimodal model that eats frames+audio → rallies directly (the "HitNet GRU / temporal-action-localization" direction from the research). That's a bigger build needing far more labels, and it'd *replace* the modular stack rather than augment WASB. Not now; noted as a long-horizon option.

The plan doc says "fuse with WASB" but doesn't spell out *that WASB stays frozen and fusion is strictly in the rally layer* — which is exactly the confusion you just flagged. Want me to add a short **"Integration architecture"** section to `AUDIO_RALLY_DETECTION_PLAN.md` (with that diagram + the "WASB is not modified" principle) so it's unambiguous for whoever builds it?

72	## 7. Risks & mitigations
73	- **Shuttle SNR (top risk):** E1 is the empirical gate; if NO-GO, audio is deferred, not forced.
74	- **No crowd applause** in amateur single-camera clips (a cue the literature leans on for end-of-point) → rely
75	  on **hit-cadence cessation**, not applause.
76	- **Mic occlusion / wind** → recording guideline (audio on, unobstructed, avoid aggressive wind-suppression).
77	- **A/V sync:** audio and frames share the same file timeline → no sync work needed.

### 590. assistant (2026-06-07T01:54:39.781Z)

Captured exactly as you framed it. Here's what went into ADR-007 (new **"Integration architecture"** section) + the plan doc, in PR #34:

**Option A (now) — the detailed "fuse" explanation:**
- *Why not feed audio into WASB:* WASB is a **frozen, vision-only shuttle detector** (frames → x,y,visibility; MIT weights we run, not train). It has no audio path, doesn't decide rallies at all, and bolting audio on would mean **retraining** with per-frame shuttle+audio labels we don't have (forking the MIT model). And audio answers a *different* question — **"when" a hit happened**, not **"where"** the shuttle is.
- *So fusion lives in our rally layer,* in two modes: **feature-level** (audio features → the single distilled classifier, E3) and **decision-level recall-recovery** (a hit-cluster where WASB found nothing → propose a rally). Each cue stays a swappable black box. Added a data-flow diagram.

**Option B (the intended destination) — your call, now documented:**
> Once a healthy golden corpus exists, **collapse the modular cues into a single OWNED end-to-end multimodal model** (frames + audio + pose/stance + … → rallies), **fine-tuned on our own data instead of depending on WASB's pretrained weights** — removing both the recall ceiling WASB's windows impose and the licensing question on its academic weights.

And the rationale you gave — that the modular cues aren't a detour: **"Option A is how we earn Option B."** Each cue (audio → stance heuristic → other signals) is cheap, debuggable, and *generates the very labels* the end-to-end model will need. We jump to B only when the data supports training it.

**To fully clean the slate, two small docs/chore PRs to merge:**
- **#33** — removes the stale annotator copy that slipped onto master.
- **#34** — this ADR-007 expansion (+ repoints the annotator reference).

No conflicts between them. After both merge, master is pristine, the audio direction is fully specified, and Option B is on the record as the north star. 🧭

## You are here (2026-06-07)
- **Rally-detection stack matured & all merged.** WASB shuttle-trajectory substrate is **resumable/
  checkpointed** (md5-ish cache, incremental detector flush, tracker re-run). On top: a rally layer =
  windowing thresholds + a **net-crossing "gate A"** + a **distilled classifier** (`distill_local`).
  Eval harness: `calibrate_wasb` / `calibration.py` (LOVO) / `metrics.py`.
- **Honest core finding:** WASB-only threshold tuning **ceilings ~F1 0.44** and the learned distilled
  model **underperforms the baseline at n=2 videos**. The blocker is **golden data + WASB candidate
  recall**, not the method. (Gemini-as-teacher gave us the first real measurement.)
- **Strategy decided (ADR-007):** Gemini = **offline teacher only**; **AUDIO** hit-detection is the next
  local cue, **fused in our rally layer** (WASB stays a frozen black box) — Option A. **Pose/stance =
  parked #2.** **Option B = the eventual end-state**: one OWNED end-to-end multimodal model
  (frames+audio+pose→rallies) fine-tuned on our data, dropping WASB-weight dependence + the recall ceiling.
- **Golden data path:** owner **self-rates** clips with the new **standalone VLC annotator repo**
  (github.com/avidullu/rally-annotator, MIT, multi-sport) → CSV → in-repo `--labels` loaders / collector
  `import-local`. Human labels > the Gemini silver labels the model was starving on.
- **Both repos clean.** Indexer: PRs #25–#34 merged, master green (202 tests), tree clean. Annotator:
  public, `main` clean. Modeling is **PARKED** pending golden labels.

## Next steps / open threads
1. **Owner:** rate a few clips in VLC (annotator v1.2 — needs a live multi-sport smoke test), and/or merge
   nothing pending (none open).
2. **Then E1 (audio GO/NO-GO):** extract audio → energy-peak hits → overlay on WASB windows + golden labels
   → does audio recover WASB-missed rallies on **real GoPro audio**? (Top risk = quiet shuttle SNR.) Full
   plan: `docs/AUDIO_RALLY_DETECTION_PLAN.md` (E1–E4). Build `backend/audio/hit_detector.py` (numpy+scipy)
   if E1 passes; then E3 = fuse audio features into `distill_local`.
3. Stance heuristic (#2) after the owner's "other hunch"; Option B once the corpus is healthy.
4. Cached artifacts LEFT IN PLACE for re-use (owner will rate): `Temp\{adarsh,testlarge}_frames` + trajectories;
   `Temp\verylarge_frames` (partial). Adarsh/TestLargeVideo trajectories are ready to feed calibration.

## Key decisions (don't relitigate without reason)
- **Audio now → stance parked → Option B eventual** (ADR-007). Modular cues are how we *earn* Option B
  (each is cheap/debuggable and generates the labels the end-to-end model needs).
- **Fusion is in OUR rally layer, never inside WASB** (WASB = frozen vision-only shuttle *detector*; no audio
  path; retraining needs x,y+audio labels we lack). Two modes: feature-level (→ distilled classifier) +
  decision-level recall-recovery. (ADR-007 §Integration architecture.)
- **Local-only inference = privacy lever**; **permissive licensing only** (ADR-005); Gemini paid+offline only.
- **Distilled model > threshold tuning** is the direction, but **needs more videos** (n=2 overfits).
- Per-video adaptation = calibration on held-out; never mix train/eval. Frequent **small PRs** + continuous
  [[current-state]] updates (this handoff on request only).

### 630. assistant (2026-06-07T03:15:11.138Z)

"""Audio shuttle-hit ("thwack") detection — local, dependency-light (numpy + ffmpeg).

Stages 0–1 of docs/AUDIO_RALLY_DETECTION_PLAN.md: extract the audio track, build a
spectral-flux onset envelope (with pre-emphasis to boost the broadband HF transient
a racket–shuttle contact makes), threshold it adaptively, and return (timestamp,
strength) hit candidates. Pure numpy + stdlib `wave` (NO scipy); ffmpeg (subprocess)
only for extraction. Consumed by the E1 go/no-go probe; later feeds rally fusion
(ADR-007) if E1 passes. WASB is never modified — this is a separate signal.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import os
import wave
import tempfile
import subprocess
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import List, Tuple

import numpy as np


@dataclass
class HitEvent:
    t: float         # seconds
    strength: float  # onset-envelope peak value (arbitrary units)


def extract_audio(video_path: str, out_wav: str = None, sr: int = 16000) -> str:
    """ffmpeg → mono 16-bit PCM WAV at `sr`. Returns the wav path (temp if not given).
    Audio-only decode, so it's fast even for multi-GB video."""
    if out_wav is None:
        fd, out_wav = tempfile.mkstemp(suffix=".wav")
        os.close(fd)
    cmd = ["ffmpeg", "-y", "-i", video_path, "-vn", "-ac", "1", "-ar", str(sr),
           "-c:a", "pcm_s16le", out_wav]
    subprocess.run(cmd, check=True, stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL, stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL)
    return out_wav


def load_wav(path: str) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, int]:
    """Read a PCM WAV → (float32 samples in [-1,1], sample_rate). Mono-downmixes."""
    with wave.open(path, "rb") as w:
        sr, n, sw, ch = w.getframerate(), w.getnframes(), w.getsampwidth(), w.getnchannels()
        raw = w.readframes(n)
    if sw == 2:
        a = np.frombuffer(raw, dtype=np.int16).astype(np.float32) / 32768.0
    elif sw == 4:
        a = np.frombuffer(raw, dtype=np.int32).astype(np.float32) / 2147483648.0
    elif sw == 1:
        a = np.frombuffer(raw, dtype=np.uint8).astype(np.float32) / 128.0 - 1.0
    else:
        raise ValueError(f"unsupported sample width {sw}")
    if ch > 1:
        a = a.reshape(-1, ch).mean(axis=1)
    return a, sr


def _preemphasis(x: np.ndarray, coef: float = 0.97) -> np.ndarray:
    if len(x) < 2:
        return x.copy()
    return np.append(x[0], x[1:] - coef * x[:-1])


def onset_envelope(samples: np.ndarray, sr: int, frame: int = 1024,
                   hop: int = 256) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, float]:
    """Spectral-flux onset strength per hop (pure-numpy STFT). Returns (env, hop_seconds)."""
    x = _preemphasis(np.asarray(samples, dtype=np.float32))
    hop_sec = hop / float(sr)
    if len(x) < frame:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float32), hop_sec
    win = np.hanning(frame).astype(np.float32)
    n_frames = 1 + (len(x) - frame) // hop
    idx = np.arange(frame)[None, :] + hop * np.arange(n_frames)[:, None]
    frames = x[idx] * win
    mag = np.abs(np.fft.rfft(frames, axis=1))
    diff = np.diff(mag, axis=0)                                  # frame-to-frame change
    flux = np.sqrt((np.maximum(diff, 0.0) ** 2).sum(axis=1))     # positive (onset) flux
    env = np.concatenate([[0.0], flux]).astype(np.float32)       # align to n_frames
    return env, hop_sec


def _moving_stats(env: np.ndarray, win: int) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:
    """Sliding-window mean and std (reflect-padded, via cumsum). win forced odd."""
    if win % 2 == 0:
        win += 1
    pad = win // 2
    p = np.pad(env.astype(np.float64), pad, mode="reflect")
    c1 = np.cumsum(np.insert(p, 0, 0.0))
    c2 = np.cumsum(np.insert(p * p, 0, 0.0))
    s1 = c1[win:] - c1[:-win]
    s2 = c2[win:] - c2[:-win]
    mean = s1 / win
    var = np.maximum(s2 / win - mean * mean, 0.0)
    return mean[:len(env)], np.sqrt(var)[:len(env)]


def detect_hits(samples: np.ndarray, sr: int, k: float = 2.5, win_frames: int = 41,
                min_gap_sec: float = 0.08) -> List[HitEvent]:
    """Detect impact transients: local maxima of the onset envelope that exceed an
    adaptive threshold (moving mean + k·std), keeping the strongest within `min_gap_sec`.
    `k` is the sensitivity knob (lower = more hits)."""
    env, hop_sec = onset_envelope(samples, sr)
    if len(env) < 3:
        return []
    mean, std = _moving_stats(env, win_frames)
    thr = mean + k * std
    is_peak = (env[1:-1] >= env[:-2]) & (env[1:-1] > env[2:]) & (env[1:-1] > thr[1:-1])
    peak_idx = np.nonzero(is_peak)[0] + 1
    if peak_idx.size == 0:
        return []
    # enforce min inter-peak gap: greedily keep strongest first
    min_gap = max(1, int(round(min_gap_sec / hop_sec)))
    order = peak_idx[np.argsort(-env[peak_idx])]
    taken = np.zeros(len(env), dtype=bool)
    kept = []
    for i in order:
        lo, hi = max(0, i - min_gap), min(len(env), i + min_gap + 1)
        if taken[lo:hi].any():
            continue
        taken[i] = True
        kept.append(i)
    kept.sort()
    return [HitEvent(t=float(i * hop_sec), strength=float(env[i])) for i in kept]


def detect_hits_in_video(video_path: str, sr: int = 16000, **kw) -> List[HitEvent]:
    """Convenience: extract audio from a video and detect hits. Cleans up the temp wav."""
    wav = extract_audio(video_path, sr=sr)
    try:
        samples, real_sr = load_wav(wav)
        return detect_hits(samples, real_sr, **kw)
    finally:
        try:
            os.remove(wav)
        except OSError:
            pass

### 633. assistant (2026-06-07T03:15:58.583Z)

"""E1 — audio GO/NO-GO probe (docs/AUDIO_RALLY_DETECTION_PLAN.md).

Answers, on REAL audio: does the shuttle "thwack" survive well enough that hit
peaks (a) cluster inside rallies far more than dead time, (b) recover rallies the
WASB windows miss (our recall bottleneck), and (c) form usable audio-only rally
proposals? No training, no fusion — purely diagnostic, so we know whether to build
the rest of the audio path.

Reuses: audio.hit_detector, calibrate_wasb.load_golden_gts/make_predict_fn,
eval.metrics. Labels may be human-gold OR Gemini-silver — silver is fine for the
SNR/efficacy question E1 asks.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
from typing import List, Tuple

from backend.audio.hit_detector import detect_hits_in_video, HitEvent
from backend.eval.calibrate_wasb import load_golden_gts, make_predict_fn, BASELINE
from backend.eval.metrics import match_intervals, score
from backend.utils.video import get_video_duration

Interval = Tuple[float, float]


def cluster_hits(hits: List[HitEvent], gap_sec: float = 2.0, min_hits: int = 2,
                 pad: float = 0.3) -> List[Interval]:
    """Group hits whose inter-hit gap <= gap_sec into rally proposals; keep clusters
    with >= min_hits (a lone hit = a service feed, not a rally)."""
    ts = sorted(h.t for h in hits)
    if not ts:
        return []
    out: List[Interval] = []
    start = last = ts[0]
    cnt = 1
    for t in ts[1:]:
        if t - last <= gap_sec:
            last, cnt = t, cnt + 1
        else:
            if cnt >= min_hits:
                out.append((max(0.0, start - pad), last + pad))
            start = last = t
            cnt = 1
    if cnt >= min_hits:
        out.append((max(0.0, start - pad), last + pad))
    return out


def _hits_in(hits: List[HitEvent], s: float, e: float) -> int:
    return sum(1 for h in hits if s <= h.t <= e)


def run(video: str, labels_csv: str, trajectory: str, fps: float, frame_width: float,
        k: float = 2.5, cluster_gap: float = 2.0, min_hits: int = 2, iou: float = 0.5) -> dict:
    hits = detect_hits_in_video(video, k=k)
    gts = load_golden_gts(labels_csv)
    wasb = make_predict_fn(trajectory, fps, frame_width)(BASELINE)
    dur = get_video_duration(video) or (max((e for _, e in gts + wasb), default=0.0))

    print(f"=== E1 audio probe: {video} ===")
    print(f"  {len(hits)} hits detected (k={k}) over {dur:.0f}s = {len(hits)/max(1,dur)*60:.1f} hits/min | "
          f"{len(gts)} labeled rallies, {len(wasb)} WASB baseline windows")

    # (1) Discrimination: hit density inside rallies vs dead time.
    rally_dur = sum(e - s for s, e in gts)
    dead_dur = max(1e-6, dur - rally_dur)
    hits_in = sum(_hits_in(hits, s, e) for s, e in gts)
    hits_out = len(hits) - hits_in
    dens_in = hits_in / max(1e-6, rally_dur)
    dens_out = hits_out / dead_dur
    ratio = dens_in / dens_out if dens_out > 0 else float("inf")
    rallies_with_hits = sum(1 for s, e in gts if _hits_in(hits, s, e) >= 2)
    print(f"  [discrimination] in-rally {dens_in:.2f} hits/s vs dead-time {dens_out:.2f} hits/s "
          f"→ ratio {ratio:.1f}x | rallies with >=2 hits: {rallies_with_hits}/{len(gts)} "
          f"({100*rallies_with_hits/max(1,len(gts)):.0f}%)")

    # (2) Recall recovery: of rallies WASB MISSES, how many have an audio hit-cluster?
    mr = match_intervals(wasb, gts, iou)
    missed = [gts[i] for i in mr.unmatched_gt_indices]
    recoverable = [r for r in missed if _hits_in(hits, *r) >= min_hits]
    rec_rate = len(recoverable) / len(missed) if missed else 0.0
    print(f"  [recall recovery] WASB misses {len(missed)}/{len(gts)} rallies; "
          f"audio hit-clusters sit in {len(recoverable)} of them → {100*rec_rate:.0f}% recoverable")

    # (3) Audio-only rally proposals vs labels.
    props = cluster_hits(hits, cluster_gap, min_hits)
    sc = score(props, gts, iou)
    print(f"  [audio-only proposals] {len(props)} rallies | P={sc.precision:.3f} R={sc.recall:.3f} "
          f"F1={sc.f1:.3f} (vs WASB-only F1 {score(wasb, gts, iou).f1:.3f})")
    return {
        "video": video, "k": k, "n_hits": len(hits), "duration": dur,
        "discrimination_ratio": ratio, "rallies_with_hits_pct": 100 * rallies_with_hits / max(1, len(gts)),
        "wasb_missed": len(missed), "recall_recoverable_pct": 100 * rec_rate,
        "audio_only": sc.as_dict(), "wasb_only_f1": score(wasb, gts, iou).f1,
    }


def main() -> None:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="E1 audio go/no-go probe.")
    ap.add_argument("--video", required=True)
    ap.add_argument("--labels", required=True, help="golden or Gemini-silver rally CSV")
    ap.add_argument("--trajectory", required=True, help="WASB trajectory CSV")
    ap.add_argument("--fps", type=float, default=59.94)
    ap.add_argument("--frame-width", type=float, default=1920.0)
    ap.add_argument("--k", type=float, default=2.5, help="onset sensitivity (lower = more hits)")
    ap.add_argument("--cluster-gap", type=float, default=2.0)
    ap.add_argument("--min-hits", type=int, default=2)
    ap.add_argument("--iou", type=float, default=0.5)
    args = ap.parse_args()
    run(args.video, args.labels, args.trajectory, args.fps, args.frame_width,
        k=args.k, cluster_gap=args.cluster_gap, min_hits=args.min_hits, iou=args.iou)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

### 636. assistant (2026-06-07T03:16:28.794Z)

"""Audio hit detector (pure DSP; no ffmpeg/video). Synthetic planted transients."""
import numpy as np

from backend.audio import hit_detector as hd
from backend.audio.hit_detector import HitEvent
from backend.eval.audio_e1_probe import cluster_hits


def _synth(sr=16000, dur=6.0, times=(0.5, 1.2, 2.0, 3.1, 4.4, 5.0), seed=0):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    x = (rng.standard_normal(int(sr * dur)) * 0.002).astype(np.float32)   # quiet background
    burst = int(0.005 * sr)                                                # 5ms transient
    for t in times:
        i = int(t * sr)
        x[i:i + burst] += rng.standard_normal(burst).astype(np.float32) * 0.5  # loud "thwack"
    return x, sr, times


def test_detect_hits_finds_planted_transients():
    x, sr, times = _synth()
    hits = hd.detect_hits(x, sr, k=2.5)
    detected = [h.t for h in hits]
    assert len(hits) >= len(times) - 1                       # find most planted hits
    for t in times:
        assert any(abs(t - d) < 0.06 for d in detected), f"missed planted hit @{t}s; got {detected}"


def test_detect_hits_empty_on_silence():
    assert hd.detect_hits(np.zeros(16000 * 2, dtype=np.float32), 16000) == []


def test_moving_stats_shape_and_values():
    env = np.ones(50, dtype=float)
    m, s = hd._moving_stats(env, 11)
    assert len(m) == len(env) == len(s)
    assert np.allclose(m, 1.0) and np.allclose(s, 0.0)       # constant signal: mean 1, std 0


def test_onset_envelope_length_aligns_to_frames():
    x, sr, _ = _synth(dur=2.0)
    env, hop_sec = hd.onset_envelope(x, sr, frame=1024, hop=256)
    assert hop_sec == 256 / sr
    assert len(env) == 1 + (len(x) - 1024) // 256


def test_cluster_hits_drops_lone_keeps_cluster():
    hits = [HitEvent(10.0, 1.0)] + [HitEvent(t, 1.0) for t in (30.0, 30.6, 31.3, 32.0)]
    cl = cluster_hits(hits, gap_sec=2.0, min_hits=2)
    assert len(cl) == 1                                       # lone hit @10 dropped
    s, e = cl[0]
    assert s < 30.0 and e > 32.0                              # cluster span (with pad)

### 653. assistant (2026-06-07T03:20:57.011Z)

Here's the honest E1 verdict, and where I'd go next.

## E1 verdict: AMBER — promising for *recall*, not clean enough to stand alone (yet)

**Built & shipped** (PR #35, 207 tests): the audio hit-detector + E1 probe — numpy/ffmpeg, no scipy, WASB untouched.

**The good (real signal):**
- Audio fires in **100% of rallies** on both videos.
- **100% of the rallies WASB *misses*** have audio hit-clusters → audio could directly attack our recall bottleneck.

**The caution (why it's amber):**
- **Discrimination is only ~2.8×** at best (in-rally vs dead-time hit density), with a sensitivity cliff just past k=4. At default sensitivity it's 1.3× and ~56 hits/min — the broadband detector is catching lots of *non-shuttle* transients (footwork, voices, ambient).
- **Audio-only F1 (0.13–0.27) trails WASB** → audio is a **fusion/recall cue, not a standalone segmenter**.

**Two honest caveats that make this a floor, not a ceiling:**
1. These are **Gemini *silver* labels** — the "dead time" likely contains unlabeled activity (warm-up hits, knock-ups), which *inflates* the dead-time hit count and *understates* the true discrimination. **Your human-gold labels will sharpen this.**
2. The detector is **v1 broadband** (pre-emphasis only). The obvious untried precision lever is a **shuttle-band band-pass** — a shuttle "click" is HF-rich; voices/footsteps are lower-frequency, so band-passing should separate them and lift discrimination well above 2.8×.

## The decision (your call)
- **(A) I add the shuttle-band band-pass now** — cheap, no labels needed, still pre-fusion. It directly tests "can we make the audio signal clean?" If discrimination jumps (say 4–6×), audio fusion becomes clearly worth building; if it doesn't budge, that's a strong honest signal to lean on stance instead. **My recommendation** — highest info per effort, and it parallelizes with your rating.
- **(B) Pause audio, wait for your human-gold labels**, then re-run E1 to get the true (un-penalized) discrimination before any more detector work.

I lean **(A)** — it's a quick, label-free experiment that either greenlights audio or tells us to pivot, while you keep rating. Want me to go? (And memory's already refreshed per the new auto-checkpoint policy — both `current-state` and the handoff reflect this E1 result.)

107	                min_gap_sec: float = 0.08) -> List[HitEvent]:
108	    """Detect impact transients: local maxima of the onset envelope that exceed an
109	    adaptive threshold (moving mean + k·std), keeping the strongest within `min_gap_sec`.
110	    `k` is the sensitivity knob (lower = more hits)."""
111	    env, hop_sec = onset_envelope(samples, sr)

118	    if peak_idx.size == 0:

### 682. assistant (2026-06-07T03:56:20.015Z)

Welcome back — hope the morning was good. 🙏 Here's the holistic look at Option A.

## Bottom line: classical-onset audio underdelivers on real GoPro audio — band-pass didn't rescue it

I ran the band-pass, a full **band × sensitivity sweep** (low/mid/high bands × k at 32 kHz), and a **label-artifact check**, on both Adarsh and TestLargeVideo. All committed to **PR #35** (209 tests green); both memory files refreshed.

**What the evidence says:**
- ✅ **Recall signal is real:** the detector fires in **100% of rallies** and **100% of WASB-missed rallies have hit-clusters** — audio genuinely lights up where play is.
- ❌ **Precision ceilings at ~2.2×** (in-rally vs dead-time hit density) on *both* videos — too low to be a clean cue (audio-only F1 0.13–0.27, below WASB).
- ❌ **Band-pass made it worse, not better.** The HF "shuttle-click" bands gave *lower* discrimination than full-band — shuttle hits have no band-separable signature in this audio.
- ❌ **Not a warm-up/label artifact.** The *mid-match* dead time (between rallies) alone runs ~0.6 hits/s vs ~1.3 in-rally → the 2.2× holds. **Root cause:** between-point activity — the loser tossing the shuttle back (literally the false-fire we're fighting), footwork, talk — makes transients a classical onset detector can't tell from rally hits.

**Honest reflection:** the research ranked audio #1, but on *your* GoPro audio it's weaker than the (broadcast/tennis) literature implied — exactly the shuttle-SNR + amateur-vs-broadcast caveat the research flagged. The good news is the **E1 gate did its job**: ~30 min of probing saved us from building the whole audio-fusion stack on a cue that wouldn't have carried it.

## Your call — where next

- **(A) Don't sink more into classical audio.** Get golden labels flowing (your VLC rating) to unblock the **distilled model + a real LOVO** on ≥3 videos, and let's hear your **"other hunch."** ← *my honest lean.*
- **(B) Try the one remaining audio lever: an E2 learned *timbre* classifier** (MFCC → shuttle-thwack vs toss/footstep/voice) — the only thing that can separate what a band-pass can't. But it **needs labeled hits** (real work; we'd bootstrap from your gold labels), so it's gated on data anyway.
- **(C) Cheap re-confirm:** re-run E1 on your human-gold labels — though the warm-up check suggests it won't move the ~2.2× much.
- **Pose/stance (#2)** also moves up the priority list now that classical audio capped out.

Nothing else is running; master is clean (only PR #35 open, ready when you want it). No rush — take the holistic look and tell me which thread to pull. And whenever you've rated a clip or two, that single action unblocks the most valuable next step regardless of which option you pick.

### 715. assistant (2026-06-07T04:48:49.132Z)

# Audio cue (E1) — quantitative findings & reusable lessons

**Date:** 2026-06-07 · **Status:** decision recorded (classical-onset audio NOT adopted for fusion).
Companion to ADR-007 and `AUDIO_RALLY_DETECTION_PLAN.md` §10. Purpose: record the **gains and losses
quantitatively** so we don't repeat the dead ends when adding the next signal (pose/stance, etc.) or
when building the Option-B end-to-end model.

## TL;DR
On real GoPro badminton audio, a classical onset hit-detector has a **strong recall signal but a precision
ceiling of ~2.2×** — too weak to be a clean rally cue. **Band-pass filtering did not help.** Net: keep the
cheap, tested detector; **do not build audio fusion on the classical onset cue**; the only path past ~2.2× is
a *learned timbre classifier* (E2), which needs labels. The E1 gate returned this verdict **before** any fusion
was built — a few hours, no wasted downstream work.

## Quantitative scorecard (existing GoPro audio vs Gemini **silver** labels)

**GAINS — what audio gave us:**
| Signal | Adarsh (36 rallies, 676 s) | TestLargeVideo (7 rallies, 101 s) |
|---|---|---|
| Rally coverage (rallies with ≥2 detected hits) | **100%** (36/36) | **100%** (7/7) |
| WASB-missed rallies that contain an audio hit-cluster (recall-recovery) | **100%** (14/14) | **100%** (5/5) |
| Hits detected (@k=3.5) | 486 | 94 |

**LOSSES / LIMITS:**
| Metric | Adarsh | TestLargeVideo |
|---|---|---|
| Discrimination = in-rally ÷ dead-time hit density (fair, mid-match) | **~2.2×** | **~2.3×** |
| …at k=2.5 / best-coverage k=4 | 1.3× / 2.8× (coverage drops to 30/36) | 1.5× |
| Audio-**only** proposal F1 (P / R) | **0.27** (0.22 / 0.36) | **0.13** |
| WASB-only F1 (reference baseline) | 0.54 | 0.33 |
| Band-pass effect on discrimination | **none / negative** (HF bands *worse*) | — |
| Sensitivity cliff | k=6 → ~0 hits (43 → 0.6 hits/min) | — |

**What we built/ran (cost):** `backend/audio/hit_detector.py` (ffmpeg→numpy spectral-flux onset→adaptive peak,
no scipy) + `backend/eval/audio/e1_probe.py` + band×k sweep + warm-up/label-artifact check. ~209 tests; one
session; **no fusion built** (gated correctly).

## Root cause of the ceiling
Between-point activity — the **loser tossing the shuttle back** (literally our false-fire), footwork, and talk —
produces transients a classical onset detector cannot distinguish from rally hits. The signal is **band-agnostic**
(HF "shuttle-click" bands gave *lower* discrimination, not higher), so the separation problem is **timbre, not
frequency band** — a filter can't fix it; a learned classifier might.

## Caveats on the numbers
- Labels are **Gemini silver**, not human-gold → dead-time may include unlabeled activity, so ~2.2× is a plausible
  *floor*. But the warm-up check (mid-match dead-time *alone* is ~0.6 hits/s vs ~1.3 in-rally on both clips) says
  human-gold is unlikely to move it much.
- Only **2 videos, 1 GoPro setup**. Re-confirm on human-gold when available.

## Generalizable lessons — apply to the NEXT cue (pose/stance) and Option B
1. **Gate before build.** A cheap probe on *real target data* against *existing* labels gave a decisive go/no-go
   before any fusion code. Make every new signal earn an E1-style probe first.
2. **Test on the real distribution, not the literature.** The research ranked audio #1 (from broadcast/tennis);
   on amateur GoPro badminton it underdelivered — the exact SNR/domain caveat the research flagged. Lit rankings
   are hypotheses; the probe is ground truth.
3. **Discrimination ratio (in-class ÷ out-of-class signal density) is a fast, label-light cue-quality screen.**
   Compute it first. Rule of thumb from this run: **>>2× to be worth fusing; ~2× = weak.**
4. **Separate the recall role from the precision role.** Audio was excellent at recall (100% coverage) and poor
   at precision — a cue can be one and not the other. Decide its job before fusing.
5. **Band ≠ timbre.** Frequency band-passing can't separate same-band, different-timbre sounds. If discrimination
   is band-agnostic, the lever is a learned timbre model, not a filter.
6. **Silver for the gate, gold for the verdict.** Gemini silver labels were enough to reach go/no-go cheaply;
   reserve scarce human-gold for confirmation.
7. **Keep the cheap knob, record the negative.** We kept the `band=` param (may help other mics/human-gold) but
   documented it didn't help here, so we don't re-explore it.
8. **The recurring blocker is golden labels, not cleverness.** Past ~2.2× needs a learned classifier (labels) —
   echoing the distilled-model finding (n=2 too few). Across cues, *more golden labels* is the highest-leverage move.

## Decision recorded
- **Classical-onset audio: NOT adopted for fusion now.** Detector + E1 probe kept (merged, tested).
- **Next audio step:** E2 learned **timbre** classifier *iff* labels make it cheap; else re-prioritize.
- **Sequenced behind golden-label generation** (owner self-rating via the VLC annotator). The next session resumes
  on either the **E2 audio classifier** or **golden evals** (owner's pick).

### 726. assistant (2026-06-07T04:59:02.434Z)

---
name: audio-e1-findings
description: "Quantitative gains/losses of the audio (E1) cue + reusable lessons for adding the NEXT signal. Classical-onset audio NOT adopted; detector kept."
metadata:
  node_type: memory
  type: project
---

Durable record of the audio experiment (2026-06-07). Core numbers also in
`docs/AUDIO_RALLY_DETECTION_PLAN.md` §10 (on master via PR #35). The fuller dedicated doc
`docs/AUDIO_E1_FINDINGS.md` was written but its **PR #36 was CLOSED (not merged)** → NOT on master
(commit f643d64 recoverable). This memory note is the authoritative cross-session record.

## Verdict
Classical-onset audio hit-detection on real GoPro badminton audio: **strong recall signal, precision ceiling
~2.2× → too weak as a clean cue.** Band-pass did NOT help. Detector + E1 probe KEPT (merged, tested, PR #35);
**no audio fusion built** (E1 gate returned the verdict first). Next audio step = E2 learned **timbre** classifier
(needs labels) IF cheap; else re-prioritize.

## Scorecard (existing GoPro audio vs Gemini SILVER labels; Adarsh=36 rallies/676s, TestLarge=7/101s)
GAINS: rally coverage (≥2 hits) **100%** both; WASB-missed rallies with an audio hit-cluster (recall-recovery)
**100%** both (Adarsh 14/14, TestLarge 5/5).
LOSSES: discrimination (in-rally ÷ mid-match dead-time hit density) **~2.2× / ~2.3×** (1.3× @k2.5; 2.8× @k4 but
coverage drops to 30/36; cliff at k6→~0 hits). Audio-only proposal **F1 0.27 (Adarsh) / 0.13 (TestLarge)** vs
WASB-only 0.54 / 0.33. **Band-pass: none/negative** (HF "shuttle-click" bands gave LOWER discrimination).
ROOT CAUSE: between-point activity (loser tossing shuttle back = our false-fire, footwork, talk) = transients a
classical onset detector can't separate. Band-agnostic ⇒ a **timbre** problem, not a band problem.
CAVEAT: silver labels (floor, not ceiling); warm-up check shows it's not a pre-match artifact (mid-match dead
alone ~0.6 vs ~1.3 in-rally) so human-gold unlikely to move it much. n=2 videos, 1 GoPro.

## Reusable LESSONS (apply to pose/stance #2 and Option B)
1. **Gate before build** — a cheap probe on real target data + existing labels gave a decisive go/no-go before any
   fusion. Every new cue earns an E1-style probe first.
2. **Test on the real distribution, not the literature** — research ranked audio #1 (broadcast/tennis); on amateur
   GoPro badminton it underdelivered (the SNR/domain caveat). Lit = hypothesis; probe = truth.
3. **Discrimination ratio (in-class ÷ out-of-class density) = fast label-light cue screen.** >>2× to fuse; ~2× weak.
4. **Separate recall-role vs precision-role** — a cue can be great at one, useless at the other.
5. **Band ≠ timbre** — band-pass can't split same-band different-timbre sounds; if discrimination is band-agnostic,
   the lever is a learned classifier, not a filter.
6. **Silver labels for the gate, gold for the verdict.**
7. **Keep the cheap knob, record the negative** (kept `band=` param; documented it doesn't help so we don't re-try).
8. **The recurring blocker is golden labels, not cleverness** (past ~2.2× needs a learned model; echoes distilled
   model n=2 too few). More golden labels = highest-leverage move across all cues.

## Code (on master, PR #35)
`backend/audio/hit_detector.py` (ffmpeg→numpy spectral-flux onset→adaptive peak + `band` knob; no scipy),
`backend/eval/audio/e1_probe.py` (E1 diagnostic). 7 hit-detector tests; suite 209 green.

### 739. assistant (2026-06-07T05:01:20.115Z)

Everything's consolidated and the next incarnation is set up. Here's the sunset wrap.

## 🔊 Audio work — quantitative summary (gains & losses)

Recorded durably in **memory ([[audio-e1-findings]])** + **`docs/AUDIO_RALLY_DETECTION_PLAN.md` §10** (on master).

**GAINS**
| | Adarsh (36 rallies) | TestLargeVideo (7) |
|---|---|---|
| Rally coverage (≥2 hits) | 100% | 100% |
| Recall-recovery of WASB-missed rallies | 100% (14/14) | 100% (5/5) |

**LOSSES / LIMITS**
| | Adarsh | TestLargeVideo |
|---|---|---|
| Discrimination (in-rally ÷ dead-time) | ~2.2× | ~2.3× |
| Audio-only F1 (vs WASB-only) | 0.27 (vs 0.54) | 0.13 (vs 0.33) |
| Band-pass effect | none/negative | — |

**Verdict:** classical-onset audio is too weak to be a clean cue; band-pass can't fix it (it's a *timbre*, not a frequency-band, problem — between-point shuttle-tosses/footwork/talk fool the detector). Detector kept (merged, tested); **no fusion built** — the E1 gate paid for itself.

**The reusable lessons** (the real value, in [[audio-e1-findings]]): gate-before-build · test on the *real* distribution not the literature · discrimination-ratio is a fast cue screen (want ≫2×) · band ≠ timbre · silver labels for the gate / gold for the verdict · **golden labels are the recurring blocker across every cue**.

## 🧹 Repo state
master clean — **PRs #25–#35 merged**, 209 tests green, no open PRs, only `feat/khelacharya-shot-intelligence` retained.

**One honest flag:** PR **#36** (the *dedicated* `docs/AUDIO_E1_FINDINGS.md`) was **closed, not merged**, and I'd deleted its branch (the delete-before-confirming trap again — caught it). The doc isn't lost: commit `f643d64` is recoverable, and the full record is safe in memory + plan §10. If you want that dedicated repo doc, next session can re-land it in one step.

2. **Audio E2** — learned *timbre* classifier (needs labeled hits).

Also queued: pose/stance #2, your "other hunch", Option B (owned end-to-end model). Ramp-up kit (CODE_MAP, research, ADR-007, audio findings) is all linked.
